# What Congress Said About the Epstein Files

*A narrative catalogue: every senator and every House member, what they actually said and did, read from the record and written down, with the lines that matter quoted exactly and linked to their source.*

The Epstein Files Transparency Act passed the House 427 to 1 on November 18, 2025 and the Senate by unanimous consent the same day. The vote tells you almost nothing about any individual member; this document is what the vote hides. It covers all 537 members of the 119th Congress in two parts, the Senate and the House, each organized by state. Every entry links to that member's complete statement record in the [Where Congress Stands](https://epstein-data.com/statements) database, and every quotation links to the specific press release, post, or article it came from.

A note on counts. After these entries were written, the database was corrected in September 2026 (291 duplicate rows and 27 unrelated rows removed, 78 dates fixed, one missing member added), and each entry's "entries in the database" figure was recomputed against the corrected data. Where an entry's prose mentions the number of rows or duplicates it found, it describes the record as it stood when it was read.

<a id="terms"></a>

## Terms used in every entry

| Term | What it means here |
|---|---|
| **Position** | Our one-line summary of what the member's whole record amounts to. The prose under it is the evidence. |
| Led the push | Forced the issue: co-led the bill, wrote letters that produced an audit or a briefing, blocked nominees or nominations over the files, kept public pressure on the Justice Department. |
| Took action | Did at least one concrete thing beyond voting: a letter, a hearing question, cosponsorship, a floor speech with a demand. |
| Marked the vote only | Announced or noted the vote and did nothing else. |
| Voted to block release | On Sept. 10, 2025, voted to table (kill) the Senate amendment that would have forced release; later counted as "supporting" the bill only because it passed without a vote. |
| Deflected | Used the subject to blame Democrats, Biden, or the media, or to play it down. |
| Redirected to the Clintons | Turned the subject toward Bill or Hillary Clinton or another Democrat. |
| Said nothing | No public statement of any kind found in the record. |
| **Sept. 10, 2025 vote** | [Senate Vote #512](https://www.senate.gov/legislative/LIS/roll_call_votes/vote1191/vote_119_1_00512.htm): Republicans moved to table Schumer's amendment directing the Attorney General to release the Epstein documents. The motion passed 51 to 49. "Blocked release" means the member voted for that motion; "opposed blocking" means they voted against it. It is the only recorded Senate vote on releasing the files. |
| To table | Senate procedure: a vote to set an amendment aside, which kills it. |
| Unanimous consent | How the Senate passed the bill on Nov. 18, 2025: no roll call, no debate, no senator objected. Nobody had to vote yes to be counted as supporting it. |
| **Distinct actions** | How many different things the member actually said or did, after collapsing duplicates. The database can list the same act more than once (different outlets, or a dead-link copy), so "8 distinct actions (13 entries)" means 13 database rows describing 8 real acts. |
| All their statements ↗ | Opens every entry for that member in the live database. For a member who said nothing, it opens empty, which is the proof. |
| source ↗ | Opens the specific press release, post, or article a quote or fact came from. |
| Own voice / third party | Own voice is the member's own press release, floor remarks, or post. Third party is a news story, a fact-check, or an opponent's attack about them. An entry says which it is relying on. |
| (summary; source text not retrieved) | We could not fetch the source's text, so the entry paraphrases the database's one-line summary instead of quoting. |
| (link dead / blocked as of Sep 3 2026) | The source URL no longer loads or blocks automated checks; the quoted text was captured when the source was first gathered. |
| **Nov. 18, 2025 vote** (House) | [Roll Call 289](https://clerk.house.gov/Votes/2025289), final passage of H.R. 4405, 427 to 1. "Delegate (could not vote)" means a non-voting delegate; "Not yet seated" means the member took office after the vote. |
| **Discharge petition** (House) | [Petition #9 on H.Res. 581](https://clerk.house.gov/119/lrc/pd/petitions/DisPet0009.xml), filed by Thomas Massie on Sept. 2, 2025, to force the vote over the leadership's objection; 218 signatures were needed and were reached Nov. 12. "Signed" and "did not sign" are read from the Clerk's list. |
| Campaign plank / Attacks opponent over it / Called for release / Mentioned it (challengers) | Part III labels for candidates, who cast no vote: whether the files are a sustained part of their message, whether they aim them at a named opponent, whether they merely called for release, or only mentioned it in passing. |
| Worked against release | Voted No, or actively kept the bill off the floor or opposed release before the petition forced the vote, even if they voted yes in the end. |


<a id="part-i"></a>

# Part I. The Senate

*What every senator said about the Epstein files, read from the record, one entry each.*

The Senate never took a recorded vote on the Epstein Files Transparency Act. It passed on November 18, 2025 by unanimous consent, minutes after the House voted 427 to 1, which means no senator had to do anything to be counted as supporting it. That is why every entry below starts with a different vote.

On September 10, 2025, two months earlier, Senator Schumer offered an amendment directing the Attorney General to make the Epstein documents public. Republicans moved to table it, and the motion carried 51 to 49 ([Senate Vote #512](https://www.senate.gov/legislative/LIS/roll_call_votes/vote1191/vote_119_1_00512.htm)). Every Republican voted to table except Josh Hawley and Rand Paul. Every Democrat voted to keep the amendment alive. That is the one recorded Senate vote on releasing the files, and for most Republican senators it is the only affirmative act on the subject in their record.

Each entry gives the senator's vote on that motion, how many distinct things they actually did or said (duplicates collapsed), a link to their full record in the database, and then, in plain prose, what they said, with the lines that matter quoted exactly and linked to the specific source. Where a senator's record is entirely third-party reporting rather than their own words, the entry says so. Where a source link has died since the statement was gathered, the entry says that too.

## Entries


<a id="dan-sullivan"></a>

### Dan Sullivan (Republican-AK)
**Position:** Voted to block release (and deflected) · **Sept. 10, 2025 vote:** blocked release · **Distinct actions:** 4 (4 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Dan%20Sullivan)

Nothing in Sullivan's record is a release or post of his own; all four entries are reporting, and every quote below is his office as quoted by the press. In July 2025, after Trump ordered grand jury transcripts unsealed, his spokesperson said "Senator Sullivan believes the DOJ should release as much information as possible on Epstein's horrific crimes, while protecting victims." (source ↗ https://www.factcheck.org/2026/08/alaska-senate-candidates-attempt-to-link-each-other-to-epstein/) On September 10 he voted to table Schumer's amendment forcing release (vote #512, 51 to 49). His office defended the vote by attacking Schumer: "Chuck Schumer tried slipping the Epstein provision into the National Defense Authorization Act (NDAA), knowing full well it would be stripped out as the bill went through the process, leaving no doubt he's using Epstein's atrocious crimes as a political pawn instead of focusing on building up our military in the NDAA, which Schumer has never shown any interest in doing." (source ↗ https://thealaskacurrent.com/2025/09/12/murkowski-sullivan-vote-with-republicans-against-measure-seeking-release-of-epstein-files/) The Alaska Democratic Party called him "a deciding vote to continue the cover-up." (source ↗ https://thealaskacurrent.com/2025/09/12/murkowski-sullivan-vote-with-republicans-against-measure-seeking-release-of-epstein-files/) In November he was swept in with the rest of the Senate when the bill passed by unanimous consent, which is the vote his campaign now cites. In August 2026 he voted to confirm Todd Blanche as Attorney General; his office told KTUU he "supports his nomination," per the Alaska Current, which also relayed Meidas News reporting that his committees took $8,500 and $693 from two men who appear in Epstein's contacts and emails, neither accused of wrongdoing. (source ↗ https://thealaskacurrent.com/2026/08/10/dan-sullivan-took-nearly-10000-from-epstein-associates-then-voted-to-confirm-blanche/) FactCheck.org rated a Peltola ad calling his September vote "the deciding vote" misleading, and noted his own campaign put out an August 10 release accusing Peltola of taking money from "associates of Jeffrey Epstein," none of whom has been charged. (source ↗ https://www.factcheck.org/2026/08/alaska-senate-candidates-attempt-to-link-each-other-to-epstein/) Two of the four entries are the same Alaska Beacon story on two sites.

<a id="lisa-murkowski"></a>

### Lisa Murkowski (Republican-AK)
**Position:** Led the push (after voting to block release in September) · **Sept. 10, 2025 vote:** blocked release · **Distinct actions:** 8 (10 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Lisa%20Murkowski)

She co-led the Senate's counterpart bill, S. 2557, and the day the House passed its version she said she would vote for it "as written": "It's past time that all involved in Epstein's illegal operation be brought to justice, so the survivors of these awful crimes can have closure." (source ↗ https://www.murkowski.senate.gov/press/release/murkowski-its-past-time-that-everyone-involved-in-epsteins-illegal-operation-be-brought-to-justice) Two months earlier, though, she had voted with Republicans to table Schumer's amendment forcing release (vote #512, 51 to 49); she told the Alaska Beacon the vote was procedural and that "What Schumer did was nothing more than a political stunt," while describing herself as "a very early Republican on the Senate side who said, 'Look, just, just release these Epstein files.'" (source ↗ https://thealaskacurrent.com/2025/09/12/murkowski-sullivan-vote-with-republicans-against-measure-seeking-release-of-epstein-files/) After the law passed she became one of its hardest enforcers. With Merkley, Luján, Massie and Khanna she demanded a DOJ briefing by December 5 on the "new information" Bondi had cited to open investigations of Democrats (source ↗ https://www.merkley.senate.gov/merkley-murkowski-lujan-massie-khanna-demand-doj-briefing-on-release-of-the-epstein-files/). When DOJ missed the December 19 deadline she told Alaska's News Source "I'm not satisfied. I'm not satisfied. I mean, look at the pages and pages that are completely black, completely black," and "That's not what we passed" (source ↗ https://www.alaskasnewssource.com/2025/12/24/this-is-problem-murkowski-epstein-files-release/). She led, with Blumenthal and Merkley, the December letter asking the DOJ Inspector General to audit compliance; the letter says "By the Department's own admission, it is in violation of the law." The audit opened in April 2026 (source ↗ https://www.murkowski.senate.gov/press/release/murkowski-and-blumenthal-announce-inspector-general-audit-of-dojs-failure-to-release-the-full-epstein-files), and she posted that "we will not rest until the survivors receive the justice they are entitled to" (source ↗ https://x.com/lisamurkowski/status/2047748136244748361). In August 2026 she opposed Todd Blanche for Attorney General, listing "the handling of the release of the Epstein files" first among her reasons (source ↗ https://www.murkowski.senate.gov/press/release/murkowski-releases-statement-on-blanche-nomination). Four of her thirteen entries are dead-link duplicates of live ones; the IG letter PDF on her own site did not fetch.

<a id="katie-boyd-britt"></a>

### Katie Boyd Britt (Republican-AL)
**Position:** Deflected (blamed others or minimized) · **Sept. 10, 2025 vote:** blocked release · **Distinct actions:** 3 (7 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Katie%20Boyd%20Britt)

In July 2025 she backed the Appropriations Committee amendment requiring DOJ to report its Epstein holdings to Congress, telling 1819 News "Jeffrey Epstein was a sexual predator" and that "the American public get transparent, credible answers. President Trump and his administration are committed to that goal." (source ↗ https://1819news.com/news/item/katie-britt-supports-getting-transparent-credible-answers-on-jeffrey-epstein) On ABC News Live the same month her main point was Democrats: "I find it interesting that Democrats care about this at all because last time I checked, they were in charge for the last four years, and I can't seem to find any of them that were speaking up or compelling their own DOJ or their president of their own party to do this very same thing." (source ↗ https://1819news.com/news/item/britt-questions-democrats-sudden-interest-in-epstein-files-i-find-it-interesting-that-democrats-care-about-this-at-all) On September 10 she voted to table the amendment forcing release. When the bill passed by unanimous consent on November 18 her own release repeated the theme: "for Democrats, this was never about the victims. Under Joe Biden, Democrats did nothing to facilitate the release of these files, while Republicans have been calling for the release for years." She called passage "a historic step to ensuring justice for Epstein's victims and transparency for the American people." (source ↗ https://www.britt.senate.gov/news/press-releases/u-s-senator-katie-britt-releases-statement-following-unanimous-senate-passage-of-epstein-files-bill/) The remaining entries are an ABC video page, an aggregator copy of that release, and an AL.com story that no longer loads.

**Position:** Deflected (blamed others or minimized) · **Sept. 10, 2025 vote:** blocked release · **Distinct actions:** 4 (9 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Tommy%20Tuberville)

He added, "President Trump could care less about the Epstein files or whatever, but he's promised transparency. Again, but we want it the right way." Six of the ten entries are dead links or duplicate URLs, and two are YouTube pages with no transcript.

<a id="mark-kelly"></a>

### Mark Kelly (Democrat-AZ)
**Position:** Took action · **Sept. 10, 2025 vote:** opposed blocking · **Distinct actions:** 5 (7 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Mark%20Kelly)

He voted to keep Schumer's release amendment alive on September 10. On November 18, the day the House passed the bill, he spoke at a Capitol Hill press conference with survivors and House Democrats and signed the Democratic letter pressing Thune for an immediate vote: "From Epstein on down, the people who carried out and aided in these horrific crimes thought their wealth and power, meant that they would never have to answer for them," and "It doesn't matter if you're rich, it doesn't matter if you're a Republican or a Democrat. If you hurt people, you will answer for it." He accused Trump and Speaker Johnson of delaying Adelita Grijalva's swearing-in "just to delay accountability." (source ↗ https://www.kelly.senate.gov/newsroom/press-releases/watch-kelly-urges-accountability-for-epstein-victims/) The next morning he told KTAR that Trump "finally saw the writing on the wall that this was going to pass the House, and then he did a 180-degree turn," and that "investigators should follow the evidence here and if it makes sense that people get charged, they should be charged with crimes." (source ↗ https://ktar.com/arizona-news/epstein-files-mark-kelly-trump) After the release he posted: "If records tied to Donald Trump are missing from the Epstein files, that's not a coincidence. The FBI interviewed the victim multiple times, so those records exist. Where are they?" (source ↗ https://x.com/CaptMarkKelly/status/2027463536545243457) Two Facebook videos titled "It's time to release the Epstein files" have no retrievable text, the Thune letter PDF is a dead link, and an MSN item about him taunting a House Republican over the files is dead (summary; source text not retrieved).

<a id="ruben-gallego"></a>

### Ruben Gallego (Democrat-AZ)
**Position:** Led the push · **Sept. 10, 2025 vote:** opposed blocking · **Distinct actions:** 9 (12 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Ruben%20Gallego)

Gallego was the first senator to try to force the Senate on record. On July 17, 2025 he introduced a resolution demanding DOJ release the files and asked for unanimous consent from the floor: "If the Department of Justice has these files and there's nothing to hide, then release them and prove it. Trump's own people have told us a million times that they have it, and that they've promised to make it public, so bring it out. It doesn't matter who is implicated in that." (source ↗ https://www.gallego.senate.gov/news/press-releases/gallego-the-american-people-deserve-the-truth/) Markwayne Mullin objected. A week later Gallego tried again, offered to fold in Mullin's counter-resolution on grand jury records, and was blocked a second time; "my colleague chose to protect the powerful elites," he said. (source ↗ https://www.gallego.senate.gov/news/press-releases/watch-gallego-calls-on-the-justice-department-to-release-the-epstein-files-again/) The Hill reported Mullin called the effort "political theater" and noted Gallego had not asked for the files under Biden. (source ↗ https://thehill.com/homenews/senate/5418761-mullin-gallego-epstein-files/) On July 29 he and the Democrats on Homeland Security invoked 5 U.S.C. 2954, the five-member document demand, on Bondi; when nothing arrived they wrote again in August, "You have failed to comply and to date, the Committee has received no documents from your office," and set a September 2 deadline for a staff briefing. The same release notes his letter with Blumenthal condemning Maxwell's transfer to a minimum-security camp. (source ↗ https://www.gallego.senate.gov/news/press-releases/gallego-homeland-security-democrats-demand-doj-compliance-on-release-of-epstein-files/) He told Arizona PBS "We need to protect the victims and their identities. We need to protect the witnesses, but that's not what is happening here." (source ↗ https://azpbs.org/horizon/2025/07/senator-ruben-gallego-on-presidential-run-epstein-files/) He voted against blocking release in September and spoke on the floor when the House bill arrived in November (video only). A floor reading from a survivor's memoir and a 2026 comment calling a reported withholding of Trump-related files "the biggest political scandal" are in the record only as summaries (source text not retrieved; the first link is dead ↗ https://www.publicnow.com/view/028CC3FF93CA5B4F0CCC104B6893D76153E47036).

<a id="adam-b-schiff"></a>

### Adam B. Schiff (Democrat-CA)
**Position:** Led the push · **Sept. 10, 2025 vote:** opposed blocking · **Distinct actions:** 10 (12 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Adam%20B.%20Schiff)

Schiff worked the files from the Judiciary Committee and from cable television, and every item in his record is his own voice. At the September 2025 oversight hearing he pinned Kash Patel down on whether Epstein trafficked girls to anyone but himself ("is it your testimony today that in all the Epstein files that Jeffrey Epstein trafficked no one to anyone but himself?") and on who ordered Maxwell's transfer. (source ↗ https://www.schiff.senate.gov/news/press-releases/watch-sen-schiff-blasts-kash-patel-on-widespread-abuse-and-politicization-of-the-fbi-continued-obstruction-on-epstein-files/) On MSNBC he called the White House denial of Trump's signature on the birthday letter "another lie by Donald Trump" (source ↗ https://www.schiff.senate.gov/news/press-releases/watch-on-msnbc-sen-schiff-calls-out-trump-administrations-continued-denials-in-epstein-scandals-talks-trumps-retribution-campaign/), and when the estate emails surfaced in November he said the Blanche interview of Maxwell was designed to get "exonerating testimony for Donald Trump" and that "the administration is cherry picking what they provide to Congress." (source ↗ https://www.schiff.senate.gov/news/press-releases/watch-sen-schiff-condemns-trump-administrations-continued-stonewalling-on-epstein-files-pledges-to-push-measure-forcing-their-release-in-senate/) He voted against blocking release in September. The night Trump signed the law he predicted "more stonewalling, more cover up" (source ↗ https://www.schiff.senate.gov/news/press-releases/watch-sen-schiff-calls-on-trump-administration-to-release-full-epstein-files-after-trump-signs-bill-raises-alarm-on-trump-loyalists-at-doj-doing-presidents-bidding/), and in December he and Durbin asked the DOJ Inspector General to audit the chain-of-custody forms so the public could confirm "the Epstein files that will be released are identical to those collected by law enforcement," citing reports that about 1,000 FBI personnel had been told to flag mentions of Trump. (source ↗ https://www.schiff.senate.gov/news/press-releases/news-schiff-durbin-raise-concerns-about-epstein-files-tampering-demand-audit-of-dojs-handling-of-files/) He then signed the Blumenthal-Murkowski-Merkley letter that produced the IG compliance audit. (source ↗ https://www.schiff.senate.gov/news/press-releases/news-sens-schiff-blumenthal-murkowski-merkley-colleagues-call-for-inspector-general-audit-following-dojs-failure-to-release-the-full-epstein-files-as-demanded-by-survivors-requ/) When DOJ sent Congress its list of "politically exposed" people in February 2026, he posted that it "includes absurd references to Beyoncé, Margaret Thatcher, Princess Diana, AOC, and me" and called it "their latest brazen attempt to protect Trump, instead of victims." (source ↗ https://x.com/SenAdamSchiff/status/2023513050054066473) He also called Tom Barrack's reported contacts with Epstein "reprehensible and disqualifying." (source ↗ https://x.com/SenAdamSchiff/status/2023586392731795886)

<a id="christopher-murphy"></a>

### Christopher Murphy (Democrat-CT)
**Position:** Took action · **Sept. 10, 2025 vote:** opposed blocking · **Distinct actions:** 6 (11 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Christopher%20Murphy)

Murphy's record is television and social media rather than letters or hearings, and most of it is reporting on what he said. He voted against blocking release in September. At the Texas Tribune Festival on November 14, after the House released estate emails, he told The Bulwark's Sam Stein that Trump "is in daily panic mode as we get closer to the moment these files and the fullest truth that we have available to us is going to be made public," and went further than most colleagues: "Clearly, Donald Trump was at the center of a child sex ring." That is Murphy's allegation, not a finding. (source ↗ https://thehill.com/homenews/senate/5607275-chris-murphy-donald-trump-jeffrey-epstein-files/) On CNN on November 20, asked whether more Democrats might be named, he answered "So what?" and continued: "I'm sure there are Democrats in those files, there are Republicans in those files, there are Wall Street executives who have no political affiliation. What we want is to understand who was involved in this." He added, "The law is the law. They need to release the files." (source ↗ https://thehill.com/homenews/senate/5615755-murphy-epstein-files-release/) That exchange accounts for four of his eleven entries (The Hill, HuffPost, Mediaite, and the CNN clip). When NPR reported in February 2026 that DOJ had withheld files tied to an allegation against Trump, he posted: "The White House is obsessed with hiding the President's involvement in a child sex ring. It's what they spend most of their time on." (source ↗ https://x.com/ChrisMurphyCT/status/2026298958973141342) A March 2026 post about "Trump's team of Epstein class advisors" is an economic jab, not a files statement. (source ↗ https://x.com/ChrisMurphyCT/status/2033888545077706936) Three Google News shell links and a PressReader page carry no text.

<a id="richard-blumenthal"></a>

### Richard Blumenthal (Democrat-CT)
**Position:** Led the push · **Sept. 10, 2025 vote:** opposed blocking · **Distinct actions:** 7 (9 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Richard%20Blumenthal)

Blumenthal used every lever available to a minority senator. On July 31, 2025 he and Schumer invoked the "Rule of Five" statute on the Homeland Security Committee to demand the DOJ and FBI files; he told WSHU "What's at stake here is more than the president's promises. It's the credibility of our justice system, and the question whether there was proper criminal prosecution, whether it was complete and full, whether it was accurate, and what additional facts the American people are entitled to know." (source ↗ https://www.wshu.org/news/2025-07-31/epstein-files-release-donald-trump) He voted against blocking release in September. On December 19, the statutory deadline, after Blanche said on Fox that the full files would not come out, he called the partial release "so offensive and utterly unacceptable" and said "I don't think the Trump Administration can be trusted to follow the law, which is why I'll be calling for an audit by the Department of Justice's Office of the Inspector General to backstop the concealment Deputy Attorney General Blanche has already admitted to doing." (source ↗ https://www.blumenthal.senate.gov/newsroom/press/release/blumenthal-statement-on-the-dojs-failure-to-release-the-full-epstein-files-as-demanded-by-survivors-required-by-law) Five days later he, Murkowski and Merkley led twelve senators in asking the Acting Inspector General for exactly that audit, writing that "By the Department's own admission, it is in violation of the law." (source ↗ https://www.blumenthal.senate.gov/newsroom/press/release/blumenthal-murkowski-and-merkley-lead-call-for-inspector-general-audit-following-dojs-failure-to-release-the-full-epstein-files-as-demanded-by-survivors-required-by-law) The IG opened the audit in April 2026; he and Murkowski said "we will not rest until the survivors receive the justice they are entitled to." (source ↗ https://www.blumenthal.senate.gov/newsroom/press/release/blumenthal-and-murkowski-announce-inspector-general-audit-of-dojs-failure-to-release-the-full-epstein-files) At a hearing he told Blanche, of the survivors, "I'm asking you to apologize to them" (summary; source text not retrieved), and an MSN clip has him saying he has no trust that this Justice Department will be fair (summary; source text not retrieved).

<a id="mazie-k-hirono"></a>

### Mazie K. Hirono (Democrat-HI)
**Position:** Took action · **Sept. 10, 2025 vote:** opposed blocking · **Distinct actions:** 6 (9 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Mazie%20K.%20Hirono)

Hirono's record is mostly short posts, all her own. In 2025 she wrote on Facebook: "Every day it becomes more and more clear why Trump is so desperate to keep the Epstein Files hidden." (source ↗ https://www.facebook.com/senatorhirono/posts/every-day-it-becomes-more-and-more-clear-why-trump-is-so-desperate-to-keep-the-e/1361939981969412/) On September 5 she posted that "After calling the stories of Epstein survivors a 'hoax', Trump called on SCOTUS to reverse a ruling that found him liable for sexual abuse." (source ↗ https://x.com/maziehirono/status/1963959072773829079) She voted against blocking release five days later and cosponsored the Senate bill. She signed the December 24 letter asking the DOJ Inspector General to audit compliance (her office posted the letter; the PDF did not fetch, and the PBS report on it is dead). In 2026 she turned to Todd Blanche's nomination, posting survivors' descriptions of their meeting with him, "We left feeling demoralized," "Mr. Blanche talked around our questions," "We were gaslit," and asking "Are Republicans really going to confirm this callousness to lead our DOJ?" (source ↗ https://x.com/maziehirono/status/2080348304450232709) Three office videos on Blanche and survivors have no retrievable text.

<a id="chuck-grassley"></a>

### Chuck Grassley (Republican-IA)
**Position:** Took action (after voting to block release in September) · **Sept. 10, 2025 vote:** blocked release · **Distinct actions:** 7 (10 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Chuck%20Grassley)

Grassley, who chairs Judiciary, dates his interest to 2019, when he and Ron Johnson asked Attorney General Barr to make the FBI and IG findings on Epstein's death public; the database mis-dates that letter to July 2025. (source ↗ https://www.grassley.senate.gov/news/news-releases/johnson-grassley-call-transparency-epstein-investigations) At the September 2025 FBI oversight hearing he asked Kash Patel "was Jeffrey Epstein an intelligence asset for the U.S. Government or a foreign government?" (Patel: "Mr. Epstein was not a source for the FBI") and got a commitment to provide records "I am legally permitted to." (source ↗ https://www.grassley.senate.gov/news/news-releases/grassley-questions-patel-at-fbi-oversight-hearing) On September 10 he voted to table the amendment forcing release. When the House bill arrived in November he told KMA "I've supported full transparency on the Epstein files since I first asked for it in 2019" and that he would have voted yes on a "clean" bill. (source ↗ https://www.kmaland.com/news/grassley-supports-full-transparency-on-epstein-files/article_ce71f790-1e6f-4248-9a0a-3883ebc77d32.html) Asked in Avoca in February 2026 whether he was happy with what DOJ had released: "If they've released every page that they tell us they've released, yes. If they're holding back some, that's violation of the law and I expect every page to get out." (source ↗ https://www.3newsnow.com/southwest-iowa/sen-chuck-grassley-discusses-epstein-files-voter-id-requirements-at-avoca-high-school-visit) Chairing Todd Blanche's confirmation hearing he listed the allegations against him, "problematic redactions, insufficient efforts to follow investigative leads, a refusal to meet with victims," and then invited him to "take the opportunity to respond," which Blanche used to say about 1% of redactions had to be fixed and that "for four years there wasn't a peep about Jeffrey Epstein." (source ↗ https://www.grassley.senate.gov/news/news-releases/grassley-questions-blanche-at-attorney-general-nomination-hearing) Two MSN items report he took criticism for framing those errors as transparency (no text retrieved).

**Position:** Marked the vote only · **Sept. 10, 2025 vote:** blocked release · **Distinct actions:** 2 (2 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Joni%20Ernst)

<a id="mike-crapo"></a>

### Mike Crapo (Republican-ID)
**Position:** Voted to block release · **Sept. 10, 2025 vote:** blocked release · **Distinct actions:** 3 (3 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Mike%20Crapo)

Nothing in Crapo's record is a release of his own; all three entries are Idaho reporting, so what follows is attributed. As Finance Committee chairman he controls subpoenas for the Treasury records on Epstein's banking. The Idaho Statesman reported, citing a Wyden aide, that Crapo's staff reviewed those records with Wyden's in February 2024 and that Crapo declined Wyden's request to jointly subpoena Bank of America in September 2024 and again in 2025. His statement to the paper did not address the request: "Jeffrey Epstein was a notorious sex trafficker who committed appalling crimes. The House Oversight Committee is now leading an 'investigation of the investigators' and is the appropriate committee to uncover why Epstein was not properly investigated and prosecuted earlier." (source ↗ https://www.idahostatesman.com/news/politics-government/state-politics/article312696577.html) On September 10 he voted to table the amendment forcing release; his spokesperson called it an "attempt by the minority to control the Senate floor" and "delay gimmicks and tactics against our nation's defense." (source ↗ https://www.idahostatesman.com/news/politics-government/state-politics/article312696577.html) An Idaho Capital Sun column asked why he "put on ice his committee's oversight jurisdiction over the Treasury Department" and said his office did not respond. (source ↗ https://idahocapitalsun.com/2025/10/07/idahos-u-s-sen-mike-crapo-and-the-release-of-the-jeffrey-epstein-files/) He voted for the bill by unanimous consent in November, without a statement in the record.

<a id="richard-j-durbin"></a>

### Richard J. Durbin (Democrat-IL)
**Position:** Led the push · **Sept. 10, 2025 vote:** opposed blocking · **Distinct actions:** 11 (12 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Richard%20J.%20Durbin)

As Judiciary's ranking member Durbin ran the most sustained oversight campaign on the Democratic side. In July 2025 he and Van Hollen got the Appropriations Committee to adopt, unanimously, an amendment ordering DOJ to preserve the Epstein records and report to Congress, then wrote Bondi to release them without waiting: "There is no reason to wait until the bill with our amendment makes its way through Congress," with an August 16 deadline. (source ↗ https://www.durbin.senate.gov/newsroom/press-releases/durbin-van-hollen-call-on-attorney-general-to-immediately-release-the-epstein-files) The same month he wrote Bondi, Patel and Bongino laying out the contradictions between Bondi's "sitting on my desk" claim and the July 7 memo, and disclosed a whistleblower account that about 1,000 FBI personnel were put on 24-hour shifts in March and "were instructed to 'flag' any records in which President Trump was mentioned." (source ↗ https://www.durbin.senate.gov/newsroom/press-releases/durbin-presses-bondi-patel-bongino-on-rifts-between-doj-fbi-white-house-on-epstein-files) He asked Blanche for transparency after the Maxwell interview and for a commitment not to trade a pardon for her cooperation, and cosponsored Merkley's Senate bill. (source ↗ https://www.durbin.senate.gov/newsroom/press-releases/durbin-duckworth-join-colleagues-in-demanding-public-release-of-epstein-files) On September 10 he voted against blocking release and said "The Epstein files must come to light, in a manner that protects survivors of his abuse." (source ↗ https://www.durbin.senate.gov/newsroom/press-releases/durbin-statement-after-senate-republicans-voted-against-releasing-the-epstein-files) On November 18 he co-led the letter to Thune and, after passage, said "No more. No more lies, no more secrets." (source ↗ https://www.durbin.senate.gov/newsroom/press-releases/durbin-statement-on-senate-passing-bill-to-release-the-epstein-files) In December he and Schiff asked the IG to audit the chain-of-custody forms, and he signed the bipartisan IG compliance letter. When DOJ missed the deadline: "After mishandling the Epstein files all year, the Trump Administration is now violating federal law to protect the rich and powerful," and "They had a choice: survivors or Donald Trump. They chose the latter." (source ↗ https://www.durbin.senate.gov/newsroom/press-releases/durbin-statement-on-trump-administration-failure-to-release-the-complete-epstein-files) In 2026 he, Merkley, Murkowski and Luján asked GAO to audit DOJ's redaction decisions and "whether the release of these files has served to cover up child sexual abuse." (source ↗ https://www.durbin.senate.gov/newsroom/press-releases/bipartisan-group-of-senators-call-for-independent-audit-of-epstein-files-redactions-protocol) At Blanche's July 15, 2026 hearing he asked, "Can I get your word under oath that within the next 30 days, you will personally sit down" with survivors; Blanche said "I have somebody available to talk to them." (source ↗ https://x.com/Acyn/status/2077395711973597489) Eight of the eighteen entries are dead duplicates from his site's redesign.

(source ↗ https://news.wttw.com/2025/07/22/sen-tammy-duckworth-epstein-files-public-broadcasting-and-midterm-elections) In August she cosponsored Merkley's Senate bill: "If there's nothing in the files, there's no reason to hide them. <a id="rand-paul"></a>

### Rand Paul (Republican-KY)
**Position:** Led the push · **Sept. 10, 2025 vote:** opposed blocking · **Distinct actions:** 6 (7 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Rand%20Paul)

Paul was one of two Republicans who refused to table Schumer's amendment on September 10, joining every Democrat in the 49 votes to keep it alive. (source ↗ https://thehill.com/homenews/senate/5497260-hawley-paul-epstein-amendment/) He cosponsored the Senate bill. He told Reason's Nick Gillespie in November that "Donald Trump was for it before he was against it, before he was now for it," and was candid about his own interest: "I have no idea what's in the files. It really has not been a big pressing issue for me," but "it became a symbol for the idea of: does your government treat people differently whether they're wealthy or not?" (Reason labels the transcript AI-generated.) (source ↗ https://reason.com/podcast/2025/11/20/rand-paul-congress-is-afraid-of-the-president/) When DOJ produced a heavily redacted partial release on December 19, he told ABC's This Week it was "a big mistake": "the administration has struggled for months and months with something they initially ginned up and then sort of tried to tamp down," and "my suggestion would be: Give up all the information ... be transparent and release everything the law requires of you." (source ↗ https://thehill.com/homenews/senate/5659116-trump-administration-epstein-release/) Two days later, in his Festivus grievances, he posted a fully blacked-out note he said he had sent to DOJ for review: "They blacked out every single word. Why would they do that? Who are they protecting from my grievances??" (source ↗ https://www.mediaite.com/politics/who-are-they-protecting-rand-paul-swipes-at-trump-over-epstein-files-in-airing-of-grievances/) A Cuomo interview, an MSN item on restoring trust, and a Google News item with Massie carry no text.

<a id="john-kennedy"></a>

### John Kennedy (Republican-LA)
**Position:** Took action (after voting to block release in September) · **Sept. 10, 2025 vote:** blocked release · **Distinct actions:** 5 (10 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=John%20Kennedy)

Kennedy talked more than most Republicans and voted with leadership when it counted. At the September 2025 Judiciary oversight hearing he asked Kash Patel, "You've seen most of the files. Who, if anyone did Epstein traffic these young women to besides himself," and got the answer "Himself. There's no credible information, none," an exchange preserved in a colleague's release. (source ↗ https://www.schiff.senate.gov/news/press-releases/watch-sen-schiff-blasts-kash-patel-on-widespread-abuse-and-politicization-of-the-fbi-continued-obstruction-on-epstein-files/) Days earlier he had voted to table the amendment forcing release. On CNN on November 12, as the House discharge petition hit 218, he said "Epstein was a pig, a P-I-G, pig," that "my people want to know who else, if anyone, Epstein trafficked young women to," and "as a moral principle, I think they ought to release the records," adding "I may end up with a sombrero on my head for saying that," a reference to Trump's AI video mocking Hakeem Jeffries. (source ↗ https://www.thedailybeast.com/maga-senator-john-kennedy-fears-sombrero-treatment-from-donald-trump-for-wanting-epstein-files-released/) He would not commit to the House text, saying the Senate version "would not be identical." (source ↗ https://www.tampafp.com/epstein-was-a-pig-louisiana-sen-john-kennedy-delivers-blistering-assessment-of-disgraced-financier/) Entries on questioning Bondi and, in January 2026, Todd Blanche about the unreleased files are Google News shells with no text. Six of his ten entries carry no readable text.

<a id="edward-j-markey"></a>

### Edward J. Markey (Democrat-MA)
**Position:** Marked the vote only · **Sept. 10, 2025 vote:** opposed blocking · **Distinct actions:** 1 (3 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Edward%20J.%20Markey)

He voted against blocking release in September and signed the November 18 letter to Thune, but nothing in his record is about releasing the files. All three entries concern the August 2026 primary, when Seth Moulton claimed Markey's "name appears repeatedly" in the DOJ library because a lobbyist paid by Epstein, working for a jailed Senegalese politician, wrote in a 2015 email that he had met Markey. Markey told GBH: "I never had a meeting. I never, at any time, did anything," and called it "McCarthy-esque-like smears." His campaign said it checked calendars and no meeting occurred; GBH noted nothing in Moulton's timeline suggests Markey dealt with Epstein or knew who was paying the lobbyist. (source ↗ https://www.wgbh.org/news/politics/2026-08-05/desperate-and-absolutely-inaccurate-markey-tears-into-moulton-over-epstein-claims) A PolitiFact check that disputed Moulton's claim is a dead link (summary only).

<a id="elizabeth-warren"></a>

### Elizabeth Warren (Democrat-MA)
**Position:** Led the push · **Sept. 10, 2025 vote:** opposed blocking · **Distinct actions:** 6 (10 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Elizabeth%20Warren)

Warren's pressure runs at the banks and regulators rather than at DOJ. She voted against blocking release in September. In January 2026 she posted that "The Epstein scandal is an example of how rich and powerful insiders protect each other, regardless of politics." (source ↗ https://x.com/ewarren/status/2011492087812755495) As Banking's ranking member she wrote the Fed, OCC and FDIC that their replies to her earlier request "failed to confirm an ongoing investigation or even commit to opening one" into bankers who may have aided Epstein, demanded one be announced by March 12, and called for hearings. (source ↗ https://www.banking.senate.gov/newsroom/minority/warren-highlights-failure-of-trump-regulators-to-hold-epstein-connected-bankers-accountable) She wrote Fed chair nominee Kevin Warsh about his appearance on a 2010 St. Barts guest list in the released files, saying "it is essential the public fully understand the extent of any interactions or relationship you had with Jeffrey Epstein" (source ↗ https://www.banking.senate.gov/newsroom/minority/warren-probes-trump-fed-nominee-kevin-warsh-on-appearances-in-the-epstein-files), and later posted that when she asked whether his "secret $100 million" was in Epstein-linked vehicles, "He refused to answer." (source ↗ https://x.com/SenWarren/status/2046649111290548378) When Bondi was fired on April 2 she wrote that Bondi "will be remembered for blocking the release of the Epstein files." (source ↗ https://x.com/SenWarren/status/2039759764536569946) In July she wrote Jamie Dimon citing released Epstein-Mandelson emails about a call to the U.K. Chancellor "you reportedly made," with answers due July 24. (source ↗ https://www.banking.senate.gov/newsroom/minority/warren-presses-jpmorgan-ceo-on-banks-epstein-ties-following-resurfaced-emails) A Barclays inquiry and the Forbes story on the Dimon letter have no text.

<a id="chris-van-hollen"></a>

### Chris Van Hollen (Democrat-MD)
**Position:** Led the push · **Sept. 10, 2025 vote:** opposed blocking · **Distinct actions:** 7 (11 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Chris%20Van%20Hollen)

Van Hollen, ranking member on the appropriations subcommittee that funds DOJ, got the first Republican votes for anything on the files. In July 2025 the Appropriations Committee unanimously adopted his amendment with Durbin ordering DOJ to preserve the Epstein records and report to Congress on the case, the co-conspirators, and Epstein's "connections (if any) to the United States Government or foreign governments"; he and Durbin then wrote Bondi to release the files without waiting, by August 16. (source ↗ https://www.vanhollen.senate.gov/news/press-releases/van-hollen-durbin-call-on-ag-bondi-to-immediately-release-epstein-files) He told the Baltimore Banner Republicans "didn't want to be seen as standing in the way of transparency and accountability." (source ↗ https://www.thebanner.com/politics-power/national-politics/van-hollen-raskin-epstein-files-VJMJMWPLL5DCHLJINV6EO52VIM/) On a Crooked Media podcast on September 4 he said the Senate's unanimous consent request had been blocked and added, "the Attorney General, Pam Bondi, has told us for years that these documents should be released. So just release the damn files." (source ↗ https://crooked.com/podcast/senator-van-hollen-on-epstein-release-the-damn-files/) He voted against blocking release six days later. At a February 10, 2026 hearing his questioning got Commerce Secretary Howard Lutnick to acknowledge visiting Epstein's island in 2012, after Lutnick had said publicly he was "never in the room with him socially, for business, or even philanthropy"; he and Merkley then wrote Lutnick that "If, as you say, you have nothing to hide, then producing these records should be a straightforward matter." (source ↗ https://www.vanhollen.senate.gov/news/press-releases/van-hollen-merkley-press-lutnick-for-answers-on-epstein-ties) At a May 19 hearing Todd Blanche told him "Absolutely" when asked if he would meet survivors; on July 14 he and Patty Murray wrote that two months later "the survivors have not received the promised outreach" and that "Your responsibility as Acting Attorney General is to pursue justice, not to shield the President." (source ↗ https://www.vanhollen.senate.gov/news/press-releases/van-hollen-murray-call-out-blanche-for-failing-to-meet-with-epstein-survivors-press-him-to-make-good-on-his-commitment) A New Yorker Radio Hour interview in the record contains no Epstein quote from him; five entries are dead or duplicate URLs.

<a id="angus-s-king-jr"></a>

### Angus S. King, Jr. (Independent-ME)
**Position:** Marked the vote only · **Sept. 10, 2025 vote:** opposed blocking · **Distinct actions:** 1 (1 entry in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Angus%20S.%20King%2C%20Jr.)

He voted against blocking release in September. His only entry is the November 18 caucus letter to Thune, which he signed with 46 others and posted on his site; it says the administration has "obfuscated, misdirected, and obstructed efforts in both the Senate and the House to secure release of the Epstein files" and cites the newly released Epstein emails about Trump. (source ↗ https://www.king.senate.gov/newsroom/press-releases/king-colleagues-call-on-majority-leader-thune-to-immediately-bring-epstein-files-transparency-act-to-floor-for-vote) No words of his own on the files, and nothing since.

<a id="susan-m-collins"></a>

### Susan M. Collins (Republican-ME)
**Position:** Voted to block release · **Sept. 10, 2025 vote:** blocked release · **Distinct actions:** 3 (3 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Susan%20M.%20Collins)

As Appropriations chair she presided over the July 2025 markup that unanimously adopted the Van Hollen-Durbin amendment requiring DOJ to preserve and report on the files. On September 10 she voted to table Schumer's amendment forcing release, and her office blamed Democrats: "When Republicans tried to move that bill to the Senate floor as part of an appropriations package, the Democratic Senator who authored that provision in committee blocked it," and "If Democrats hadn't blocked it from going to the floor, this amendment could have already been passed by the Senate." The Maine Democratic Party said she "chose to protect Donald Trump and the rich and powerful." (source ↗ https://www.pressherald.com/2025/09/11/collins-votes-with-senate-republicans-to-defeat-democrats-effort-to-force-the-release-of-epstein-files/) Nine weeks later, when the bill passed by unanimous consent, she said "I support the release of the Epstein files. This is an important step for the survivors of these heinous crimes," and pointed back to the July amendment. (source ↗ https://www.collins.senate.gov/newsroom/senator-collins-statement-on-senate-approval-to-release-the-epstein-files) Nothing on DOJ's compliance since.

<a id="elissa-slotkin"></a>

### Elissa Slotkin (Democrat-MI)
**Position:** Took action · **Sept. 10, 2025 vote:** opposed blocking · **Distinct actions:** 5 (5 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Elissa%20Slotkin)

Her record is interviews and posts. On PBS's Firing Line in July 2025 she said "The president and his allies have created so much anticipation about these files at this point, it's just weird that they're not releasing them, right? The president fomented this." Fox News answered with a 2020 clip in which she said the Clinton-Epstein connection was not "front of mind"; her spokesperson replied that Trump "has been named in the very files he fanned the flames on." (source ↗ https://www.foxnews.com/politics/democrats-hammering-republicans-epstein-one-senator-brushed-off-issue-years-ago) She voted against blocking release in September and signed the November 18 letter to Thune. After Trump called her and five colleagues seditious over their video to the military, she told ABC "He's trying to distract us from the big stories of last week, which were the Epstein files and then the economy." (source ↗ https://www.salon.com/2025/11/23/american-public-isnt-stupid-slotkin-says-trumps-sedition-threats-are-a-distraction/) A Distill Social clip has her saying the files are being slow-walked and redacted to protect Trump (caption only). On February 24, 2026 she posted: "By refusing to release the full Epstein Files, President Trump is certainly acting like someone guilty of something. So he might as well just do it." (source ↗ https://x.com/SenatorSlotkin/status/2026362387192750329) The Bulwark episode in her file is about illegal orders, not the files.

### Gary C. Peters (Democrat-MI)
**Position:** Led the push · **Sept. 10, 2025 vote:** opposed blocking · **Distinct actions:** 3 (1 entry in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Gary%20C.%20Peters)

His one database entry describes three acts. As ranking member of Homeland Security he invoked 5 U.S.C. 2954 in July 2025 to demand the files from DOJ, followed up in August, and, his office says, "The Trump Administration refused to comply with both of these requests." He voted against blocking release in September. On November 18 he led the 47-signature letter to Thune, which says the victims and the public "deserve answers, accountability and the truth. So far, they have only seen empty promises from President Trump and his Administration." (source ↗ https://www.peters.senate.gov/newsroom/press-releases/peters-leads-colleagues-in-calling-on-senate-majority-leader-to-quickly-bring-the-epstein-files-transparency-act-to-floor-for-vote) He also signed the December letter that produced the DOJ Inspector General's compliance audit. (source ↗ https://www.blumenthal.senate.gov/newsroom/press/release/blumenthal-murkowski-and-merkley-lead-call-for-inspector-general-audit-following-dojs-failure-to-release-the-full-epstein-files-as-demanded-by-survivors-required-by-law)

<a id="amy-klobuchar"></a>

### Amy Klobuchar (Democrat-MN)
**Position:** Took action · **Sept. 10, 2025 vote:** opposed blocking · **Distinct actions:** 3 (7 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Amy%20Klobuchar)

Seven of her eleven entries are the same July 20, 2025 CNN interview. Jake Tapper asked why she and other Judiciary Democrats had not pushed for the files under Biden; she answered that "the president blaming Democrats for this disaster, Jake, is like that CEO that got caught on camera blaming Coldplay," and "He was president when Epstein got indicted for these charges and went to prison. He was president when Epstein committed suicide." She acknowledged "the people that have been fomenting this are right-wing influencers, members of Congress." (source ↗ https://thehill.com/homenews/senate/5410550-klobuchar-defends-democrats-inaction-epstein/) Mediaite called the answer a "bizarre deflection"; Townhall ran a similar piece. She voted against blocking release in September. On November 18 she and Tina Smith wrote Thune demanding a vote: "This case is about exploitation and abuse, and these victims deserve justice. I will vote to release these files because this transparency is owed to the American people." (source ↗ https://www.smith.senate.gov/u-s-senators-amy-klobuchar-tina-smith-call-for-senate-vote-to-release-the-epstein-files/) She signed the December letter for the Inspector General audit. (source ↗ https://www.blumenthal.senate.gov/newsroom/press/release/blumenthal-murkowski-and-merkley-lead-call-for-inspector-general-audit-following-dojs-failure-to-release-the-full-epstein-files-as-demanded-by-survivors-required-by-law) A Politico entry points to the site's homepage and has no text.

**Position:** Took action · **Sept. 10, 2025 vote:** opposed blocking · **Distinct actions:** 3 (2 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Tina%20Smith)

Her earliest act predates the files fight: in May 2023 she wrote Jamie Dimon that "JPMorgan's decision to turn a blind eye to such egregious misconduct raises serious questions about its role in facilitating Epstein's abuse," with questions due May 24, 2023; the database carries that release three times under a 2025 date. (source ↗ https://www.smith.senate.gov/u-s-senator-tina-smith-presses-jpmorgan-ceo-for-answers-on-banks-ties-to-jeffery-epstein/) She voted against blocking release in September 2025. On November 18 she and Klobuchar wrote Thune: "These young girls were misled and abused by powerful men who thought that the rules didn't apply to them," and "Should the Trump Administration continue their efforts to hide documents from public view even after this legislation passes, Congress should seek further action." That release also appears three times. (source ↗ https://www.smith.senate.gov/u-s-senators-amy-klobuchar-tina-smith-call-for-senate-vote-to-release-the-epstein-files/) A February 2026 podcast appearance is listed under the files but the page text has her discussing ICE detention in Minnesota, not Epstein.

<a id="eric-schmitt"></a>

### Eric Schmitt (Republican-MO)
**Position:** Voted to block release (after calling for release) · **Sept. 10, 2025 vote:** blocked release · **Distinct actions:** 4 (7 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Eric%20Schmitt)

The Kansas City Star laid out the sequence. In January 2025 Schmitt told Benny Johnson "Open this stuff up. Release the Epstein files," and "I'd vote for declassifying"; in March he said he would "absolutely" vote to release them and "there's no reason any of that stuff should be secret." After the July DOJ memo he told Politico "I don't know what they have. I don't know what they don't have," and "It's just hard for me to opine on it." His spokesperson then said "All credible information related to the Epstein files that can be released should be released," praised "the many additional steps President Trump is taking," and added that "Democrats in the Media and in Congress" only "care about attacking President Trump." (source ↗ https://www.kansascity.com/news/politics-government/article311321830.html) Missourinet reported he backed Bondi's handling (summary; link dead as of Sep 2026 ↗ https://www.missourinet.com/2025/07/22/missouri-sen-eric-schmitt-backs-bondi-on-handling-of-epstein-files/). On September 10 he voted to table the amendment forcing release, then for the bill by unanimous consent in November with nothing in the record. Two aggregator pages and two Google News shells repeat the January quote without text.

<a id="josh-hawley"></a>

### Josh Hawley (Republican-MO)
**Position:** Took action · **Sept. 10, 2025 vote:** opposed blocking · **Distinct actions:** 3 (6 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Josh%20Hawley)

Hawley was one of two Republicans to vote against tabling Schumer's amendment on September 10. "This has been my consistent position," he told reporters. "My position has long been I think we ought to release those files and trust the American people, just like we did with the MLK files and the JFK files." He noted Trump himself had said the attorney general should release everything unclassified: "I agree with that." (source ↗ https://thehill.com/homenews/senate/5497260-hawley-paul-epstein-amendment/) Two months earlier, after the July DOJ memo, he had told KMOV "The DOJ should release whatever they can. Whatever is not under court order and sealed, and, personally, I think they oughta go to court and ask the court to unseal everything possible," so "we can finally put the Epstein chapter behind us." (source ↗ https://www.firstalert4.com/2025/07/17/sen-hawley-calls-transparency-finally-put-epstein-chapter-behind-us/) Missourinet reported he was "very skeptical" of the memo and suggested an independent review (summary; link dead). Two YouTube entries and a Fox News link carry no text. Nothing in the record after the law passed.

<a id="thom-tillis"></a>

### Thom Tillis (Republican-NC)
**Position:** Took action (with a Voted to block release vote in the middle) · **Sept. 10, 2025 vote:** blocked release · **Distinct actions:** 7 (10 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Thom%20Tillis)

Tillis was the loudest Senate Republican for release in July 2025, and then voted to block it in September. On July 16, on Charlotte's WBT radio, he broke with Trump's "boring" line: "I have to disagree with the president. I don't think human trafficking of young teenage girls being exploited by billionaires on a private island is boring. I think it's despicable, and I believe that anybody who had anything to do with it or knowledge of it should be held accountable," and, "Just release the damn files." (source ↗ https://thehill.com/homenews/senate/5406204-3-conservative-backlash-over-epstein-files/) The same day he told McClatchy the only reasons to withhold were "you're embarrassed because you overplayed what was in it or what is in it is problematic," and neither was good enough (source ↗ https://www.charlotteobserver.com/news/politics-government/article310813735.html). A week later, at an Axios event, he dismissed the victim-privacy objection ("that's called redaction. We do that all the time") and warned the issue would be "like those zombies in The Walking Dead" through the 2026 election (source ↗ https://www.mediaite.com/politics/release-the-damn-files-top-republican-warns-epstein-saga-will-haunt-trump-gop-through-midterms/), then told ABC, "I don't know what's ambiguous about me saying we should release the files. I still stand by that." (source ↗ https://abcnews.com/Politics/tillis-continues-push-trump-administration-release-epstein-files/story?id=123997754) Seven weeks after that he voted with his party to table Schumer's amendment; no explanation appears in the record. He supported the bill by unanimous consent in November. During Todd Blanche's 2026 confirmation for Attorney General he was reported to have conditioned his vote on Blanche meeting Epstein survivors (summary; the Google News source returned no text), and after that meeting he posted: "I commend Todd Blanche for doing what all his predecessors over the last two decades never did: meet with the victims of Jeffery Epstein's horrific crimes." (source ↗ https://x.com/SenThomTillis/status/2077881334349853123) Six of the ten entries are July coverage of the same two interviews.

<a id="cory-a-booker"></a>

### Cory A. Booker (Democrat-NJ)
**Position:** Led the push · **Sept. 10, 2025 vote:** opposed blocking · **Distinct actions:** 9 (13 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Cory%20A.%20Booker)

Booker used the Judiciary Committee against two Trump nominees over the files. On July 16, 2025 he wrote to Emil Bove, then a Third Circuit nominee and formerly acting Deputy Attorney General, demanding records of his role in the DOJ's Epstein decisions, writing that "it warrants scrutiny whether the DOJ intentionally withheld evidence related to the trafficking and sexual abuse of minors to protect certain individuals" and setting a 9 a.m. next-day deadline (source ↗ https://www.booker.senate.gov/news/press/booker-demands-answers-on-emil-boves-involvement-in-doj-withholding-the-epstein-files). On MSNBC he said, "Either Bove and the Justice Department are lying about the Epstein situation…or they are showing a dangerous lack of transparency." (source ↗ https://www.booker.senate.gov/news/press/booker-either-bove-and-the-justice-department-are-lying-about-the-epstein-situationor-they-are-showing-a-dangerous-lack-of-transparency) He offered his own release amendment (a Senate video page with no text) and was reported by MSN to have called Trump a liar on the files (link dead; no text). He cosponsored the Senate bill, voted to keep Schumer's amendment alive, and signed the November 18 caucus letter to Thune (source ↗ https://www.booker.senate.gov/news/press/booker-joins-democratic-colleagues-in-calling-on-leader-thune-to-quickly-bring-the-epstein-files-transparency-act-to-floor-for-vote). In July 2026 he confronted Todd Blanche at his Attorney General hearing ("So this isn't a confirmation hearing. This is more of a performance review.") (source ↗ https://www.booker.senate.gov/news/press/at-confirmation-hearing-booker-presses-blanche-on-independence-epstein-survivors-and-dojs-targeting-of-the-presidents-political-opponents), posted that Blanche claimed the law barred him from meeting victims, "Yet he had no problem sitting down with Ghislaine Maxwell" (source ↗ https://x.com/SenBooker/status/2077486168984203637), and introduced the REDACT Act with Rep. Jayapal to let survivors sue DOJ over the unredacted personal data it published: "No survivor should have to relive unimaginable trauma because the federal government failed to protect their privacy." (source ↗ https://www.booker.senate.gov/news/press/booker-and-jayapal-introduce-redact-act-to-protect-epstein-survivors-and-strengthen-accountability-for-doj-privacy-violations) He led a survivor forum ahead of the Blanche vote and posted, "This is not the people's Department of Justice. It has become an instrument of one man's will." (source ↗ https://x.com/SenBooker/status/2080077715747606810) Four entries are dead-link duplicates of live ones.

<a id="ben-ray-luj-n"></a>

### Ben Ray Luján (Democrat-NM)
**Position:** Led the push · **Sept. 10, 2025 vote:** opposed blocking · **Distinct actions:** 10 (11 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Ben%20Ray%20Luj%C3%A1n)

Luján co-led the Senate bill with Merkley from its July 2025 introduction, calling it a response to a broken promise: "The Trump administration has failed to deliver on this promise and as a result has lost the trust of the American people." (source ↗ https://www.lujan.senate.gov/newsroom/press-releases/lujan-merkley-demand-public-release-of-epstein-files/) The day before the September vote he posted, "Disgusting. Pass the Epstein Files Transparency Act from @SenJeffMerkley and me now." (source ↗ https://x.com/senatorlujan/status/1965509644991799309) On November 18 he and Heinrich wrote Thune demanding a vote and then marked passage: "It should never have required an act of Congress to do what basic decency and accountability demanded." (source ↗ https://www.lujan.senate.gov/newsroom/press-releases/lujan-heinrich-applaud-senate-passage-of-epstein-files-transparency-act/) When DOJ missed the deadline he said the failure "further implicates this administration in a cover-up" (source ↗ https://www.lujan.senate.gov/newsroom/press-releases/lujan-statement-on-trump-administrations-failure-to-release-all-epstein-files/), and after the January dump posted, "If this is what they are willing to show the public, what they are breaking the law to hide must be far worse." (source ↗ https://x.com/SenatorLujan/status/2018434673685467389) In March 2026 he joined Merkley, Murkowski and Durbin asking GAO to audit DOJ's redactions (source ↗ https://sourcenm.com/2026/03/11/nm-u-s-sen-lujan-signs-letter-urging-audit-of-epstein-file-redactions/), then viewed the unredacted files on New Mexico's Zorro Ranch: "It has been 113 days since the Senate passed my Epstein Files Transparency Act. President Trump is breaking the law every day by not releasing all the files" (source ↗ https://www.lujan.senate.gov/newsroom/press-releases/lujan-statement-on-viewing-unredacted-epstein-files-at-doj/), telling MS NOW, "There's a question that I have about who, who was covering up for whom, and who got the benefit here." (source ↗ https://www.lujan.senate.gov/newsroom/press-releases/icymi-lujan-on-msnow-highlighting-push-to-deliver-justice-for-epstein-survivors-and-investigate-horrific-crimes-at-zorro-ranch/) In July 2026 he introduced the Epstein Files Transparency Act II with Merkley, Massie, Khanna and Leger Fernández, giving state attorneys general and victims standing to sue DOJ (source ↗ https://www.lujan.senate.gov/newsroom/press-releases/lujan-merkley-massie-khanna-leger-fernandez-introduce-the-epstein-files-transparency-act-ii/), after DOJ refused New Mexico's attorney general the Zorro Ranch records (source ↗ https://x.com/SenatorLujan/status/2078128123548209372).

<a id="martin-heinrich"></a>

### Martin Heinrich (Democrat-NM)
**Position:** Took action · **Sept. 10, 2025 vote:** opposed blocking · **Distinct actions:** 4 (8 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Martin%20Heinrich)

He cosponsored the Senate bill, voted to keep the amendment alive, and on November 18 wrote Thune with Luján before marking passage: "As we've already seen from Epstein's emails, Trump's name is written all over them, and he's spent months trying to keep the public in the dark." (source ↗ https://www.heinrich.senate.gov/newsroom/press-releases/heinrich-lujan-applaud-senate-passage-of-epstein-files-transparency-act) When Blanche said DOJ would release only part of the files by the deadline: "The law could not have been clearer: President Trump's Department of Justice had 30 days to release ALL the files." and "It's a deliberate attempt to bury the truth and protect the pedophiles who exploited and abused young girls." (source ↗ https://www.heinrich.senate.gov/newsroom/press-releases/heinrich-issues-statement-on-trump-administration-breaking-the-law-by-refusing-to-release-complete-epstein-files) His most concrete act came July 15, 2026, at Jay Clayton's DNI confirmation hearing, where he pressed the SDNY U.S. Attorney to give New Mexico's attorney general the unredacted Zorro Ranch records (Clayton said his office was "seeking dialog with the attorney general") and to own the January release of victims' personal data: "Who has a sign on their desk in this case?" Clayton: "For the Southern District documents, it was me." (source ↗ https://www.heinrich.senate.gov/newsroom/press-releases/heinrich-presses-jay-clayton-on-doj-obstruction-of-new-mexico-investigation-into-jeffrey-epsteins-crimes-at-zorro-ranch) Four entries are coverage or video of that hearing; the MS NOW item is a page shell.

### Catherine Cortez Masto (Democrat-NV)
**Position:** Took action · **Sept. 10, 2025 vote:** opposed blocking · **Distinct actions:** 3 (4 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Catherine%20Cortez%20Masto)

She voted to keep the amendment alive and signed the November caucus letter. In a Nevada Independent interview she criticized Pam Bondi's handling of the files (video only; no text), and a Google News item reports she said DOJ should be held in contempt if it missed the deadline (summary; source text not retrieved). On December 19 she posted: "The law we passed is clear: release *all* of the Epstein Files by Dec. 19 - today. The Trump Admin. has had plenty of time to release the files on their own" (source ↗ https://www.facebook.com/SenatorCortezMasto/posts/1452265252924285/); the caption is truncated in the fetch, and the other Facebook entry is the same post.

<a id="jacky-rosen"></a>

### Jacky Rosen (Democrat-NV)
**Position:** Led the push · **Sept. 10, 2025 vote:** opposed blocking · **Distinct actions:** 8 (10 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Jacky%20Rosen)

Rosen wrote Thune on November 14, 2025, before the House had even voted, asking him to schedule the bill: "The recently revealed Epstein correspondence demonstrates the clear connection between the President and Mr. Epstein's nefarious and profoundly disturbing activities." (source ↗ https://www.rosen.senate.gov/2025/11/14/rosen-urges-senate-majority-leader-to-hold-vote-on-bipartisan-bill-to-release-epstein-files/) After the February 2026 release she became the first Commerce Committee member to demand Howard Lutnick resign ("I believe that to protect the trust of the American people and to uphold the honor of his office, Howard Lutnick must resign") and introduced a resolution opposing any clemency for Ghislaine Maxwell (source ↗ https://www.rosen.senate.gov/2026/02/13/icymi-following-latest-release-of-epstein-files-rosen-pushes-for-accountability/). She posted that "Dozens of Epstein files are missing, including ones containing serious allegations by a survivor against Donald Trump" (source ↗ https://x.com/SenJackyRosen/status/2027067798921314652), and told MS NOW of Maxwell, "as far as I'm concerned, she can rot in hell." (source ↗ https://www.rosen.senate.gov/2026/02/27/icymi-rosen-on-ms-now-everyone-involved-in-the-epstein-files-should-be-held-accountable/) In March she wrote Lutnick demanding his 2005 to 2019 flight logs (source ↗ https://www.rosen.senate.gov/2026/03/19/after-commerce-secretary-misled-public-about-relationship-with-epstein-rosen-calls-on-lutnick-to-release-his-flight-logs/), and when Bondi left she posted, "She won't be missed." (source ↗ https://x.com/SenJackyRosen/status/2039788836293578914) On July 29, 2026, with Blanche's confirmation vote pending, she took the Maxwell resolution to the floor and passed it by unanimous consent: "nobody dared to come and object to my amendment." (source ↗ https://www.rosen.senate.gov/2026/07/30/icymi-rosen-passes-resolution-opposing-ghislaine-maxwell-pardon/) On the floor she called Maxwell's transfer to a minimum-security prison days after Blanche interviewed her a "Magic act" and said there should be no pardon, "Not now, not ever." (source ↗ https://www.usatoday.com/story/news/politics/2026/07/30/us-senate-passes-nevada-rosens-resolution-on-ghislaine-maxwell-pardon/91111403007/)

<a id="charles-e-schumer"></a>

### Charles E. Schumer (Democrat-NY)
**Position:** Led the push · **Sept. 10, 2025 vote:** opposed blocking · **Distinct actions:** 11 (13 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Charles%20E.%20Schumer)

The September 10 vote was on Schumer's own amendment: he attached the Merkley-Luján bill to the defense authorization and 51 Republicans tabled it. His caucus release traces earlier pushes, a July "Rule of Five" demand with Peters that DOJ turn over the files to the Homeland Security Committee and an August follow-up, both refused (source ↗ https://www.democrats.senate.gov/newsroom/press-releases/after-months-of-gop-stonewalling-the-release-of-the-files-to-protect-president-trump-leader-schumer-leads-entire-senate-democratic-caucus-in-demanding-senate-immediately-pass-the-epstein-files-transparency-act). On November 18 he led all 47 Democrats in a letter to Thune and warned, "If Leader Thune tries to bury the bill, I'll stop him" (source ↗ https://thehill.com/homenews/senate/5611187-schumer-epstein-files-bill/); that evening he obtained unanimous consent to deem the House bill passed on arrival. When DOJ missed December 19 he said "Failing to do so is breaking the law" (source ↗ https://thehill.com/homenews/senate/5657464-schumer-democrats-epstein-files/) and three days later introduced a resolution directing the Senate to sue: "Instead of transparency, the Trump administration released a tiny fraction of the files and blacked out massive portions of what little they provided." "This is a blatant cover-up." (source ↗ https://www.democrats.senate.gov/newsroom/press-releases/leader-schumer-introduces-legislation-to-require-senate-to-initiate-legal-action-against-trump-over-epstein-files) Blanche answered on Meet the Press that "all of a sudden, out of the blue, Senator Schumer suddenly cares about the Epstein files" (source ↗ https://fortune.com/2025/12/22/chuck-schumer-urges-action-after-blatant-cover-up-of-epstein-files-redaction-inaction/). Barrasso blocked the lawsuit resolution on the floor in February 2026. After DOJ declared its release complete, Schumer counted "less than 2.7 million pages" and said, "AG Bondi thinks she can dupe the American people. But we see right through her lies." (source ↗ https://www.democrats.senate.gov/newsroom/press-releases/leader-schumer-statement-on-the-department-of-justices-slow-incomplete-and-unlawful-release-of-the-epstein-files) In late February he launched a Senate Democratic "Epstein Working Group" to review the unredacted files and demanded DOJ preserve records: "Let me be blunt. There is a massive coverup going on in the Justice Department to protect Donald Trump and people associated with Jeffrey Epstein." (source ↗ https://www.democrats.senate.gov/newsroom/press-releases/transcript-leader-schumer-and-senate-democrats-launch-oversight-effort-on-the-trump-administrations-mishandling-of-the-epstein-files) He also introduced a bill named for a late Epstein survivor to end the federal civil statute of limitations for abuse claims (source ↗ https://www.schumer.senate.gov/newsroom/press-releases/schumer-standing-with-rochester-survivors-and-advocates-announces-new-legislation_in-honor-of-epstein-victims--to-end-federal-statute-of-limitations-barrier-that-lets-abusers-escape-and-denies-survivors-justice), and kept posting: "Pam Bondi does not have the right to pick and choose which Epstein files to release to the public." (source ↗ https://x.com/SenSchumer/status/2027098094798606574)
