**Position:** Took action · **Sept. 10, 2025 vote:** opposed blocking · **Distinct actions:** 2 (2 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Kirsten%20E.%20Gillibrand)

Her first entry is misdated in the database as 2025; it is the December 2018 letter she, Murray and Kaine led with 13 other Democrats asking the DOJ Inspector General to investigate Alexander Acosta's 2007 non-prosecution agreement after the Miami Herald's reporting: "That is why I am calling on the DOJ Inspector General to do a deep dive investigation to get to the bottom of the decision-making process for this case where so many young girls lives were destroyed and it was swept under the rug." (source ↗ https://www.gillibrand.senate.gov/news/press/release/following-scathing-new-report-gillibrand-murray-kaine-and-senate-democrats-call-on-department-of-justice-inspector-general-to-complete-full-investigation-of-epstein-case-process/) In 2025 she voted to keep the amendment alive and signed the November 18 caucus letter to Thune (source ↗ https://www.gillibrand.senate.gov/news/press/release/gillibrand-democratic-colleagues-call-on-leader-thune-to-quickly-bring-the-epstein-files-transparency-act-to-floor-for-vote/). Nothing in her own voice since.

<a id="bernie-moreno"></a>

### Bernie Moreno (Republican-OH)
**Position:** Deflected (blamed others or minimized) · **Sept. 10, 2025 vote:** blocked release · **Distinct actions:** 3 (6 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Bernie%20Moreno)

On CNN on July 24, 2025 Moreno blamed the outcry on the press: "No matter how much is disclosed at this point, there's going to be a small segment of the population fueled primarily by the media and the Democrats that are never going to be satisfied with what's out there," and tried to change the subject ("You know what we're not talking about, the autopen scandal"), calling the Wall Street Journal's birthday-letter report "a fake story" (source ↗ https://newrepublic.com/post/198445/maga-senator-bernie-moreno-fact-check-donald-trump-epstein; source ↗ https://www.independent.co.uk/news/world/americas/us-politics/kaitlan-collins-bernie-moreno-epstein-b2791607.html). A Columbus 10TV interview and an August newspaper interview returned no text. He voted to table the amendment in September and supported the bill by unanimous consent. The New Republic reported, citing NOTUS, that Les Wexner gave him $3,500 in June 2025, a month before the CNN appearance; Wexner was named as a co-conspirator in a 2019 FBI email unredacted in February 2026 and has never been charged (source ↗ https://newrepublic.com/post/206564/maga-senator-donation-epstein-conspirator-vote-block-files). TiffinOhio reported he had not announced plans to donate it.

<a id="jon-husted"></a>

### Jon Husted (Republican-OH)
**Position:** Voted to block release · **Sept. 10, 2025 vote:** blocked release · **Distinct actions:** 0 in his own words (8 third-party database entries) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Jon%20Husted)

Nothing in Husted's record is in his own words; every entry is reporting or a party attack, so what follows is attributed. On September 10, 2025 he voted to table the amendment that would have forced release. TiffinOhio.net reported, from FEC and Ohio records, that he had taken $116,892 from Les Wexner between 2001 and 2025, including $3,500 on July 3, 2025, and that the day before the vote he told a Bulwark reporter asking about Trump and Epstein, "I've got four minutes before I have to be in the chair [to preside over the Senate]. So I gotta run." (source ↗ https://tiffinohio.net/posts/husted-took-donations-from-epstein-co-conspirator-les-wexner-then-voted-to-block-file-release/) Wexner was named a co-conspirator in a 2019 FBI email unredacted in February 2026; he has never been charged and told Congress he never lobbied Husted. The same outlet quoted him on Fox Business on November 19, the day the bill passed by unanimous consent: "The more we learn about the transparency of the Epstein files, the more it shows that Democrats are the ones that had the strong relationships with them." (source ↗ https://tiffinohio.net/posts/jon-husted-to-donate-wexner-cash-after-vote-blocking-epstein-file-release/) The Ohio Democratic Party said he "again refused to support" release on NewsNation (source ↗ https://ohiodems.org/jon-husted-once-again-refuses-to-support-releasing-epstein-files/). His campaign gave $34,300 of the Wexner money to an anti-trafficking charity and, the Salem News reported, will not give the remaining $45,600 (source ↗ https://www.salemnews.net/news/local-news/2026/08/brown-husted-campaigns-discuss-epstein-related-funds-from-wexner/). His 2026 race against Sherrod Brown is a duel of Epstein-donor ads examined by FactCheck.org (source ↗ https://www.factcheck.org/2026/06/ohio-senate-candidates-spar-over-donations-tied-loosely-or-not-to-epstein/) and PolitiFact (source ↗ https://www.politifact.com/article/2026/jun/08/epstein-ohio-senate-husted-brown-donations/); his own ad says he "voted to release the Epstein files," which rests on the unanimous consent that required no affirmative act.

### James Lankford (Republican-OK)
**Position:** Deflected (blamed others or minimized) · **Sept. 10, 2025 vote:** blocked release · **Distinct actions:** 5 (5 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=James%20Lankford)

Lankford said release the files and, in the same breath, that the idea Trump was being protected was laughable. In a July 30, 2025 Facebook Live: "I have some folks on the left that are saying Trump is secretly hiding all this information," and, "That makes me laugh, honestly because if there was something about President Trump in there, do you think Joe Biden and Kamala Harris would not have released it out in a heartbeat during the campaign." (source ↗ https://www.kswo.com/2025/08/01/sen-lankford-addresses-rural-healthcare-education-epstein-files-more-live-qa/) To News 9 the next day: "Let's just get all the information out, but let's also tamp down on the conspiracy theories that are out there, and make sure we're actually tracking the facts." (source ↗ https://www.news9.com/story/688b60c0ade31892847fd27a/sen-lankford-talks-epstein-files-military-funding-legacy-of-former-oklahoma-gov-george-nigh) A GOP clip has him saying "people aren't going to be satisfied ever" (video; no further text). He voted to table the amendment in September and supported the bill by unanimous consent. A November Tulsa World interview is unreadable in the fetch (encoded text), and a NewsOn6 piece paraphrases him warning that names in the files do not mean guilt (source ↗ https://m.youtube.com/watch?v=IrShjRZThXc). Two entries are dead duplicates.

<a id="jeff-merkley"></a>

### Jeff Merkley (Democrat-OR)
**Position:** Led the push · **Sept. 10, 2025 vote:** opposed blocking · **Distinct actions:** 13 (12 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Jeff%20Merkley)

Merkley was the lead Senate sponsor of the Epstein Files Transparency Act, introduced with Luján and 24 cosponsors in July 2025, and the record shows him using every procedural tool he had. When the Senate tabled Schumer's identical amendment on September 10 he asked, "What do Senate Republicans have to hide?" and said Thune and his conference "all bent the knee to Donald Trump and opposed my bill to release the Epstein Files." (source ↗ https://www.merkley.senate.gov/senate-republicans-block-merkleys-epstein-files-transparency-act-justice-for-epsteins-vic) He then made repeated unanimous-consent attempts with Wyden, Van Hollen, Durbin and Blumenthal, each blocked (source ↗ https://www.merkley.senate.gov/merkley-blasts-senate-republicans-for-blocking-his-bill-to-release-the-epstein-files/). On November 18 he marked passage: "We say no more to the rich and powerful using their influence, money, and connections to cover up the abuse of young girls." (source ↗ https://www.merkley.senate.gov/merkley-lujan-praise-senate-passage-of-epstein-files-transparency-act-to-restore-publics-) In early December he, Murkowski, Luján, Massie and Khanna demanded a DOJ briefing by December 5 on the new investigations Bondi had announced (source ↗ https://www.merkley.senate.gov/merkley-murkowski-lujan-massie-khanna-demand-doj-briefing-on-release-of-the-epstein-files/); when it did not come, he and Luján blocked more than 90 civilian nominations from carrying over to the new session, and on the December 19 deadline he said "they have chosen to illegally disregard the law I led the fight in the Senate to pass." (source ↗ https://www.merkley.senate.gov/merkley-trump-administration-breaking-the-law-by-failing-to-comply-with-epstein-files-tra) He spoke on the floor about the redactions (source ↗ https://www.c-span.org/program/us-senate/sen-jeff-merkley-on-release-of-epstein-files/673007) and held a December 16 press conference his office titled "We smell a rat." With Blumenthal and Murkowski he led the letter asking the DOJ Inspector General to audit compliance, arguing "a neutral assessment of its compliance with the statutory disclosure requirements is essential" (source ↗ https://www.merkley.senate.gov/blumenthal-murkowski-merkley-lead-call-for-inspector-general-audit-following-dojs-failure-to-release-the-full-epstein-files-as-demanded-by-survivors-required-by-law/); the IG opened that audit in April 2026, and the same month GAO accepted his separate request to investigate DOJ's redaction practices: "GAO accepts your request as work that is within the scope of its authority." (source ↗ https://www.merkley.senate.gov/at-merkleys-request-independent-government-watchdog-launches-investigation-into-trump-dojs-handling-of-the-epstein-files/) In July 2026 he and Luján introduced the Epstein Files Transparency Act II to let victims, state attorneys general and members of Congress sue DOJ: "we need the Epstein Files Transparency Act II to strengthen the original law we wrote, crack down on the DOJ's illegal non-compliance, and stand with survivors" (source ↗ https://www.koin.com/news/politics/oregon-senator-merkley-second-epstein-files-act/). Five entries are duplicate URLs of the same releases.

<a id="ron-wyden"></a>

### Ron Wyden (Democrat-OR)
**Position:** Led the push · **Sept. 10, 2025 vote:** opposed blocking · **Distinct actions:** 7 (10 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Ron%20Wyden)

Wyden ran his own money investigation from the Finance Committee, separate from the files fight, and it produced the record's hardest numbers. On September 2, 2025 he wrote Treasury Secretary Bessent renewing a demand for Treasury's Epstein suspicious-activity reports, naming dozens of individuals and banks: "it is preposterous and unacceptable that only Epstein himself and Ghislaine Maxwell have faced prosecution." (source ↗ https://www.finance.senate.gov/ranking-members-news/as-bessent-withholds-epstein-files-wyden-expands-investigation-and-demands-financial-records) He voted to keep the amendment alive and joined Merkley's floor attempts. On November 20 he released a staff report on JPMorgan Chase, posting, "My staff have seen a paper trail with their own eyes. Banks that helped enable him should be investigated." (source ↗ https://x.com/ronwyden/status/1991527281286869263) On March 3, 2026 Republicans blocked his Produce Epstein Treasury Records Act by unanimous consent; on the floor he said his investigators had seen records of "4,725 wire transfers adding up to more than $1 billion in and out of his JPMorgan bank accounts in the decade after 2003" and that "Bessent is lying" about Treasury's role (source ↗ https://www.finance.senate.gov/ranking-members-news/senate-republican-blocks-wyden-bill-mandating-treasury-hand-over-epstein-bank-records). Weeks later he wrote Blanche accusing him of stopping the DEA from producing an unredacted 2015 drug-trafficking memo on Epstein ("Your alleged interference in this matter is highly disturbing"); Blanche replied that "A sitting U.S. Senator has completely fabricated a story for clicks" (source ↗ https://www.cbsnews.com/news/justice-department-blocked-release-of-secret-epstein-drug-probe-file-u-s-senator-says/). On August 4, 2026 he published "Looking the Other Way," naming bank officials he says delayed reporting Epstein's transfers: "This report is a ready-made roadmap for prosecutors, investigators and members of Congress to finally start holding the Epstein class accountable." (source ↗ https://www.finance.senate.gov/ranking-members-news/wyden-releases-new-report-on-failure-of-wall-street-banks-to-blow-the-whistle-on-jeffrey-epsteins-sex-trafficking-and-money-laundering-schemes) The OregonLive item is dead (403).

### David McCormick (Republican-PA)
**Position:** Voted to block release · **Sept. 10, 2025 vote:** blocked release · **Distinct actions:** 1 (1 entry in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=David%20McCormick)

His only statement is a July 30, 2025 WJAC interview in which, the station reported, he said transparency was necessary but "there are much more important issues to focus on at this time," and declined to say whether he would back a bill compelling release because he "could not comment specifically on legislation that does not exist, at least not yet" (source ↗ https://wjactv.com/news/connect-to-congress/connect-to-congress-senator-dave-mccormick-comments-public-calls-release-epstein-files-fema-cuts-budget-pennsylvania-legislation-ai-tech-summit-investments-; station paraphrase, not a direct quote). When the legislation did exist, on September 10, he voted to table it. He supported the bill by unanimous consent in November without a statement. The other entry is a dead duplicate.

### John Fetterman (Democrat-PA)
**Position:** Took action · **Sept. 10, 2025 vote:** opposed blocking · **Distinct actions:** 2 (5 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=John%20Fetterman)

He voted to keep the amendment alive in September. On November 12, 2025, the day House Democrats released Epstein's emails about Trump, he told CNN's Dana Bash, "Yeah, it's absolutely troubling, you know, to see that. And I think we probably need more," said he would press Thune for a vote, and added, "everything should come out" (source ↗ https://thehill.com/homenews/senate/5602489-fetterman-troubled-epstein-trump/; source ↗ https://www.mediaite.com/media/tv/everything-should-come-out-john-fetterman-declares-trump-epstein-bombshell-troubling/). MSN reported he also faulted Democrats for not releasing the files under Biden (summary; no text retrieved, and one copy is dead). He signed the November 18 caucus letter to Thune. Nothing after passage.

<a id="jack-reed"></a>

### Jack Reed (Democrat-RI)
**Position:** Led the push · **Sept. 10, 2025 vote:** opposed blocking · **Distinct actions:** 9 (11 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Jack%20Reed)

Reed's focus was Ghislaine Maxwell's treatment. He cosponsored the Senate bill on July 31, 2025, the week Blanche interviewed Maxwell ("Instead of lifting a finger to release the Epstein files, President Trump is doing everything he can to prevent them from coming out") (source ↗ https://www.reed.senate.gov/news/releases/reed-presses-for-release-of-epstein-files). On August 8 he wrote the Bureau of Prisons director demanding the justification for her transfer to a minimum-security camp days after that interview: "These circumstances raise serious questions of whether Ms. Maxwell is being given special treatment in exchange for providing favorable information." (source ↗ https://www.reed.senate.gov/news/releases/reed-demands-answers-on-special-treatment-of-convicted-sex-offender-ghislaine-maxwell) He voted to keep the amendment alive and on November 18 called passage "a positive step toward justice. But it's still an uphill climb," predicting the new Clinton investigation would be used as an "active investigation" excuse to withhold (source ↗ https://www.reed.senate.gov/news/releases/us-senate-passes-bill-to-compel-the-release-of-epstein-files). In December he asked the DOJ Inspector General to probe the Maxwell treatment (link dead as of Sep 2026; text not preserved ↗ https://www.reed.senate.gov/news/releases/reed-asks-doj-watchdog-to-probe-trump-admins-favorable-treatment-for-convicted-sex-offender-ghislaine-maxwel), and in February 2026 urged Republicans to join a lawsuit to compel release (link dead ↗ https://www.reed.senate.gov/news/releases/as-trump-admin-continues-to-unlawfully-hide-full-epstein-files-reed-urges-republicans-to-join-democrats-in-t; an aggregator summary survives at https://www.quiverquant.com/news/Press+Release:+Jack+Reed+Calls+for+Legal+Action+Against+Trump+Administration+Over+Epstein+Files+Transparency). He posted, "If President Trump has nothing to hide in the Epstein Files, why is his Admin apparently still withholding critical documents that mention his name?" (source ↗ https://x.com/SenJackReed/status/2026370438066999772) and, ahead of the Blanche vote, that "DOJ under Todd Blanche is still NOT complying with the Epstein Files Law." (source ↗ https://www.facebook.com/SenJackReed/posts/its-important-to-note-that-doj-under-todd-blanche-is-still-not-complying-with-th/1581963226648233/) His August 2026 pre-vote statement is a dead link. Six of fifteen entries are dead or duplicate.

<a id="sheldon-whitehouse"></a>

### Sheldon Whitehouse (Democrat-RI)
**Position:** Led the push · **Sept. 10, 2025 vote:** opposed blocking · **Distinct actions:** 8 (15 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Sheldon%20Whitehouse)

Whitehouse turned oversight letters and the floor into a running investigation. In August 2025 he demanded documents on Maxwell's prison transfer (Google News item; no text). He voted to keep the amendment alive and signed the caucus letter. In December he and Durbin wrote White House chief of staff Susie Wiles after she told Vanity Fair she had read "the Epstein file" and that Trump "is in the file": "Please be kind enough to explain when and where and under what authority you gained access to this material." (source ↗ https://www.whitehouse.senate.gov/news/release/whitehouse-and-durbin-ask-trumps-chief-of-staff-how-she-received-access-to-epstein-) He signed the Blumenthal-Murkowski-Merkley IG audit request and told MS NOW the inspector general could determine "whether this was incompetence, whether this was deliberate, whether this was misbehavior" (source ↗ https://www.ms.now/news/epstein-files-subpoena-congress-les-wexner-trump). On March 5, 2026 he gave a 48-minute floor speech on Trump, Epstein and Russia, saying "We also know that there is a coverup afoot at the Department of Justice. The MAGA Department of Justice is trying to shield Trump from something in the Epstein files." (source ↗ https://www.whitehouse.senate.gov/news/release/whitehouse-speech-on-trump-epstein-russia-triangle-goes-viral/) When DOJ published three previously missing FBI interview records naming Trump, he wrote Bondi and Patel demanding preservation of all records on the withholding decision, citing the Federal Records Act (source ↗ https://www.whitehouse.senate.gov/news/release/as-doj-continues-to-withhold-epstein-files-about-accusations-against-trump-whitehouse-demands-that-doj-follow-the-law-and-preserve-all-related-documents/). He opposed Blanche for Attorney General on the files: "Todd Blanche participated in the cover up, continues to participate in the cover up, and is responsible for a massive screw up of the release of the documents," adding that DOJ never examined Treasury's suspicious-activity reports (source ↗ https://www.c-span.org/clip/senate-committee/sen-whitehouse-points-to-epstein-files-as-reason-for-opposition-of-ag-nominee-blanche/5204004). Nine of seventeen entries are duplicates, dead Google News links or aggregator mirrors.

### Tim Scott (Republican-SC)
**Position:** Deflected (blamed others or minimized) · **Sept. 10, 2025 vote:** blocked release · **Distinct actions:** 1 (1 entry in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Tim%20Scott)

His one statement is a July 18, 2025 media call on the tax bill, reported by the Post and Courier: he said Trump and Bondi had kept "plenty of light on the scene," that "The more information we have, the better," then blamed the prior administration and the press, saying Biden had the files for years and "frankly, did nothing with them because there was nothing to do with them," and, "We're talking more about a reference from a tabloid than we are the future of our nation." (source ↗ https://www.postandcourier.com/politics/sc-tim-scott-graham-norman-epstein-files/article_ea867303-f706-4e48-86ba-d1f7aa1c1cc9.html) He voted to table the amendment in September and supported the bill by unanimous consent without a statement.

<a id="john-thune"></a>

### John Thune (Republican-SD)
**Position:** Voted to block release · **Sept. 10, 2025 vote:** blocked release · **Distinct actions:** 6 (7 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=John%20Thune)

As Majority Leader, Thune spent July through November keeping the files off the Senate floor, then let the bill through when the House vote made that untenable. On July 14, 2025: "I'll leave that up to DOJ and to the FBI. I think that's in their purview." Asked July 17 whether any Senate Republicans wanted to force disclosure: "I'm not hearing it." (source ↗ https://thehill.com/homenews/senate/5406289-senate-thune-epstein-files/) On Fox News he said "I'm always a believer in transparency" and "I think more is always better," while suggesting "maybe there were unrealistic expectations" (source ↗ https://nypost.com/2025/07/17/us-news/senate-majority-leader-john-thune-supports-full-disclosure-of-epstein-files/). He told Politico he had discussed Epstein with Trump "in passing" (source ↗ https://www.politico.com/live-updates/2025/07/23/congress/thune-interview-on-epstein-johnson-00471670). On September 10 he voted to table Schumer's amendment, which Schumer got to the floor only because Thune had not filed a motion blocking amendments; that month he argued "There's a pile of records already out there on that." (source ↗ https://thehill.com/homenews/senate/5609780-thune-senate-epstein-files-vote/) On November 18, after the House voted 427-1, he declined Speaker Johnson's request to amend the bill, "When a bill comes out of the House 427-1 and the president has said he's going to sign it, I'm not sure that amending it is in the cards," and agreed to deem it passed on arrival (source ↗ https://thehill.com/homenews/senate/5611756-senate-epstein-files-bill/). He has said nothing in the record since DOJ missed the deadline. The MSN and Politico November items returned no text.

Two YouTube entries are the same KOTA interview.

<a id="bill-hagerty"></a>

### Bill Hagerty (Republican-TN)
**Position:** Deflected (blamed others or minimized) · **Sept. 10, 2025 vote:** blocked release · **Distinct actions:** 1 (1 entry in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Bill%20Hagerty)

He voted to table the amendment in September. His one statement, on November 18 after the House vote, claimed the issue as his own and blamed Democrats: "From day one, I have called for full transparency on the investigation into the criminal actions of Jeffrey Epstein. Democrats held power in Washington for four years during the Biden administration, having the full capacity to release the files, yet they are now focused on this issue out of political convenience," closing, "President Trump supports the release of these files, as do I." (source ↗ https://www.chattanoogan.com/2025/11/18/511450/Hagarty-Praises-House-Vote-Calling-For.aspx) The record contains no earlier call from him. The second entry is a duplicate URL.

<a id="marsha-blackburn"></a>

### Marsha Blackburn (Republican-TN)
**Position:** Took action (then voted to block release) · **Sept. 10, 2025 vote:** blocked release · **Distinct actions:** 10 (11 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Marsha%20Blackburn)

Blackburn's record on Epstein is older than almost anyone's in the Senate, and it turns on the September vote. In November 2023 she tried to subpoena Epstein's flight logs through the Judiciary Committee and Chairman Durbin blocked it; she said Democrats "don't want to have a conversation about the estate of Jeffrey Epstein to find out the names of every person who participated in Jeffrey Epstein's human trafficking ring." (source ↗ https://www.blackburn.senate.gov/2023/11/news/breaking-judiciary-chair-durbin-blocks-senator-blackburn-s-request-to-subpoena-sotomayor-staff-epstein-flight-logs) In January 2024, after a judge unsealed the Maxwell civil documents, she wrote Durbin holding him to a promised bipartisan subpoena of the estate and the FBI (source ↗ https://www.blackburn.senate.gov/2024/1/news/blackburn-urges-durbin-to-follow-through-on-commitment-to-obtain-epstein-records) and pledged, "I will not stop fighting for complete and total transparency until all of the records are released" (source ↗ https://www.blackburn.senate.gov/2024/1/news/blackburn-statement-on-federal-judge-unsealing-epstein-court-documents). In February 2025 she wrote FBI Director Patel and the IRS seeking the unredacted records (Patel letter dead; described by the Nashville Banner). After the July 2025 DOJ memo she said she was "hopeful we can still uncover this information from the financial records" (source ↗ https://www.wate.com/news/tennessee/blackburn-responds-as-doj-shuts-down-epstein-file-request/). Then on September 10 she voted to table Schumer's amendment, her office calling it "nothing more than a desperate political stunt" that threatened troop pay (source ↗ https://www.wkrn.com/news/tennessee-politics/blackburn-calls-epstein-amendment-political-stunt-votes-block/). She supported the bill by unanimous consent, welcomed the January release as "one step closer" (source ↗ https://x.com/MarshaBlackburn/status/2017359352026337402), and at Blanche's July 2026 hearing attacked Durbin: "So, the Democrats at that point didn't want to know what was in the Epstein files until they lost the majority, and then they were hell bent to get that." (source ↗ https://www.blackburn.senate.gov/2026/7/video-blackburn-scorches-durbin-for-grandstanding-on-epstein-files-after-he-previously-blocked-subpoena-requests) Wyden's August 2026 report alleged her office declined his 2024 and 2025 requests to help subpoena Epstein's bank records and ignored a request to cosponsor his Treasury records bill; her spokesperson called that "laughable" and cited the 2023 subpoena attempts (source ↗ https://nashvillebanner.com/2026/08/13/marsha-blackburn-jeffrey-epstein-ron-wyden-report/).

### John Cornyn (Republican-TX)
**Position:** Deflected (blamed others or minimized) · **Sept. 10, 2025 vote:** blocked release · **Distinct actions:** 3 (5 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=John%20Cornyn)

Nothing in Cornyn's record is in his own release; all entries are reporting. At a July 2025 Judiciary hearing he was quoted: "To our colleagues, the intrigue and salacious details of the Epstein case are a great distraction from the issues that make a meaningful difference in the lives of ordinary Americans." (source ↗ https://www.thebarbedwire.com/john-cornyn-ted-cruz-vote-epstein-files/) He voted to table the amendment on September 10, which the San Antonio Current reported as voting "against releasing Epstein files" (source ↗ https://www.sacurrent.com/news/texas-news/u-s-senators-ted-cruz-and-john-cornyn-of-texas-voted-against-releasing-epstein-files/), and supported the bill by unanimous consent. In 2026 he voted in committee to advance Todd Blanche for Attorney General and, Raw Story reported, dismissed survivors' concerns afterward (summary; only a page description retrieved), clashing with Booker in the hearing (video; no text). A Google News item quoting a survivor calling his vote "really depressing" is dead. His primary opponents made the files an issue; he did not.

<a id="ted-cruz"></a>

### Ted Cruz (Republican-TX)
**Position:** Voted to block release · **Sept. 10, 2025 vote:** blocked release · **Distinct actions:** 5 (8 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Ted%20Cruz)

Cruz called for release repeatedly and then voted to block it. In early 2025 he told the Bulwark Trump should "absolutely" release the files: "I have long called for the administration to release the Jeffrey Epstein files, and also the P. Diddy files. We deserve to know who is implicated in abusing children." (source ↗ https://www.thebulwark.com/p/gop-sens-trump-needs-to-reveal-epstein) On February 27 he told Hugh Hewitt, "I've been calling for the Epstein files to be made public for a long time, and I'm glad that Pam Bondi is following through on that." (source ↗ https://hughhewitt.com/senator-ted-cruz-r-tx-on-landman-the-epstein-files-the-administrative-state-and-more) He told Piers Morgan the public "has every right to know" (video; no further text). At a September 9 Judiciary hearing, answering Booker's call for bipartisanship, he said "How about we all come together and say 'Let's stop attacking pedophiles.'" (source ↗ https://www.sacurrent.com/news/texas-news/ted-cruz-says-stop-attacking-pedophiles-in-gaffe-during-senate-hearing/) The next day he voted to table Schumer's amendment; no explanation appears in the record. Later that month he attacked Rep. Greene, who had signed the House discharge petition, over a post about threats to her: "Why do crazy people keep thinking 'the Jews' are trying to kill them?" (source ↗ https://www.huffpost.com/entry/ted-cruz-marjorie-taylor-greene-jeffrey-epstein-files_n_68daa849e4b0a3d9991e5b6b) He supported the bill by unanimous consent in November without a statement.

<a id="tim-kaine"></a>

### Tim Kaine (Democrat-VA)
**Position:** Took action · **Sept. 10, 2025 vote:** opposed blocking · **Distinct actions:** 9 (11 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Tim%20Kaine)

Kaine's record starts with Alexander Acosta. He questioned Acosta about the Epstein deal at his 2017 Labor confirmation, led the December 2018 Inspector General request with Murray, and after a judge ruled the plea deal violated victims' rights said, "It's appalling that Acosta and his team let Epstein get away with pleading guilty to prostitution charges" (source ↗ https://www.kaine.senate.gov/press-releases/kaine-statement-on-jeffrey-epstein-case-ruling). In April 2019 he and Murray asked DOJ's Office of Professional Responsibility to publish its findings (source ↗ https://www.kaine.senate.gov/press-releases/murray-kaine-urge-department-of-justice-to-publicize-findings-of-investigation-into-handling-of-jeffrey-epstein-case); that July: "Acosta must go. He handed a sweetheart deal to a serial sexual predator." (source ↗ https://x.com/timkaine/status/1148336681965277185) When OPR found "poor judgment" but no misconduct in 2020 he asked, "How is letting a child sex trafficker walk free anything but gross professional misconduct?" (source ↗ https://www.kaine.senate.gov/press-releases/kaine-statement-on-dojs-finding-of-no-misconduct-in-2006-epstein-case) In 2025 he voted to keep the amendment alive, signed the November 18 letter, and said Congress "will be watching closely to make sure the Justice Department complies with the letter and spirit of this legislation in good faith" (source ↗ https://www.kaine.senate.gov/press-releases/kaine-statement-on-senate-passage-of-bill-to-release-epstein-files). On Meet the Press after the deadline he said Clinton "should address" any unanswered questions (source ↗ https://thehill.com/homenews/senate/5658917-kaine-says-clinton-should-address-questions-on-epstein-files/) and called contempt or impeachment "premature," preferring appropriations leverage (source ↗ https://thehill.com/homenews/senate/5660147-epstein-files-contempt-impeachment-tim-kaine/). In February 2026: "The DOJ is running a Trump cover-up operation." (source ↗ https://x.com/timkaine/status/2026780473461014946)

### Maria Cantwell (Democrat-WA)
**Position:** Marked the vote only · **Sept. 10, 2025 vote:** opposed blocking · **Distinct actions:** 2 (5 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Maria%20Cantwell)

She joined the 2018 letter with Murray asking DOJ to investigate the Acosta deal (link dead as of Sep 2026; no text preserved ↗ https://www.cantwell.senate.gov/news/press-releases/cantwell-murray-colleagues-urge-department-of-justice-to-investigate-decision-making-process-for-epstein-cas). In 2025 she voted to keep the amendment alive, signed the November 18 caucus letter and issued a release marking passage that quotes the letter: Trump "and his subordinates have obfuscated, misdirected, and obstructed efforts in both the Senate and the House to secure release of the Epstein files." (source ↗ https://www.cantwell.senate.gov/news/press-releases/senate-unanimously-approves-bill-to-compel-public-release-of-the-epstein-files) The letter PDF, a Google News item and an aggregator mirror returned no text.

<a id="patty-murray"></a>

### Patty Murray (Democrat-WA)
**Position:** Led the push · **Sept. 10, 2025 vote:** opposed blocking · **Distinct actions:** 8 (9 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Patty%20Murray)

Murray co-led the 2018 Inspector General request and the 2019 OPR letter with Kaine, and in 2026 used the Appropriations Committee to press Todd Blanche directly. She voted to keep the amendment alive and co-led the November 18 caucus letter to Thune (source ↗ https://www.murray.senate.gov/murray-peters-schumer-durbin-merkley-and-democratic-colleagues-call-on-leader-thune-to-quickly-bring-the-epstein-files-transparency-act-to-floor-for-vote/). Google News items say she brought an Epstein survivor as her State of the Union guest and made a Senate disclosure that drew a response from Kash Patel (summaries; no text). In March she posted that if Republicans were "going to waste time deposing Hillary Clinton, it's well past time Congress gets some answers out of President Trump" (source ↗ https://x.com/PattyMurray/status/2029972428273705151). At the May 19, 2026 budget hearing she pressed Blanche repeatedly to apologize for publishing survivors' names and images ("I just want to hear you say I apologize to those victims") and to meet them; he said "Absolutely" (source ↗ https://www.murray.senate.gov/at-hearing-with-acting-ag-blanche-senator-murray-blasts-outrageous-creation-of-1-8-billion-maga-slush-fund-presses-for-apology-to-epstein-victims/). Two months later, with no meeting scheduled, she and Van Hollen wrote him demanding a date by July 28: "Your responsibility as Acting Attorney General is to pursue justice, not to shield the President." (source ↗ https://www.murray.senate.gov/murray-van-hollen-call-out-blanche-for-failing-to-meet-with-epstein-survivors-press-him-to-make-good-on-his-commitment/) She posted, "We will not let Todd Blanche dismiss or ignore Epstein survivors." (source ↗ https://x.com/PattyMurray/status/2077190011636596988)

<a id="ron-johnson"></a>

### Ron Johnson (Republican-WI)
**Position:** Marked the vote only · **Sept. 10, 2025 vote:** blocked release · **Distinct actions:** 3 (7 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Ron%20Johnson)

On July 16, 2025 he told the Milwaukee Journal Sentinel, "I'm always for transparency," calling the client-list affair a "bizarre situation" and adding, "So there's something that just doesn't smell right here." (source ↗ https://www.yahoo.com/news/articles/did-wisconsin-senators-ron-johnson-155422332.html; source ↗ https://www.jsonline.com/story/news/politics/2025/07/16/ron-johnson-wisconsin-republicans-call-for-transparency-on-epstein/85246725007/) He raised doubts about the files' integrity with Buck Sexton (video; no text). On September 10 he voted to table the amendment; five days later, a Google News item says, he told a telephone town hall he wants the files released (summary; no text). He supported the bill by unanimous consent and, the Journal Sentinel noted, issued no statement on its passage. Three entries are dead Google News links.

**Position:** Marked the vote only · **Sept. 10, 2025 vote:** opposed blocking · **Distinct actions:** 3 (3 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Tammy%20Baldwin)

She voted to keep the amendment alive and signed the November 18 caucus letter (source ↗ https://www.baldwin.senate.gov/news/press-releases/baldwin-demands-senate-immediately-pass-bill-to-release-epstein-files). The next day she told Wisconsin Radio Network the votes should not have been necessary: "We should remind folks that the president could order the release of these files on his own. We don't have to go through these series of votes." (source ↗ https://northwestnow.net/baldwin-says-shed-vote-to-release-epstein-files-but-trump-could-just-release-them-himself/) In 2026 she posted, "Epstein survivors deserve justice. Epstein's associates who were complicit in his horrendous crimes must be held accountable." (source ↗ https://x.com/SenatorBaldwin/status/2021662417974178225)

<a id="james-c-justice"></a>

### James C. Justice (Republican-WV)
**Position:** Deflected (blamed others or minimized) · **Sept. 10, 2025 vote:** blocked release · **Distinct actions:** 1 (1 entry in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=James%20C.%20Justice)

His one statement came six days before the vote, to WBOY on September 4, 2025: "The Epstein situation needs to die and go away. The people that are victims, I have all the compassion in the world for and that's why I say, 'release the information,'" and, "But, I think President Trump is probably doing a balancing act on those files as best can be done." (source ↗ https://www.wboy.com/news/west-virginia/west-virginia-politics/release-the-information-senator-justice-speaks-on-epstein-files-vaccine-exemptions/) He then voted to table the amendment and supported the bill by unanimous consent without a statement.

<a id="shelley-moore-capito"></a>

### Shelley Moore Capito (Republican-WV)
**Position:** Voted to block release · **Sept. 10, 2025 vote:** blocked release · **Distinct actions:** 1 (2 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Shelley%20Moore%20Capito)

Both entries are the same July 17, 2025 press call. She said "full sunlight is always the best policy" and that the issue would not go away, but that Congress need not act: "I don't think that we need to have congressional action on this, and so I haven't actually voted on that. I don't know that that's an opportunity that I'll have, or should have. I think the Department of Justice and the president are going to feel the pressure here." (source ↗ https://wvmetronews.com/2025/07/17/capito-epstein-documents-should-be-made-public-but-congressional-action-isnt-necessary/) The opportunity came September 10 and she voted to table the amendment. She supported the bill by unanimous consent without a statement. The Wayne County News copy is paywalled and partly unreadable.

<a id="cynthia-m-lummis"></a>

### Cynthia M. Lummis (Republican-WY)
**Position:** Took action · **Sept. 10, 2025 vote:** blocked release · **Distinct actions:** 2 (7 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Cynthia%20M.%20Lummis)

She voted to table the amendment in September. A constituent letter published November 17, 2025 gave her position as support for releases already made and for more: "I believe sunlight is the best disinfectant and transparency is key to the justice system. I will continue to push for the release of additional internal Justice Department memos and evidence that remains classified." (source ↗ https://oilcity.news/opinion/2025/11/17/opinion-lummis-responds-on-epstein-files-transparency-must-protect-victims/) She supported the bill by unanimous consent. On February 9, 2026, the first day DOJ let members view the unredacted files, she went, and outside the building told a reporter: "I've not been one of the members who has glommed on to this as an issue," and, "Well, initially, my reaction to all this was, 'I don't care. I don't know what the big deal is.' But now I see what the big deal is, and it was worth investigating." (source ↗ https://cowboystatedaily.com/2026/02/10/now-i-see-what-the-big-deal-is-lummis-gets-a-look-at-unredacted-epstein-files/) Her spokesman then said she "has always supported justice for Jeffrey Epstein's victims of all ages" (source ↗ https://capcity.news/wyoming/politics-wyoming/2026/02/10/sen-lummis-lawmakers-pushing-for-epstein-file-release-were-not-wrong/). Five entries are national coverage of that one remark. She is retiring.

<a id="john-barrasso"></a>

### John Barrasso (Republican-WY)
**Position:** Voted to block release · **Sept. 10, 2025 vote:** blocked release · **Distinct actions:** 5 (5 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=John%20Barrasso)

As Majority Whip, Barrasso voted to table the amendment in September and twice used the floor to block enforcement of the law he let pass. Google News items say he backed Bondi during the July 2025 furor and told NBC the November vote was a Democratic attempt to make Trump a "lame duck" (summaries; no text). On Meet the Press two days before the House vote: "We'll see if they send something to the Senate, and if they do, we'll take a look at that if it passes the House and we'll see what it says." (source ↗ https://cowboystatedaily.com/2025/11/19/barrasso-senate-gop-ignore-house-leader-and-pass-bill-to-release-epstein-files/) After Trump reversed, Senate leaders let it pass by unanimous consent; his spokeswoman declined to say why. On February 5, 2026 he objected to Schumer's request to pass a resolution authorizing the Senate to sue DOJ: "This is another reckless political stunt designed to distract Americans from Democrats' dangerous plan to shut down the Department of Homeland Security." (source ↗ https://thehill.com/homenews/senate/5724576-epstein-files-lawsuit-senate-schumer-barrasso/; C-SPAN video of the objection ↗ https://www.c-span.org/program/us-senate/sens-schumer-and-barrasso-on-release-of-epstein-files/673006) When DOJ opened the unredacted files to members, his spokeswoman said he "doesn't need to review the unredacted files to confirm what he and the rest of the world already know: Jeffrey Epstein was a disgusting monster." (source ↗ https://cowboystatedaily.com/2026/02/10/now-i-see-what-the-big-deal-is-lummis-gets-a-look-at-unredacted-epstein-files/)

<a id="part-ii"></a>

# Part II. The House

*What every House member said about the Epstein files, read from the record, one entry each.*

The House did what the Senate never did: it took a recorded vote. On November 18, 2025 it passed the Epstein Files Transparency Act 427 to 1 ([Roll Call 289](https://clerk.house.gov/Votes/2025289)). The one No was Clay Higgins of Louisiana. Every entry below gives that vote.

The vote happened only because it was forced. For four months after the bill was introduced, the Republican leadership kept it off the floor. In July the Rules Committee rejected, 5 to 7, an amendment that would have forced the Attorney General to publish the records, with Ralph Norman the only Republican voting for it ([Tampa Free Press](https://www.tampafp.com/south-carolina-rep-norman-only-republican-to-back-epstein-file-release/)), and advanced instead a non-binding resolution that Democrats called toothless ([CNBC, July 18, 2025](https://www.cnbc.com/2025/07/18/epstein-files-trump-johnson-transparency.html)). Days later Speaker Johnson adjourned the House early for its August break rather than hold a vote, a decision that drew bipartisan backlash ([Inforum](https://www.inforum.com/news/north-dakota/north-dakotas-congressional-delegation-supports-release-of-epstein-files)). On September 2, 2025, Thomas Massie filed a discharge petition on H.Res. 581, the procedural motion that lets a majority of members force a floor vote over the leadership's objection ([the petition, with every signature](https://clerk.house.gov/119/lrc/pd/petitions/DisPet0009.xml)). It needed 218 signatures. For months it sat at 217 names ([Cowboy State Daily](https://cowboystatedaily.com/2025/11/19/barrasso-senate-gop-ignore-house-leader-and-pass-bill-to-release-epstein-files/)). It reached 218 on November 12, when Adelita Grijalva, elected in a September special election, was sworn in and signed the same day ([Miami Herald](https://www.miamiherald.com/news/politics-government/state-politics/article312920650.html)). The Speaker's office tied the seven-week delay in seating her to the government shutdown; Democrats said it was to keep her off the petition, and one estimate put the vote as much as 50 days later than it would otherwise have been ([CNS Maryland](https://cnsmaryland.org/2025/11/18/marylands-house-members-unanimous-as-epstein-files-release-passes/)). **Two hundred fourteen Democrats and four Republicans signed: Massie, Nancy Mace, Lauren Boebert, and Marjorie Taylor Greene.** Every other Republican who then voted yes had declined, for two months, to be one of the 218. Every entry says whether the member signed.

Each entry gives the member's Nov. 18 vote, whether they signed the petition, how many distinct things they actually did or said once duplicates are collapsed, a link to their full record in the database, and then, in plain prose, what they said, with the lines that matter quoted exactly and linked to the specific source. Where a member's record is entirely third-party reporting rather than their own words, the entry says so. Where a source link has died since the statement was gathered, the entry says that too. The terms used in every entry are defined once, in the Terms table at the front of this document.

## Entries


<a id="h-nicholas-j-begich-iii"></a>

### Nicholas J. Begich III (Republican-AK-0)
**Position:** Marked the vote only · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** did not sign · **Distinct actions:** 2 (3 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Nicholas%20J.%20Begich%20III)

Nothing in the record is in his own voice; all three entries are other people writing about him. He did not sign the petition and then voted for the bill. Before the vote, the Alaska Democratic Party attacked his silence after the House adjourned early in July, saying he "hasn’t released a single statement about the adjournment, the files, or why Alaskans should accept his silence." (source ↗ https://alaskademocrats.org/nick-begich-chooses-silence-over-transparency/) Columnist Dermot Cole quoted a constituent letter in which Begich explained why he would not sign: "What Congress can do, and what I support, is to direct the administration to make publicly available any records that are not under jurisdiction of the Federal District Courts," and "I remain fully committed to supporting any lawful effort that encourages the public release of these records." (source ↗ https://www.dermotcole.com/reportingfromalaska/2025/11/13/begich-the-third-refuses-to-back-release-of-the-epstein-files) On the day of the vote he told Alaska Public Media he would have voted yes even if Trump had kept opposing the bill: "The American people deserve transparency," and said he hoped the Senate would amend it to protect victims, the kind of change the petition's backers feared would weaken it. (source ↗ https://alaskapublic.org/news/politics/washington-d-c/2025-11-18/begich-like-rest-of-u-s-house-votes-to-release-epstein-files)

<a id="h-barry-moore"></a>

### Barry Moore (Republican-AL-1)
**Position:** Deflected, and redirected to the Clintons · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** did not sign · **Distinct actions:** 4 (9 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Barry%20Moore)

He did not sign the petition. On September 3, 2025 he voted for the House Republican rule that carried a non-binding resolution endorsing the Oversight Committee's investigation, the leadership's alternative to the petition, and put out a statement: "Today, I voted for the Oversight Committee to continue its full and thorough investigation into Jeffrey Epstein," adding that "President Trump's administration has been the most transparent administration in history." (source ↗ https://alpolitics.com/rep-barry-moore-releases-statement-on-continuing-oversight-committee-investigation-into-jeffrey-epstein/) Both copies of that release on his House site are dead. When the petition forced the vote he voted yes and said he did so "to build on the transparency that President Trump’s Department of Justice and House Oversight Committee Republicans are already working to ensure," and that "any suggestion otherwise is a lie meant to distract and divide Republicans." (source ↗ https://alpolitics.com/barry-moore-votes-yes-on-epstein-files-release/) The next morning on WVNN radio he said the Democrats' push "has totally backfired," claimed the files showed "two sitting members of Congress on the Democrat Party that were tied very closely" to Epstein, asserted that Hakeem Jeffries "was soliciting Epstein for donations after he was a convicted felon," and brought up Stacey Plaskett and Bill Clinton's flights. (source ↗ https://1819news.com/news/item/moore-democrat-push-to-release-epstein-files-has-totally-backfired) In 2026, by then a candidate for Alabama's open Senate seat, he told Newsmax that Attorney General Bondi's dismissal reflected "some things that Pam has missed" on the Epstein files and that "it's time for a change." (source ↗ https://1819news.com/news/item/barry-moore-bondi-dismissal-gives-opportunity) Six of his eleven entries are dead links (two House releases, two Facebook posts, two MSN copies of the Newsmax appearance).

<a id="h-shomari-figures"></a>

### Shomari Figures (Democrat-AL-2)
**Position:** Marked the vote only · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** signed · **Distinct actions:** 3 (4 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Shomari%20Figures)

He signed the petition and voted for the bill; nothing in the record is in his own voice. In September 2025 he discussed the files in a Nexstar interview whose page carries only the video (source ↗ https://www.dcnewsnow.com/news/capitol-conversations-rep-shomari-figures-talks-epstein-files-disaster-aid/), and AL.com reported him arguing that Republicans would want the files out if Obama had been involved (summary; source text not retrieved) (link dead as of Sep 2026 ↗ https://www.al.com/news/2025/09/alabama-congressman-would-gop-want-epstein-files-released-if-obama-was-involved.html). After the vote he told Alabama Daily News: "I’m glad to get the vote done, get this behind us, and hopefully get on with doing some work that actually matters in terms of driving down the cost for life here in America." (source ↗ https://aldailynews.com/alabamas-delegation-votes-to-release-epstein-files/)

<a id="h-robert-b-aderholt"></a>

### Robert B. Aderholt (Republican-AL-4)
**Position:** Said nothing · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** did not sign · **Distinct actions:** 0 · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Robert%20B.%20Aderholt) (returns empty)

Did not sign the petition; voted for the bill. The database holds nothing under his name, though an Alabama Daily News report filed under his colleagues quotes him on the vote: "I think that this is probably the best way that this could have gone forward." (source ↗ https://aldailynews.com/alabamas-delegation-votes-to-release-epstein-files/)

<a id="h-dale-w-strong"></a>

### Dale W. Strong (Republican-AL-5)
**Position:** Took action · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** did not sign · **Distinct actions:** 3 (3 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Dale%20W.%20Strong)

He called for release months before the vote, on the radio rather than on the petition, which he did not sign. In summer 2025, while Trump was calling the matter a hoax, he told WVNN's Dale Jackson that the party had "misplayed" the issue: "I believe that it has been handled poorly, to say the least. Everybody knows my position. Put the information out on Epstein," and "How in the world the Republican Party is now playing defense on this issue is absolutely ridiculous." He paired the demand with calls to release the Kennedy and King assassination files. (source ↗ https://1819news.com/news/item/put-the-information-out-u-s-rep-strong-calls-on-trump-administration-to-release-epstein-mlk-jfk-files) A second copy of that story is a dead link. On the day of the vote he told Alabama Daily News he had been talking about the need for this vote "for months" and that "it's time" for the files to come out (source ↗ https://aldailynews.com/alabamas-delegation-votes-to-release-epstein-files/); WSFA reported he released his own statement a couple of hours after the vote, but the MSN copy of that story carries no text. (source ↗ https://www.wsfa.com/2025/11/18/alabama-us-lawmakers-react-after-voting-epstein-files/) Nothing in the record is in his own voice; everything is reporters quoting him.

<a id="h-gary-j-palmer"></a>

### Gary J. Palmer (Republican-AL-6)
**Position:** Marked the vote only · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** did not sign · **Distinct actions:** 1 (2 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Gary%20J.%20Palmer)

He did not sign the petition, voted for the bill, and issued one statement, which both entries record; the copy on a press-release aggregator is dead. The Alabama Gazette quoted it: "The release of these documents, along with the Oversight Committee's thorough investigation, bring us closer to providing the victims of Jeffery Epstein with the justice they deserve. I hope that these documents will provide the American people with answers and result in those that committed wrongdoing being held accountable." (source ↗ https://www.alabamagazette.com/story/2025/11/17/news/representative-gary-palmer-votes-for-release-of-epstein-files-citing-justice-for-victims/9020.html) The same day he told Alabama Daily News, in a report filed under his colleagues: "I’ve got some reservations because the resolution is so poorly written," and "I’m concerned that it could possibly expose some victims and potentially child victims." (source ↗ https://aldailynews.com/alabamas-delegation-votes-to-release-epstein-files/)

<a id="h-terri-a-sewell"></a>

### Terri A. Sewell (Democrat-AL-7)
**Position:** Marked the vote only · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** signed · **Distinct actions:** 2 (3 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Terri%20A.%20Sewell)

She signed the petition and, shortly before the vote, posted a video captioned "On my way to vote ‘YES’ on releasing the Epstein files!" as WSFA reported (source ↗ https://www.wsfa.com/2025/11/18/alabama-us-lawmakers-react-after-voting-epstein-files/); Alabama Daily News reported that on social media she said "transparency is always the best policy." (source ↗ https://aldailynews.com/alabamas-delegation-votes-to-release-epstein-files/) Her one own-voice entry came on April 2, 2026, when Trump fired Attorney General Bondi: "It’s about time! Pam Bondi weaponized the Justice Department to protect the rich and powerful and do the bidding of Donald Trump," including what she called "a sweeping cover-up of the Epstein files." (source ↗ https://x.com/RepTerriSewell/status/2039821348017782882)

**Position:** Said nothing · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** did not sign · **Distinct actions:** 0 · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Eric%20A.%20%22Rick%22%20Crawford) (returns empty)

<a id="h-j-french-hill"></a>

### J. French Hill (Republican-AR-2)
**Position:** Worked against release, then voted yes · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** did not sign · **Distinct actions:** 3 (3 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=J.%20French%20Hill)

Nothing in the record is in his own voice; the substance comes from a constituent letter published by the Arkansas Times and a radio interview. In mid-July 2025 he voted with his party against a Democratic attempt to force the files out. Asked why by a Little Rock constituent, he wrote that the vote "was about making sure we could continue doing the work that Americans sent us here to do," and, per the paper, called the Democratic efforts stunts. He added: "I also believe the American people deserve transparency from their government, which is why I was pleased that President Trump ordered the Attorney General to release all relevant information. However, we must balance transparency with the need to protect innocent victims and witnesses." (source ↗ https://arktimes.com/arkansas-blog/2025/07/25/rep-french-hill-blames-democrats-for-trump-not-releasing-epstein-files) Two database entries carry that one article. Days later he told Talk Business & Politics: "I would say people want the files released as a general matter. They want transparency for the American people," while cautioning that "there are also some rules around what actually can be released as it relates to a federal judge sealing it or grand jury testimony." (source ↗ https://talkbusiness.net/2025/07/rep-hill-discusses-crypto-trade-and-epstein-files/) He did not sign the petition and voted for the bill once it reached the floor; the only entry on the vote is a Google News shell with no text.

<a id="h-steve-womack"></a>

### Steve Womack (Republican-AR-3)
**Position:** Marked the vote only · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Not voting · **Discharge petition:** did not sign · **Distinct actions:** 1 (4 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Steve%20Womack)

He missed the vote, the first he had missed since 2011, because of a family member's medical emergency, and issued one statement, which all four entries record. "The federal government owes the American people transparency and accountability. The public has a right to know the identities of any individuals who were directly or indirectly involved in the despicable crimes of Jeffrey Epstein. Had I been in D.C. today, I would have voted in support of H.R. 4405." (source ↗ https://womack.house.gov/news/documentsingle.aspx?DocumentID=410481) The Southwest Times Record reported that his intended yes vote was entered into the Congressional Record. (source ↗ https://www.swtimes.com/story/news/2025/11/18/arkansas-rep-steve-womack-explains-missed-vote-on-epstein-records-bill/87341443007/) He did not sign the petition. Two 5NEWS copies carry no text.

<a id="h-bruce-westerman"></a>

### Bruce Westerman (Republican-AR-4)
**Position:** Marked the vote only · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** did not sign · **Distinct actions:** 1 (3 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Bruce%20Westerman)

He did not sign the petition and voted for the bill without a statement of his own; the only entry on the vote is a Google News shell. His one recorded remark came in a hallway exchange with Zeteo, after the unredacted files were opened to members: he said he had not gone to see them and hadn't "decided yet" whether he would, because "I've got a lot of stuff going on." (source ↗ https://x.com/zeteo_news/status/2021764573666463863) The database dates the clip to November 2025; the post identifier and its subject put it in February 2026.

<a id="h-david-schweikert"></a>

### David Schweikert (Republican-AZ-1)
**Position:** Marked the vote only · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** did not sign · **Distinct actions:** 2 (3 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=David%20Schweikert)

Nothing in the record is in his own voice. A constituent's letter published in the Daily Independent said he had voted against releasing the files in July 2025 and that, asked to explain, his office sent "a form letter thanking me for sharing my opinion" and told the writer to wait for a newsletter. (source ↗ https://www.yourvalley.net/stories/schweikert-needs-to-explain-his-vote-on-the-epstein-files,614641) He did not sign the petition and voted for the bill. On the day of the vote he discussed it in a Politico video interview; neither that page nor the Google News copy of it yields any text (summary; source text not retrieved). (source ↗ https://www.politico.com/video/2025/11/18/rep-david-schweikert-r-ariz-talks-epstein-file-vote-and-financial-cybercrime-1838945)

<a id="h-elijah-crane"></a>

### Elijah Crane (Republican-AZ-2)
**Position:** Took action, then pulled back · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** did not sign · **Distinct actions:** 4 (6 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Elijah%20Crane)

He was an early supporter who did not follow through on the petition. By his own account he cosponsored Massie's H.Res. 581 on July 16, 2025 (source ↗ https://crane.house.gov/2025/11/18/rep-crane-supports-epstein-files-transparency-act/), and at a digital town hall a week later, asked whether he backed releasing the full unredacted file, said: "I believe transparency should be the hallmark of every administration whether they’re Democrat or Republican." (source ↗ https://www.kjzz.org/politics/2025-07-23/arizona-rep-eli-crane-key-trump-supporter-joins-call-for-release-of-epstein-files) When Massie refiled the discharge petition in September, Crane did not sign. The Arizona Republic reported that he "has retreated from the issue," was noncommittal when CNN asked him about it, did not return the paper's calls, and on the day Democrats released the birthday note reposted a Republican leadership statement accusing them of "cherry-picking documents and politicizing information." (source ↗ https://redraiderswire.usatoday.com/story/news/politics/arizona/2025/09/09/eli-crane-jeffrey-epstein-files/86049222007/) He voted for the bill once the petition forced it and said: "By voting to release records related to Jeffrey Epstein, we are taking an important step toward justice for the victims and consequences for those responsible for these egregious crimes," while urging the Senate to "avoid unfairly damaging the reputations of those who did nothing wrong." (source ↗ https://crane.house.gov/2025/11/18/rep-crane-supports-epstein-files-transparency-act/) His Facebook post says the same. (source ↗ https://www.facebook.com/rep.elicrane/posts/today-i-voted-in-favor-of-the-epstein-files-transparency-act-to-support-accounta/841365098835183/) One KJZZ copy and an aggregator copy are dead, and one USA Today entry returns an unrelated 2015 Florida story.

<a id="h-yassamin-ansari"></a>

### Yassamin Ansari (Democrat-AZ-3)
**Position:** Led the push · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** signed · **Distinct actions:** 10 (10 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Yassamin%20Ansari)

An Oversight Committee member and original cosponsor of the bill who signed the petition and has stayed on the Justice Department since. With Ranking Member Garcia and fourteen other Oversight Democrats she wrote to Chairman Comer asking for a hearing built around survivors: "If we are to hold powerful people to account, our investigation must center the voices they tried to silence." (source ↗ https://ansari.house.gov/media/press-releases/ansari-leads-oversight-democrats-call-for-congressional-hearing-with-survivors-of-epstein-abuse) On the day of the vote she said from the floor, "Donald Trump could direct the Department of Justice to release the files today. He is choosing not to," and in her statement called it "an ongoing cover-up by the Trump White House and enabled by Republicans." (source ↗ https://ansari.house.gov/media/press-releases/ansari-statement-on-voting-yes-on-hr-4405-the-epstein-files-transparency-act) In December she posted a video after reviewing unredacted files at the Justice Department (the page yields no text) (source ↗ https://ansari.house.gov/media/videos/just-left-the-doj-where-i-was-looking-at-some-more-unredacted-epstein-files) and, when Bondi missed the December 19 deadline, said: "The deadline on Friday was not meant to be an initial deadline. It was not a suggestion; that was the legal deadline." (source ↗ https://cactuspolitics.com/2025/12/yassamin-ansari-puts-doj-under-fire-for-incomplete-epstein-release/) After the committee deposed Les Wexner in February 2026 she told CNN it was "hard to believe him," adding, "There’s no name that I have heard more from the victims, after I hear about Ghislaine [Maxwell] and Jeffrey himself, than Les Wexner, because they say, ‘Follow the money.'" (source ↗ https://thehill.com/homenews/house/5744986-yassamin-ansari-les-wexner-jeffrey-epstein-files/) When Bondi was fired in April 2026 she wrote that Bondi "MUST come to testify before the Oversight Committee or be held in contempt of Congress." (source ↗ https://x.com/RepYassAnsari/status/2041915901314847234) In June 2026 she joined a survivor and legal analyst Katie Phang on a panel at a public reading room of the released files, saying "The Trump Administration’s handling of the Epstein Files has been nothing short of disgusting." (source ↗ https://ansari.house.gov/media/press-releases/ansari-to-lead-panel-discussion-at-epstein-files-redacted-reading-room) A video calling the handling of the files "a massive cover-up," a March 2026 video on "Major new developments," and a report that she said Todd Blanche must be investigated carry no readable text; two House releases are dead copies of live ones.

<a id="h-greg-stanton"></a>

### Greg Stanton (Democrat-AZ-4)
**Position:** Took action · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** signed · **Distinct actions:** 3 (4 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Greg%20Stanton)

He signed the petition and voted for the bill, and spoke up when Speaker Johnson sent the House home early in July 2025 rather than face votes on the files: "Right now Congress should be working on lowering cost for the American people… instead I'm heading to the airport. Mike Johnson, the speaker, is sending us home early all because they refuse to vote on the Epstein files," and "The American people deserve to know the truth. Release the Epstein files and let's get back to doing the people's work in Congress." (source ↗ https://cactuspolitics.com/2025/07/rep-stanton-speaks-out-on-early-dismissal-from-congress-due-to-epstein-files/) Two entries carry that one article. After the law took effect he posted on Facebook: "The law says DOJ can’t hide Epstein files or notes because they’re embarrassing or “politically sensitive.” The only redactions allowed are to protect victims and basic safety." (source ↗ https://www.facebook.com/repgregstanton/posts/the-law-says-doj-cant-hide-epstein-files-or-notes-because-theyre-embarrassing-or/1402940441201198/) A Spanish-language video in which he discussed the Justice Department's release of more than three million documents is in the record with only its description (summary; source text not retrieved). (source ↗ https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=drMC-b6Oaik)

<a id="h-andy-biggs"></a>

### Andy Biggs (Republican-AZ-5)
**Position:** Worked against release, then Deflected · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** did not sign · **Distinct actions:** 5 (6 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Andy%20Biggs)

A longtime "Where is Epstein's client list?" voice, by then running for governor with Trump's endorsement, he was one of two Republicans on a House Oversight subcommittee who voted on July 23, 2025 against a Democratic motion to demand the files from the Justice Department; the motion passed 8-2 with three Republicans joining the Democrats. Phoenix New Times reported the vote and that his chief of staff did not respond. (source ↗ https://www.phoenixnewtimes.com/news/arizona-rep-biggs-votes-against-releasing-epstein-files-22170627/) He did not sign the petition. The day after Adelita Grijalva's signature brought it to 218, he told KJZZ he would vote for release: "I don’t know what the Epstein files are, but what I’m in favor of is releasing and being transparent with all the documentation that they have that they can release." (source ↗ https://azmirror.com/briefs/andy-biggs-says-he-will-vote-to-release-the-epstein-files-despite-trumps-opposition/) His vote-day statement turned on the other side: "For four years, the Biden administration and congressional Democrats remained silent on the release of Epstein-related records," and "Their ultimate goal is not to help the victims get justice and peace, but to try to deliver a body blow to the President." (source ↗ https://biggs.house.gov/media/press-releases/congressman-biggs-issues-statement-houses-passage-epstein-transparency) In February 2026 he posted that Oversight "is deposing Epstein associate Ghislaine Maxwell today, and we’re looking for answers." (source ↗ https://x.com/RepAndyBiggsAZ/status/2020876126113927315) Two Google News entries (one on his break with Trump, one on a C-SPAN appearance about the Ron Klain deposition) carry no text.

<a id="h-juan-ciscomani"></a>

### Juan Ciscomani (Republican-AZ-6)
**Position:** Marked the vote only · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** did not sign · **Distinct actions:** 2 (6 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Juan%20Ciscomani)

KJZZ counted him among the Arizona Republicans who "have remained mum on the Epstein case" in July 2025 (source ↗ https://www.kjzz.org/politics/2025-07-24/arizona-congressional-republicans-divided-in-their-response-to-epstein-controversy), and he did not sign the petition. His first recorded comments came on November 17, the day after Trump dropped his opposition, in a Fox News Radio interview where, by the host's summary, he stressed releasing key information while protecting victims' identities. (source ↗ https://radio.foxnews.com/2025/11/17/juan-ciscomani-epstein-release/) After voting yes he posted: "Today, I voted in favor of releasing the Epstein files because I support full transparency from the federal government to the American people, and my number one concern is making sure that victims..." (the post is captured only to that point). (source ↗ https://www.facebook.com/repciscomani/posts/today-i-voted-in-favor-of-releasing-the-epstein-files-because-i-support-full-tra/851402527842624/) Three entries are that one post; a Google News entry on his pledge to vote yes is dated July in the database but describes the November reversal, and one KJZZ copy is dead.

<a id="h-adelita-s-grijalva"></a>

### Adelita S. Grijalva (Democrat-AZ-7)
**Position:** Led the push · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** signed · **Distinct actions:** 6 (12 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Adelita%20S.%20Grijalva)

Hers was the 218th signature. She promised during the special election to sign the petition (source ↗ https://cronkitenews.azpbs.org/2025/09/25/adelita-grijalva-house-petition-vote-epstein-files/), won on September 23, 2025, and then waited seven weeks while Speaker Johnson declined to swear her in during the shutdown. She told NPR: "I remember on election night, someone came up to me and said, 'I don't think they're going to swear you in because of those Epstein files,'" and "I thought, 'oh my gosh, that's very much a conspiracy theory. Like that's not going to happen.' And here we are." (source ↗ https://www.npr.org/2025/10/16/g-s1-93709/adelita-grijalva-mike-johnson-swearing-in) Sworn in on November 12, she signed on the House floor minutes later with two survivors in the gallery, saying "Justice cannot wait another day" and "It's past time for Congress to restore its role as a check and balance on this administration." (source ↗ https://abcnews.com/Politics/rep-elect-grijalva-sworn-months-after-special-election/story?id=127417242) The Hill reported that Johnson, rather than wait out the petition's seven-day clock, announced a vote for the following week. (source ↗ https://thehill.com/homenews/house/5602658-discharge-petition-epstein-files-grijalva/) On the day of the vote she joined Senator Kelly, Robert Garcia and survivors at a press conference and said: "I am standing here because survivors forced Washington to pay attention," crediting the petition's success "despite Speaker Johnson doing everything in his power to prevent this from happening, including calling an early summer recess, and delaying my swearing in for seven weeks." (source ↗ https://grijalva.house.gov/media/press-releases/congresswoman-grijalva-statement-on-house-passage-of-the-epstein-files-transparency-act) When the Justice Department released only part of the files on the December 19 deadline she said the administration "is in direct violation of the law and is actively defying Congress." (source ↗ https://grijalva.house.gov/media/press-releases/rep-grijalva-statement-on-the-trump-administration-defying-congress-and-failing-to-release-full-epstein-files) In June 2026, reacting to a New York Times account of White House crisis meetings, she wrote: "Trump turned the White House Situation Room into an Epstein damage-control bunker." (source ↗ https://x.com/Rep_Grijalva/status/2064754518592794983) Four Google News entries carry no text, and one "statement" entry points only to her House homepage.

<a id="h-kevin-kiley"></a>

### Kevin Kiley (Independent-CA-3)
**Position:** Marked the vote only · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** did not sign · **Distinct actions:** 4 (8 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Kevin%20Kiley)

Then a Republican, he was the only member of his party to vote "present" on September 3, 2025 when leadership folded a non-binding resolution backing the Oversight investigation into a rule, telling reporters "I don't like having these very divergent topics wrapped into the same rule." Asked whether he would sign the petition Massie had just filed, he said, "I'm looking at it. I think that the central issue for me is whether the protections for victims are adequate." (source ↗ https://www.yahoo.com/news/articles/kevin-kiley-declines-vote-epstein-210707319.html) He never signed. When the vote came he voted yes and said: "Throughout this process, I have consistently supported transparency, while ensuring that the privacy and dignity of victims remain fully protected," and "This should not be a partisan issue." (source ↗ https://www.theunion.com/news/rep-kevin-kiley-votes-to-release-epstein-files-responds-to-court-blocking-texas-maps/article_72b6bb91-14e1-4f28-9cf1-42f2d830d7a9.html) A second local copy and a dead link carry the same release; the House site link for it now serves an unrelated September 2026 post. An MS NOW interview in which, per the summary, he said the files must come out by law yields no text. Two entries describing him brushing off Trump's threat of "consequences" over the Epstein vote misread a February 2026 CNN post: the threat concerned his vote to rescind Canada tariffs, and Epstein came up only in follow-up questions. (source ↗ https://x.com/mkraju/status/2022386915237155302)
