<a id="h-ryan-k-zinke"></a>

### Ryan K. Zinke (Republican-MT-1)
**Position:** Deflected · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** did not sign · **Distinct actions:** 3 (4 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Ryan%20K.%20Zinke)

There is no own-voice statement in the record; everything comes from interviews as reported. On NewsNation in July 2025, after the Justice Department said there was no client list, he defended the administration: "I think Pam Bondi has done a great, great job," "everyone's trying to find a nugget that doesn't exist," and "There's not a huge issue there." He allowed that "It'd be helpful too if they sat down and explained it, maybe with cameras." (source ↗ https://thehill.com/homenews/administration/5403419-ryan-zinke-trump-administration-jeffrey-epstein-files/) The Daily Inter Lake reported that in a separate NonStop Local interview that aired July 24 he said the administration "should release files that are not sealed," and that he did not respond to questions about how he would vote in November. (source ↗ https://dailyinterlake.com/news/2025/nov/18/zinke-downing-stay-mum-on-vote-to-release-epstein-files/) A third July item, on the files and the early recess, has no readable text beyond its opening line. (source ↗ https://www.montanarightnow.com/montana/montanas-zinke-addresses-epstein-files-and-summer-recess-concerns/article_028190c5-4c93-4c2a-86f8-f8790b0fe6a5.html) He did not sign the petition and voted for the bill without a statement in the record. In February 2026 the Montana Democratic Party said he "voted to block the release of the Epstein files last year" and asked whether he had "changed his tune"; that is the party's claim, and the packet contains no roll call for it. (source ↗ https://www.montanademocrats.org/news/zinke-everyones-trying-to-find-epstein-nugget-that-doesnt-exist)

<a id="h-troy-downing"></a>

### Troy Downing (Republican-MT-2)
**Position:** Deflected · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** did not sign · **Distinct actions:** 3 (3 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Troy%20Downing)

He did not sign the petition and, until the day before the vote, would not say how he would vote. At a September 2025 telephone town hall, as reported by KGVO, he said "Republicans are on the side of truth and transparency when it comes to securing justice for the victims of Jeffrey Epstein" and pointed to his vote for House Resolution 668, the leadership measure directing the Oversight Committee to continue its investigation: "While other politicians may grandstand and engage in political theater, I will continue to advocate for transparency and a process that leverages the maximum weight of the law while protecting the victims of these heinous crimes." (source ↗ https://newstalkkgvo.com/montana-downing-epstein-files-release/) (KGVO's story ends by saying he "has proposed H.R. 4405"; he did not, Massie did.) The Daily Inter Lake reported that at a November 5 town hall he said "I am all about making sure that every bit of information about the perpetrators is made public" but "stopped short of vowing support" for the bill, and that on November 12 he said his staff needed to review it to "make sure it is not putting any victims in harm's way." (source ↗ https://dailyinterlake.com/news/2025/nov/18/zinke-downing-stay-mum-on-vote-to-release-epstein-files/) On November 17 he told Bloomberg he planned to vote for release. (source ↗ https://www.bloomberg.com/news/videos/2025-11-17/i-plan-on-voting-for-epstein-doc-release-rep-downing-video) He voted for the bill.

**Position:** Marked the vote only · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** signed · **Distinct actions:** 1 (2 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Donald%20G.%20Davis)

<a id="h-deborah-k-ross"></a>

### Deborah K. Ross (Democrat-NC-2)
**Position:** Led the push · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** signed · **Distinct actions:** 10 (10 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Deborah%20K.%20Ross)

A Judiciary Committee member and Democratic Women's Caucus policy co-chair whose record runs from the petition to a Justice Department inspector general investigation. She signed the petition the night of September 2, 2025 and, after meeting survivors the next day, said "Any Republican who refuses to sign onto the discharge petition will be remembered by history as a bystander who chose the wealthy and powerful over innocent women and girls." (source ↗ https://ross.house.gov/2025/9/congresswoman-ross-statement-following-meeting-with-epstein-survivors) On September 26 she and Raskin led Judiciary Democrats in a letter asking Chairman Jordan to let survivors testify: "The release of Justice Department files related to the sex trafficking ring led by Epstein, while necessary, is not a substitute for the voices of these survivors." (source ↗ https://ross.house.gov/2025/9/rep-ross-and-ranking-member-raskin-lead-judiciary-democrats-in-urging-chairman-jordan-to-invite-epstein-survivors-to-testify-before-judiciary-committee) On the vote: "After months of delays orchestrated by Donald Trump and his enablers in Congress, the House has finally voted to force the release of the Epstein files." (source ↗ https://ross.house.gov/2025/11/congresswoman-ross-votes-to-release-epstein-files) On January 30, 2026 she and Sydney Kamlager-Dove led 67 caucus members in asking the acting inspector general to investigate the department's compliance with the law. (source ↗ https://ross.house.gov/2026/1/ross-leads-democratic-women-s-caucus-in-demanding-that-office-of-the-inspector-general-investigate-doj-s-failure-to-release-the-epstein-files) At Bondi's February hearing, according to her office, Bondi "admitted to Congresswoman Ross for the first time that she did not authorize Maxwell's transfer and doesn't know who did"; on March 2 she, Raskin and Khanna wrote to the Bureau of Prisons that "in the 3.5 million documents produced so far, there does not appear to be a single record documenting her transfer or who authorized it." (source ↗ https://ross.house.gov/2026/3/ross-raskin-khanna-demand-answers-on-ghislaine-maxwell-s-transfer-to-club-fed) She viewed the unredacted files in March: "The reach of this criminal enterprise is deeper than imaginable." (source ↗ https://x.com/RepDeborahRoss/status/2033974440367640844) On April 24 her office announced the inspector general had agreed to open the investigation; she said "Trump's Department of Justice has refused to comply with the Epstein Files Transparency Act in multiple ways." (source ↗ https://ross.house.gov/2026/4/following-push-from-ross-and-dwc-members-doj-inspector-general-launches-investigation-into-mishandling-of-the-epstein-files) In June she opposed Todd Blanche's promotion: "Todd Blanche botched the release of the Epstein Files. Of course Trump wants to give him a promotion." (source ↗ https://x.com/RepDeborahRoss/status/2064105791452279247) Three entries are truncated, dead copies of her own releases, and her campaign site's "Release all the files" page carries only a pointer to an NPR story.

**Position:** Marked the vote only · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** did not sign · **Distinct actions:** 2 (3 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Gregory%20F.%20Murphy)

He added, "my concerns were, and still are, that some innocent individuals become guilty by proxy or just by simply being named in these voluminous articles." <a id="h-valerie-p-foushee"></a>

### Valerie P. Foushee (Democrat-NC-4)
**Position:** Took action · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** signed · **Distinct actions:** 3 (3 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Valerie%20P.%20Foushee)

She signed the petition and voted for the bill; a 2025 item recording her support for release is a dead aggregator link with no text. Her record is what came after the files started coming out. On February 14, 2026 she called for Commerce Secretary Howard Lutnick to resign, saying the released files "reference Secretary Lutnick 223 times" and that he "misled the public about the extent of his relationship with Epstein, which included multiple interactions after Epstein's conviction for soliciting prostitution from a minor. He must resign immediately." (source ↗ https://foushee.house.gov/media/press-releases/ranking-member-foushee-calls-for-resignation-of-secretary-lutnick-for-relationship-to-epstein) (link dead as of Sep 3 2026; text preserved from earlier fetch) In March 2026 she was an original cosponsor of Summer Lee's articles of impeachment against Attorney General Bondi, which her office said cite "defiance of the Epstein Files Transparency Act" among other charges; she said "Under the corrupt leadership of Attorney General Bondi, the Department of Justice has lost the trust of the American people." (source ↗ https://foushee.house.gov/media/press-releases/icymi-rep-foushee-supports-introduction-of-articles-of-impeachment-against-attorney-general-pam-bondi) Two entries are dead copies of the Lutnick release. <a id="h-virginia-foxx"></a>

### Virginia Foxx (Republican-NC-5)
**Position:** Worked against release, then marked the vote · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** did not sign · **Distinct actions:** 6 (8 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Virginia%20Foxx)

As Rules Committee chair she was the member who, for four months, kept the bill off the floor. In July 2025 she presided over the committee votes that rejected Jim McGovern's amendments to force release; when Teresa Leger Fernández told Republicans "You will be voting against the release of the Epstein files," Foxx answered, "You don't know our minds. You don't know our hearts." (source ↗ https://www.newsobserver.com/news/politics-government/article310705085.html) On September 9 she voted against another McGovern amendment, 8 to 4, and her office said "the Massie-Khanna bill is entirely unnecessary" because the Oversight Committee's subpoenas already had the force of law and the Senate would not take it up. (source ↗ https://myfox8.com/national/nc-rep-virginia-foxx-votes-with-republicans-against-advancing-bill-to-force-house-vote-on-epstein-files/) A Politico item from the day the petition was filed, in which she reportedly said Republicans would not try to turn it off, has no text (summary; source text not retrieved). (source ↗ https://www.politico.com/live-updates/2025/09/02/congress/jeffrey-epstein-files-thomas-massie-virginia-foxx-house-rules-committee-00540014) On November 18 she managed the rule on the floor and said she would vote for the bill; McGovern replied, "I gave the gentlelady nine opportunities in the Rules Committee to bring this measure to the floor," and she said "Those of us who did not sign the discharge petition are just as concerned about the victims, or as some are calling them, survivors, as our colleagues who signed the discharge petition." (source ↗ https://www.newsobserver.com/news/politics-government/article312962814.html) The Greensboro News & Record, whose text is readable only after decoding its subscriber scramble, added that she said "it was under President Trump's Department of Justice that Jeffrey Epstein was arrested and tried." (source ↗ https://greensboro.com/news/local/government-politics/article_4872b3bb-7e70-4025-9bd3-09f54332d684.html) She voted for the bill and posted: "Today, I cast my vote in favor of releasing the Epstein files. I also voted to censure Delegate Stacey Plaskett for blatantly colluding with Jeffrey Epstein. Disgraceful behavior!" (source ↗ https://www.facebook.com/RepVirginiaFoxx/posts/1372866434202019/) The C-SPAN listing of the rule debate and a second copy of the Facebook post are duplicates.

**Position:** Marked the vote only · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** did not sign · **Distinct actions:** 5 (7 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Julie%20Fedorchak)

She backed the Oversight Committee route all the way and never signed the petition, but she also never opposed release. In July 2025 she told the Bismarck Tribune, "I support the House Oversight Committee's ongoing work, including its recent vote to authorize a subpoena directing the Department of Justice to release documents related to the Epstein case" and that "If these documents are not released by the time Congress reconvenes, I would support legislative action to compel their release." (source ↗ https://www.inforum.com/news/north-dakota/north-dakotas-congressional-delegation-supports-release-of-epstein-files) In September, explaining why she preferred H.Res. 668 to the petition, she told The Flag: "It takes effect immediately. Any other bill that comes forward will likely have to be passed by the Senate and signed by President Trump which would take who knows how long." (source ↗ https://fischbach.house.gov/2025/9/fischbach-fedorchak-explain-support-for-gop-committee-to-get-epstein-files-not-the-entire-house) On November 14 she told WDAY, "The Oversight Committee is really delivering results and bringing about justice now and faster than this discharge petition will. But that said, I support transparency, I support justice for those victims, and I will support the release of those files through this petition as well." (source ↗ https://www.inforum.com/news/north-dakota/fedorchak-backs-house-vote-to-release-all-epstein-case-files) Her vote-day release credits the September resolution with 65,000 pages and says, "After multiple administrations and Congresses have kept this case in darkness, today is another big step forward into the light." (source ↗ https://www.kxnet.com/news/fedorchak-supports-epstein-transparency/) She told the North Dakota Monitor the same day that she does not support a pardon for Maxwell; the Monitor noted she and Senator Cramer both criticized Democrats for inaction under Biden. (source ↗ https://northdakotamonitor.com/briefs/north-dakota-rep-fedorchak-votes-to-release-unclassified-epstein-files/) KX News and a Flag radio item duplicate the release and the September interview.

<a id="h-mike-flood"></a>

### Mike Flood (Republican-NE-1)
**Position:** Deflected · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** did not sign · **Distinct actions:** 5 (7 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Mike%20Flood)

He said he wanted the files out while opposing the mechanism that got them out, and he kept turning the question toward the previous administration. On a July 23, 2025 press call, reported verbatim by KLIN, he said "If it's up to me, yes, put the report out," but also "I have never supported a discharge petition, which essentially reroutes the entire House majority around our elected leadership," and "nothing happened for the better part of four years with an administration that was actively charging and prosecuting these kind of crimes day in day out." He said the best route was to "stick the pros on our side on this, and that's James Comer from Kentucky." (source ↗ https://klin.com/2025/07/24/put-the-report-out-rep-mike-flood-weighs-in-on-epstein-case/) At an August 4 town hall an audience member asked "Why are you covering up the Epstein files?"; Mediaite reported his answer: "At the next pro forma session of Congress, you'll find my name as a sponsor on a resolution from the House Rules Committee to release the Epstein files, to protect the victims and not re-victimize them again," and "I am for the release of those records." (source ↗ https://www.mediaite.com/media/tv/republican-town-hall-erupts-after-audience-asks-why-are-) He voted for the bill: "I support releasing the Epstein files and voted to do so today. Epstein's victims deserve justice." (source ↗ https://flood.house.gov/media/press-releases/congressman-flood-votes-epstein-files-transparency-act) Pressed at a May 2026 town hall on why the department had not released everything, CNN reported he said, "If President Trump was in the Epstein files, it would have been released," arguing the Biden Justice Department would have publicized it. (source ↗ https://www.cnn.com/2026/05/26/politics/mike-flood-town-hall-anti-weaponization-fund) Five entries are dead links (two Facebook videos, a duplicate release, Nebraska Public Media and NBC), and the one NBC link that loads returns an unrelated 2021 story about messaging apps.

<a id="h-don-bacon"></a>

### Don Bacon (Republican-NE-2)
**Position:** Marked the vote only · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** did not sign · **Distinct actions:** 3 (5 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Don%20Bacon)

He declined the petition, voted for the leadership resolution in September, and then became one of the first Republicans to say he would vote yes on the bill. His release on the resolution vote explained: "I voted yes because the discharge petition only affects files from the Department of Justice, and it's not as instant as people think," and "Many associated with the Discharge petition are just trying to score political points instead of getting to the truth. I've personally talked to Oversight Committee Chairman Comer. He is 100% dedicated to acquiring all the info and releasing it." (source ↗ https://bacon.house.gov/news/documentsingle.aspx?DocumentID=2761) His Facebook post, "I voted today to release the Epstein Files. It is more far reaching than the discharge bill," appears to describe that same September vote although the database dates it to November. (source ↗ https://www.facebook.com/RepDonBacon/posts/i-voted-today-to-release-the-epstein-files-it-is-more-far-reaching-than-the-disc/1488288149757636/) On November 14 KNLV reported he would vote for the bill, quoted him saying "I'm for transparency" and "Everything should be out" apart from victims' names, and reported that he called the White House's handling a "public relations disaster" while doubting the files held surprises because "the Biden administration would have already released the worst about Trump, and vice versa." (source ↗ https://www.knlvradio.com/2025/11/14/rep-don-bacon-supports-release-of-epstein-files-urges-transparency-but-warns-against-bombshell-expectations/) An MSN video and two duplicates carry no text.

**Position:** Marked the vote only · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** did not sign · **Distinct actions:** 1 (3 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Adrian%20Smith)

He did not sign the petition (a Nebraska Examiner item noting that the state's House members declined to sign is a Google News shell with no text), voted for the bill, and issued one release, which he also posted on Facebook: "The crimes of Jeffrey Epstein are appalling. <a id="h-chris-pappas"></a>

### Chris Pappas (Democrat-NH-1)
**Position:** Took action · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** signed · **Distinct actions:** 7 (9 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Chris%20Pappas)

He signed the petition and voted for the bill; the vote-day announcement itself survives only as a Google News shell. His record after that is pressure on the Justice Department and, once he was running for the Senate, pressure on his opponent. On February 24, 2026, responding to NPR's report that pages about accusations against Trump were missing from the public database, he wrote: "Let's be clear: this is illegal. Congress voted to release *all* the Epstein files, not just the ones that don't implicate Trump. The Justice Department needs to release the missing pages now." (source ↗ https://x.com/ChrisPappasNH/status/2026379974710624593) Later he posted, "We passed a bipartisan bill to release the Epstein Files. It wasn't a suggestion to the administration. It was a legal requirement. Every day they stonewall Congress is another day without the answers the American people deserve." (source ↗ https://x.com/ChrisPappasNH/status/2086860571878531096) In July 2026, after the Washington Post reported that John Sununu had falsely denied a 2010 trip to Doha that appears in Epstein's emails, his campaign said "John Sununu was caught brazenly lying about a trip he took to an event that was used by Jeffrey Epstein for networking" and that voters "deserve to know the full truth about Sununu's ties to the Epstein class." (source ↗ https://chrispappas.org/2026/07/chris-pappas-statement-on-new-bombshell-washington-post-report-on-john-sununu-and-the-epstein-files/) He posted, "My opponent's name is in the Epstein files. He got caught lying about it." (source ↗ https://x.com/ChrisPappasNH/status/2079643728461828298) and, "Out of all the people who Epstein could have recommended his associate meet, he pointed to 'john sununu.' Voters deserve answers." (source ↗ https://x.com/ChrisPappasNH/status/2081779044975104405) On MeidasTouch he called Sununu "a poster child for the Epstein class" and said "I think he needs to come clean about what he knows." (source ↗ https://chrispappas.org/2026/07/watch-chris-pappas-discusses-stakes-of-the-nh-senate-race-donald-trumps-corrupt-agenda-and-john-sununus-connection-to-jeffrey-epstein-on-meidastouch/) Granite Post reported that Sununu's campaign acknowledged the false denial while still denying he met Epstein. (source ↗ https://granitepostnews.com/news/video/chris-pappas-calls-out-senate-race-opponent-over-epstein-lies/) Eleven of the eighteen entries are duplicates; the "legal requirement" post is dated November 2025 in the database but its post ID places it in mid-2026.

**Position:** Marked the vote only · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** signed · **Distinct actions:** 2 (5 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Maggie%20Goodlander)

<a id="h-melanie-a-stansbury"></a>

### Melanie A. Stansbury (Democrat-NM-1)
**Position:** Led the push · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** signed · **Distinct actions:** 10 (12 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Melanie%20A.%20Stansbury)

An Oversight Committee member whose district sits near Epstein's Zorro Ranch, she has called the case a cover-up since the petition was filed. After a closed briefing with survivors on September 2, 2025 she said the victims revealed "a cover-up of epic proportions involving the wealthy, the well-connected, and even our own government" reaching, in her words, all the way to the top, and on the floor that day she told Republicans backing the leadership resolution, "You don't want the files released. You want it controlled by the Oversight Committee." (source ↗ https://www.tricityrecordnm.com/articles/rep-stansbury-epstein-cover-up-goes-all-the-way-to-the-top/) On TMZ in September she said Speaker Johnson and Chairman Comer were "actively colluding with the White House to try to prevent further investigation"; on November 12 she told the same show "Donald Trump knows that he's in the Epstein files, and they are engaged in a massive cover-up right now, and they are trying to make Congress complicit." (source ↗ https://www.santafenewmexican.com/news/elections/the-roundhouse-report-stansbury-accuses-white-house-of-epstein-files-cover-up/article_b6ea1c96-ab52-444a-9b19-8a7ebf2de4bb.html) She signed the petition and voted for the bill. When the department's first release came, she told CNN on December 22 that a partial release violated the law and said she would explore inherent contempt of Congress against the department, as reported by the Herald Times. (source ↗ https://www.heraldtimes.org/2025/12/23/rep-melanie-stansbury-partial-release-of-the-epstein-files-violates-the-law/) After Maxwell took the Fifth before the committee on February 9, 2026, she said Maxwell had used the deposition "to send a direct message" to the president "that her silence can be bought through clemency," and that Trump "is named over 38,000 times in the files that were released two weeks ago alone." (source ↗ https://stansbury.house.gov/media/press-releases/video-stansbury-statement-following-ghislaine-maxwells-deposition) After viewing the unredacted files she posted the FBI's list of "Prominent Names" it was tracking: "This list appears in this slide show, FBI email correspondence, and FBI tip sheets, all of which I saw unredacted today. Believe the survivors." (source ↗ https://x.com/Rep_Stansbury/status/2021428487295221960) On a KRQE podcast in March she said "We may only have like two percent of what is in possession of the federal government" and backed a state truth commission on Epstein's New Mexico ties. (source ↗ https://www.krqe.com/digital-shows/insiders/inside-the-epstein-files-with-rep-melanie-stansbury/) On March 14 she said the depositions of Epstein's lawyer and accountant, Darren Indyke and Richard Kahn, mattered because "Lawyers, accountants, and business associates" enabled the crimes and "most of them have never had to answer for their role." (source ↗ https://x.com/Rep_Stansbury/status/2032881536094392609) Nine entries are duplicates or dead links, including her announcement of a state truth-commission leader as her State of the Union guest and a Santa Fe New Mexican story quoting her calling the department's handling "very truly a cover-up." The FBI-list post is dated November 2025 in the database but was posted in February 2026.

As chair of the Democratic Women's Caucus and a Rules Committee member, she pressed from the July 2025 committee votes through a second bill in 2026. In the Rules Committee she told Republicans, "You will be voting against the release of the Epstein files. You have the power, let's see how you vote." (source ↗ https://www.newsobserver.com/news/politics-government/article310705085.html) After the caucus met survivors on September 3 she said, "To my Republican colleagues, if these heartbreaking stories aren't enough, sign the petition for your daughters and for all the women in your lives that you would want protected from pedophiles." (source ↗ https://fernandez.house.gov/news/documentsingle.aspx?DocumentID=523) She signed the petition and voted for the bill. On the December 19 deadline: "Donald Trump and the Department of Justice are now violating federal law and insulting the American people with their release of heavily redacted files that are not searchable and are not complete as required by the Epstein Files Transparency Act." (source ↗ https://democraticwomenscaucus.house.gov/news/documentsingle.aspx?DocumentID=672) On MS NOW in February 2026, the day after Bondi said everything had been released, she said the department "has been engaged in an effort to basically protect pedophiles, to protect the president, and to protect those predators that the Epstein survivors have said we want to expose." (source ↗ https://www.mediaite.com/politics/dem-lawmaker-trump-doj-has-aimed-to-protect-pedophiles-with-redacted-names-in-epstein-files/) She backed New Mexico's own investigation of Zorro Ranch: "In New Mexico, we're trying to turn the Epstein files into trials." (source ↗ https://x.com/RepTeresaLF/status/2032511552784347592) In April 2026 she welcomed the inspector general investigation her caucus had requested. (source ↗ https://ross.house.gov/2026/4/following-push-from-ross-and-dwc-members-doj-inspector-general-launches-investigation-into-mishandling-of-the-epstein-files) On July 15 she introduced the Epstein Files Transparency Act II with Massie and Khanna: "Our bill gives survivors and state attorneys general the power to take the U.S. Department of Justice to court as long as it withholds, delays, or unlawfully redacts the Epstein files like they're doing now." (source ↗ https://x.com/RepTeresaLF/status/2077514427931205657) Announcing it with Attorney General Raúl Torrez on August 12, she said "Attorney General Blanche is not fit to serve, should not be confirmed and should instead resign" (source ↗ https://fernandez.house.gov/news/documentsingle.aspx?DocumentID=648) and told the Santa Fe New Mexican the sponsors would use another discharge petition if the Speaker did not bring it up: "That's if they want to be honorable, that's what they would do." (source ↗ https://www.santafenewmexican.com/news/local_news/torrez-leger-fern-ndez-tout-bill-to-force-more-epstein-disclosures/article_4a6bb524-d32f-4516-ae78-1866f65c2a05.html) A Substack podcast and three news copies of the August announcement fill out the record.

Such accusations are damning for @POTUS." (source ↗ https://x.com/repdinatitus/status/2026353773325558037) When House leaders extended a ban on resolutions of inquiry she wrote, "@POTUS refused to release the Epstein files in full and concealed pertinent information related to Iran." (source ↗ https://x.com/repdinatitus/status/2037243506029191452) When Bondi's exit was reported: "Bye Bye Bondi. Release the Epstein files NOW!" (source ↗ https://x.com/repdinatitus/status/2039746131400282566)

**Position:** Marked the vote only · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** signed · **Distinct actions:** 2 (3 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Steven%20Horsford)

(source ↗ https://www.contrariannews.org/p/we-demand-transparency-rep-steven) Months later he posted, "The House and Senate voted months ago to release the Epstein files, yet the public is still waiting. (the preview cuts off there) (source ↗ https://www.facebook.com/RepHorsford/posts/the-house-and-senate-voted-months-ago-to-release-the-epstein-files-yet-the-publi/1306085248004953/) The third entry duplicates that post.

<a id="h-nick-lalota"></a>

### Nick LaLota (Republican-NY-1)
**Position:** Worked against release, then deflected · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** did not sign · **Distinct actions:** 4 (4 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Nick%20LaLota)

He opposed the petition in public, voted for the bill once it was forced, and used his vote statement to attack the other party. In July 2025 he told Newsday that "If the government has evidence of trafficking, prosecutors should bring charges and make their case" (source ↗ https://www.newsday.com/long-island/politics/trump-epstein-o7ky6v5p); in August he called the petition "reckless" and said it was led by "extremists from each party more interested in stirring the pot than solving problems." (source ↗ https://www.newsday.com/long-island/politics/epstein-files-fl5hm4ak) On November 17 Newsday reported he would vote yes, that he called the bill "hastily" and "sloppily" written, and that he insisted "I announced my intention to vote yes well before the President urged support." (source ↗ https://www.newsday.com/long-island/politics/lalota-garbarino-epstein-files-alugixdb) His November 19 editorial says "I voted YES this week to make them public" and that the bill "lacks adequate protections for victims," then turns: "From 2021 through 2024, the Department of Justice released no Epstein files, brought no new indictments, and showed no urgency," and "You cannot claim to stand with victims of exploitation while ignoring children harmed by your own policies," a reference to migrant children under Biden. (source ↗ https://lalota.house.gov/media/editorial/congressman-nick-lalotas-statement-release-epstein-files) In March 2026 American Journal News, a Democratic-aligned outlet, reported that he had taken $40,000 in campaign donations from people who appear in the files, including Leonard Blavatnik, Ronald Lauder, Anthony Ressler and John Paulson; that is the outlet's reporting, and it noted that appearing in the files does not imply a crime. (source ↗ https://americanjournalnews.com/gop-rep-nick-lalota-took-40000-from-epstein-associates/)

The American people deserve full transparency and accountability from everyone connected to these..." (the preview cuts off there); its wording suggests the September Oversight resolution rather than the November bill. (source ↗ https://www.facebook.com/RepAndrewGarbarino/posts/1251061370375215/)

<a id="h-hakeem-s-jeffries"></a>

### Hakeem S. Jeffries (Democrat-NY-8)
**Position:** Led the push · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** signed · **Distinct actions:** 5 (8 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Hakeem%20S.%20Jeffries)

As Democratic leader he signed the petition and used his podium to press the majority to let it come to a vote. On November 3, 2025, with the petition one signature short and Speaker Johnson declining to swear in Adelita Grijalva during the shutdown, he told reporters, "The Trump administration and Mike Johnson are running a pedophile protection program," adding, "That's what they've been doing, and that's the reason why they refuse to swear in Rep.-elect Adelita Grijalva, for weeks now." (The Hill reported the remarks; the database dates this item July 2025, but the article is dated November 3.) (source ↗ https://thehill.com/homenews/house/5587043-jeffries-trump-johnson-epstein-files/) On the night of the vote he went on CNN, where Kaitlan Collins pressed him three times on Stacey Plaskett's texts with Epstein and on a 2013 fundraising email a consulting firm had sent Epstein in his name. He said of Plaskett, "Stacey Plaskett is not accused of violating any House rule, any law, any statute, and she's clearly and unequivocally denounced Jeffrey Epstein," and of the email, "I have no recollection of the email. I've never had a conversation with him, never met him, know nothing about him other than the extreme things that he's been convicted of doing." (source ↗ https://jeffries.house.gov/2025/11/18/leader-jeffries-on-cnn-our-focus-today-is-on-making-sure-that-the-jeffrey-epstein-files-were-released/) Newsweek covered the same interview and noted the email was sent by the firm, not by Jeffries, and shows no contact or donation. (source ↗ https://www.newsweek.com/hakeem-jeffries-responds-to-jeffrey-epstein-email-revelations-11071268) After the Justice Department's December 19 release he told ABC's This Week, "it does appear, of course, that this initial document release is inadequate. It falls short of what the law requires," and, asked about impeaching Bondi, said "there needs to be a full and complete explanation and then a full and complete investigation as to why the document production has fallen short of what the law clearly requires." (source ↗ https://abcnews.com/Politics/jeffries-doj-release-epstein-files-inadequate-andfalls-short/story?id=128597125) The Hill carried the same interview. (source ↗ https://thehill.com/homenews/house/5658916-jeffries-epstein-files-release-investigation/) Two Google News entries (his calling Comer a "stone cold liar" over the fundraising claim, and a warning that Bondi might try to block disclosure) carry no article text, and a NewsBreak copy of the ABC story is dead.

### Daniel S. Goldman (Democrat-NY-10)
**Position:** Led the push · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** signed · **Distinct actions:** 10 (11 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Daniel%20S.%20Goldman)

A former federal prosecutor on the Judiciary Committee, he was pressing the Justice Department months before the petition existed. On May 12, 2025 he wrote to Bondi about "what appears to be a concerted effort by you to delay and even prevent the release of the Jeffrey Epstein Files in their entirety" and asked whether Trump had intervened; "there is no proper basis to redact the name, identify, or likeness of President Trump." (source ↗ https://goldman.house.gov/media/press-releases/rep-dan-goldman-demands-ag-bondi-release-promised-jeffrey-epstein-files-share) On July 8 he and Raskin led Judiciary Democrats in demanding "all documents in the Epstein files that mention or reference Donald Trump." (source ↗ https://goldman.house.gov/media/press-releases/reps-goldman-raskin-lead-judiciary-democrats-demanding-ag-bondi-release) On July 16 he moved in the Homeland Security Committee to subpoena Bondi and Patel: "Pam Bondi and Kash Patel need to release the files or come in and testify about what they are hiding." (source ↗ https://goldman.house.gov/media/press-releases/rep-dan-goldman-moves-subpoena-pam-bondi-and-kash-patel-over-epstein-files) When Todd Blanche announced he would meet Maxwell, Goldman called it "an end-run around the SDNY" that was "tacitly floating a pardon for Maxwell in return for information that politically benefits President Trump." (source ↗ https://goldman.house.gov/media/press-releases/statement-rep-dan-goldman-dojs-announcement-it-would-meet-ghislaine-maxwell) On CNN the day the petition was filed: "Now, all of a sudden, they don't want them released. They are trying to hide something." (source ↗ https://thehill.com/homenews/house/5483480-goldman-trump-hiding-epstein-files/) He signed the petition and voted for the bill. In February 2026 he said the department's "inept job of redacting identification info of survivors is so bad, it seems like an intentional effort to intimidate the victims" (source ↗ https://x.com/danielsgoldman/status/2018450690465542237) and asked why the department would "track our search history of the 'unredacted' Epstein Files." (source ↗ https://x.com/danielsgoldman/status/2021765937339486521) In March he displayed on the House floor a 2009 email, redacted for the public but not for Congress, in which Epstein's lawyer reported that Trump's lawyer said Epstein "was never asked to leave" Mar-a-Lago; the New Republic quoted him: "if the attorney general is covering up this information that she then reveals to Congress, what else is she covering up about Donald Trump's involvement in the Epstein files?" (source ↗ https://newrepublic.com/post/207950/democratic-congrssman-goldman-epstein-file-trump-epstein-mar-a-lago) In May he wrote that "There are Epstein victims who want to provide evidence to DOJ but are not being allowed to. But Ghislaine Maxwell gets two days with Blanche personally and then moves to a camp. The cover up is massive" (source ↗ https://x.com/danielsgoldman/status/2057988861729312992) and told Blanche that a "room full of Epstein survivors stood up during my line of questioning of your former boss to confirm that they have wanted to give information to the DOJ but have been denied." (source ↗ https://x.com/danielsgoldman/status/2058678720798597250) One WION link is dead; a second copy carries the same story.

<a id="h-jerrold-nadler"></a>

### Jerrold Nadler (Democrat-NY-12)
**Position:** Took action · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** signed · **Distinct actions:** 4 (5 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Jerrold%20Nadler)

His record is from 2019, when he chaired the Judiciary Committee, and from the Bondi hearing in 2026. In July 2019 he signed the Crime Subcommittee's letter asking the Justice Department for a briefing on Epstein's 2007 non-prosecution agreement and "any related commission of professional misconduct by then-United States Attorney Alexander Acosta." (source ↗ https://nadler.house.gov/news/documentsingle.aspx?DocumentID=394000) The day Epstein died he posted that he was "Deeply disturbed to learn of Jeffrey Epstein's death while supposedly on watch at the Metropolitan Correctional Center." (source ↗ https://nadler.house.gov/news/documentsingle.aspx?DocumentID=394034) He signed the petition and voted for the bill. At the February 11, 2026 Judiciary hearing he asked Bondi how many of Epstein's co-conspirators the department had indicted and cut her off: "Answer my question"; she replied, "No, I'm gonna answer the question the way I want to answer the question." (source ↗ https://thenationaldesk.com/news/americas-news-now/ag-bondi-clashed-with-rep-jerry-nadler-regarding-epsteins-co-conspirators) A Google News item on the same exchange has no text.

Most of her record is posts, and most of the database entries for them are Google News shells with no text. After the July 2025 no-client-list memo she wrote, "Wow who would have thought that electing a rapist would have complicated the release of the Epstein Files?"; HuffPost noted the 2023 civil verdict she was referring to and the White House's reply calling her "a sad, miserable blockhead." (source ↗ https://www.huffpost.com/entry/aoc-trump-epstein-files-rapist_n_6872d57fe4b07a168b47c6ea) A June 2025 item in which she reportedly said Trump was willing to "risk world war" to distract from the files, and a November post reportedly calling the bill's text "rushed," carry no text (summary; source text not retrieved). On the December 19 deadline: "Now the coverup is out in the open. This is far from over. Everyone involved will have to answer for this. Pam Bondi, Kash Patel, whole admin. Protecting a bunch of rapists and pedophiles because they have money, power, and connections. Bondi should resign tonight." (source ↗ https://ross.house.gov/2026/1/ross-leads-democratic-women-s-caucus-in-demanding-that-office-of-the-inspector-general-investigate-doj-s-failure-to-release-the-epstein-files)

<a id="h-patrick-ryan"></a>

### Patrick Ryan (Democrat-NY-18)
**Position:** Led the push · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** signed · **Distinct actions:** 5 (5 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Patrick%20Ryan)

A cosponsor of the Massie-Khanna bill, which his office describes as "Congressman Ryan's bipartisan resolution," he pressed the seven New York Republicans by name. Before the July 2025 recess he asked Transportation Secretary Sean Duffy at a hearing to commit to a public conversation about release; his office says Duffy "dodged the question." When Speaker Johnson sent the House home early, Ryan called it a "weak, pathetic move by politicians hellbent on preventing transparency." On September 26, with the petition one signature short, he named LaLota, Garbarino, Malliotakis, Lawler, Stefanik, Langworthy and Tenney and said "All it takes is one of the seven New York Republicans to finally deliver." (source ↗ https://patryan.house.gov/media/press-releases/congressman-pat-ryan-calls-new-york-republicans-provide-deciding-vote-release) He signed the petition and voted for the bill; his passage release, in a Quiver Quantitative mirror, repeats that "Bringing predators to justice is a simple question of law and order." (source ↗ https://www.quiverquant.com/news/Press+Release:+Congressman+Pat+Ryan's+Resolution+to+Release+Epstein+Files+and+Hold+Predators+Accountable+Passes+House) The Bulwark quoted him the day after: "If there weren't explosive stuff in there, they wouldn't be fighting like hell to protect them." When Hochul told her to "Release the Epstein files," she answered with a list of Hochul's own "files" to release, on nursing homes and Medicaid. In August 2026 he posted on X, without mentioning Epstein, that "In light of all the renewed interest in my family affairs I am going to file my own paperwork for an ethics investigation into myself to clear my name from these horrific attacks on myself and family. I have absolutely nothing to hide." (source ↗ https://x.com/MaxMillerOH/status/2084665339799822436) The database ties that post to Epstein files coverage; the post itself does not say what the attacks concern.

### Warren Davidson (Republican-OH-8)
**Position:** Took action · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** did not sign · **Distinct actions:** 4 (6 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Warren%20Davidson)

He did not sign the petition but broke with the president in public before the vote. Three Google News entries, all without article text, record him saying Attorney General Bondi should testify about the handling of the files, jabbing her over the hearing, and being profiled as a rare Republican willing to disagree with Trump on the subject (summary; source text not retrieved). Two YouTube clips, a CNN exchange with Manu Raju about the newly released Epstein emails and a Forbes segment on Speaker Johnson's refusal to advance the bill, carry only titles. Spectrum News reported on November 13, the day after Trump posted that "Only a very bad, or stupid, Republican would fall into that trap," that Davidson planned to vote yes anyway: "If crimes have been committed, I want the Attorney General of the United States or the Attorneys General in the U.S. district courts to bring charges against individuals, for the evidence to come out at trial, where there's convictions and jail time. That's what I think the people want." He added that he did not expect the files to show improper conduct by the president. (source ↗ https://spectrumnews1.com/oh/columbus/news/2025/11/13/epstein-discharge-petition-davidson-trump)

HuffPost reported the exchange: "There are 3 million more documents," she said, and "What is true is that the president has lied about being on Epstein's plane, and the unredacted files prove that." (source ↗ https://www.huffpost.com/entry/todd-blanche-madeleine-dean-epstein-files-congressional-testimony_n_6a1f6fd2e4b0ba317304387c) PBS NewsHour carried her line, "It's all covered up," and "The American people are not stupid. They know that when members of Congress have to go in and actually unredact, try to find the truth for these victims, something is corrupt. Something is corrosive." (source ↗ https://x.com/NewsHour/status/2062258828700217458) She went on CNN in June and again in August 2026 to say the department had violated the act (source ↗ https://dean.house.gov/2026/8/congresswoman-dean-on-doj-s-release-of-the-epstein-files). Six of her fourteen entries are dead links or empty Google News shells for the same hearings.

<a id="h-mary-gay-scanlon"></a>

### Mary Gay Scanlon (Democrat-PA-5)
**Position:** Took action · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** signed · **Distinct actions:** 5 (5 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Mary%20Gay%20Scanlon)

At a Rules Committee hearing on September 2, 2025, the day the petition opened for signatures, she raised the department's redactions in the files it had released; only the video's title survives. She signed the petition and posted in the weeks before the vote that "Trump's DOJ continues to block the release of the Epstein files" (source ↗ https://www.facebook.com/RepMGS/posts/yet-another-reminder-that-trumps-doj-continues-to-block-the-release-of-the-epste/1464659718799736/) and, after another busy week, "you know what didn't happen? The release of the Epstein files." (source ↗ https://www.facebook.com/RepMGS/posts/1314263177172725/) On the day of the vote: "The government should not be a part of a cover up for the rich and powerful. The House and Senate must vote to release ALL the Epstein files without any amendments or loopholes." (source ↗ https://x.com/RepMGS/status/1990801438981956013) When Bondi testified behind closed doors in May 2026 she wrote that at Bondi's February Judiciary appearance "it became clear that her handling of the Epstein files had become a blatant coverup to protect the President and his rich and powerful friends." (source ↗ https://x.com/RepMGS/status/2060368205219037564)

<a id="h-chrissy-houlahan"></a>

### Chrissy Houlahan (Democrat-PA-6)
**Position:** Took action · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** signed · **Distinct actions:** 3 (7 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Chrissy%20Houlahan)

On September 3, 2025, the day after the petition opened, she was among the Democratic Women's Caucus steering committee members who met with survivors who had come to the Capitol; the caucus statement her office posted, issued by Chair Teresa Leger Fernández, said "The survivors called on Members of Congress to use their power to force the release of the files" and urged Republicans to "sign the petition for your daughters and for all the women in your lives." (source ↗ https://houlahan.house.gov/news/documentsingle.aspx?DocumentID=4739) She signed the petition and, by two Google News entries without text, called on every member to do the same. On the day of the vote she said: "Survivors of Jeffrey Epstein's child sex-trafficking crime ring have waited far too long for transparency and accountability. They deserve the truth, and we all deserve a justice system that doesn't shield the powerful and well-connected." (source ↗ https://houlahan.house.gov/news/documentsingle.aspx?DocumentID=4836) Two Facebook copies of that statement returned no text, and a Quiver Quantitative entry is about an unrelated Head Start letter.

<a id="h-ryan-mackenzie"></a>

### Ryan Mackenzie (Republican-PA-7)
**Position:** Marked the vote only · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** did not sign · **Distinct actions:** 4 (11 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Ryan%20Mackenzie)

A swing-district freshman, he took the question at a July 23, 2025 telephone town hall the night after Speaker Johnson sent the House home early. Asked whether Trump is on a client list and whether it should be released, he said, "I'm in support of full transparency," that Biden should have released the files, and, per NPR's account: "I know they have not released as much as I would like to see to date, but hopefully they're going to be doing that. And if not, then Congress should potentially step in and compel them to do that." (source ↗ https://www.kpbs.org/news/politics/2025/07/24/questions-about-epstein-files-follow-lawmakers-home) WHYY carried the same exchange. (source ↗ https://whyy.org/articles/epstein-files-release-pennsylvania-congress-town-hall/) He did not sign the petition that would have compelled release. On November 17, after Trump reversed himself, he told reporters, "As of now, it is scheduled by the speaker to be voted on tomorrow, and I'll be voting in favor of it." (source ↗ https://www.lehighvalleypublicmedia.org/news/local/u-s-rep-ryan-mackenzie-says-hell-vote-to-release-the-epstein-files-tuesday/) After voting yes he posted, "As I have made clear since my town hall in July, the public deserves full transparency in the Epstein Case." (source ↗ https://x.com/RepMackenzie/status/1990868768382394708) Three Google News entries about his town halls carry no text, two links to his release are dead, and a Quiver Quantitative entry concerns FEMA hospital funding, not Epstein.

**Position:** Deflected · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** did not sign · **Distinct actions:** 1 (1 entry in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Daniel%20Meuser)

He said he and Trump had held that "all credible and legal information related to Jeffrey Epstein should be released, and that is exactly what we have done and what we will continue to do." ### Scott Perry (Republican-PA-10)
**Position:** Took action · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** did not sign · **Distinct actions:** 6 (7 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Scott%20Perry)

His record is longer than his silence on the petition suggests, but almost none of it survives as readable text. On July 16, 2025 he sent a letter to Attorney General Bondi pressing the department on its handling and release of Epstein material; the PDF on his site no longer loads (summary; source text not retrieved). The York Daily Record reported on July 24 that he "bucks GOP leadership in seeking release of Jeffrey Epstein files" and that he "has also asked the director of federal prisons to protect Epstein's associate until she can testify before Congress"; the article is paywalled beyond that. (source ↗ https://www.ydr.com/story/news/politics/2025/07/24/u-s-rep-perry-votes-to-release-jeffrey-epstein-files/85358315007/) PennLive fact-checked Epstein claims he made in an August tele town hall (dead link). He did not sign the petition, then voted for the bill. In March 2026 he was, by two Google News entries without text, one of five Republicans who voted to subpoena Bondi in the House Epstein inquiry, and PennLive reported he called that vote consistent with his calls for transparency (dead link; summary; source text not retrieved). A YouTube interview on how the controversy was "gumming-up the legislative process" carries only its title.

<a id="h-lloyd-smucker"></a>

### Lloyd Smucker (Republican-PA-11)
**Position:** Deflected · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** did not sign · **Distinct actions:** 1 (5 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Lloyd%20Smucker)

He did not sign the petition, then voted for the bill and posted a statement late that night on Facebook, which LancasterOnline reprinted in full. It credits Chairman Comer's committee, says "Democrats had access to the same documents under the Biden administration but did not release them. This was never about politics for me or my fellow Republicans. I hope as a nation we can move forward," and turns on the press: "I encourage the media to stop obsessing over every detail of Epstein's past and focus its attention where it belongs." (source ↗ https://lancasteronline.com/news/politics/smucker-vote-to-release-epstein-files-was-in-interest-of-transparency-justice-and-accountability/article_0b0978bb-7c54-4196-bcb2-f40262f63d22.html) The paper noted his statement does not mention the petition. The Facebook post itself is in the record (source ↗ https://www.facebook.com/RepSmucker/posts/1381624226666627/); three other LancasterOnline links are dead, and a Quiver Quantitative entry is about an unrelated budget resolution.

<a id="h-summer-l-lee"></a>

### Summer L. Lee (Democrat-PA-12)
**Position:** Led the push · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** signed · **Distinct actions:** 9 (14 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Summer%20L.%20Lee)

As ranking member of the Oversight subcommittee on federal law enforcement, she got a subpoena moving four months before the House voted. On July 23, 2025, at a hearing on immigration and children, she told Republicans, "Right now, Speaker Johnson is helping Donald Trump block the release of all the files relating to child sex trafficker Jeffrey Epstein. If you want to take a stand against child trafficking, let's do it together," then moved "that the subcommittee issue a subpoena to the Department of Justice for the full, complete, un-redacted Epstein Files." (source ↗ https://summerlee.house.gov/newsroom/press-releases/rep-summer-lee-calls-for-epstein-files-to-be-released) It passed 8 to 2 with three Republicans. (source ↗ https://summerlee.house.gov/newsroom/press-releases/house-subcommittee-passes-rep-summer-lee-s-motion-to-subpoena-epstein-files) When the department missed the August 19 deadline she said "This cover-up is obvious" (source ↗ https://summerlee.house.gov/newsroom/press-releases/rep-summer-lee-statement-on-doj-failure-to-comply-with-epstein-files-subpoena), and when the first batch arrived she said "The released Epstein documents are largely thousands of pages of recycled content already made available to the public," citing a Democratic review that found only three percent new. (source ↗ https://summerlee.house.gov/newsroom/press-releases/rep-summer-lee-slams-doj-release-of-recycled-epstein-files) She signed the petition and, on the day of the vote, reminded colleagues that "The Department of Justice is already compelled to release the Epstein files to the Oversight Committee because of my motion to subpoena, and they have slow-walked their release for months." (source ↗ https://summerlee.house.gov/newsroom/press-releases/rep-summer-lee-statement-on-vote-to-release-epstein-files) At the January 21, 2026 markup on holding the Clintons in contempt, she voted for those resolutions and offered an amendment to hold Bondi in contempt too: "They have handed over one percent of the files and heavily redacted. We are five months overdue, Mr. Comer. You have made no effort to bring in Pam Bondi." (source ↗ https://summerlee.house.gov/newsroom/press-releases/rep-summer-lee-moves-to-hold-pam-bondi-in-contempt-for-failing-to-comply-with-subpoena-to-release-epstein-files) With Robert Garcia she later wrote Bondi demanding compliance, saying "If the DOJ continues to be unresponsive to our lawful subpoena, Bondi must be held in civil contempt of Congress." (source ↗ https://oversightdemocrats.house.gov/news/press-releases/ranking-member-robert-garcia-and-congresswoman-summer-lee-demand-ag-pam-bondi-comply-with-oversight-subpoena-and-release-all-epstein-documents-immediately) At the Oversight Democrats' May 12, 2026 field hearing in Palm Beach, where survivors testified, she said, "The DOJ seems perfectly capable of concealing the names of these rich and powerful in the files that have been released but they did not give that same level of care to the actual survivors"; the same release notes she had introduced articles of impeachment against Bondi. (source ↗ https://summerlee.house.gov/newsroom/press-releases/rep-summer-lee-joins-oversight-dems-for-epstein-investigation-in-palm-beach) A podcast interview on the subpoena strategy is also recorded. (source ↗ https://wearefindout.com/summer-lee-on-forcing-the-epstein-files-release/) Seven of her twenty entries are dead house.gov links that now show a job posting; a Post-Gazette item from July 2025 carries only a headline.

### Seth Magaziner (Democrat-RI-2)
**Position:** Marked the vote only · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** signed · **Distinct actions:** 2 (2 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Seth%20Magaziner)

Nothing in the record is in his own voice; all of it comes through the Rhode Island press. On July 22, 2025 GoLocalProv reported that his office "refused to respond" to a request for comment, and recounted its own 2019 reporting that his father, Clinton adviser Ira Magaziner, appears in Epstein's flight logs and contact book. (source ↗ https://www.golocalprov.com/news/one-ri-congressman-has-nothing-to-say-about-jeffrey-epstein) (link blocked at verification, Sep 3 2026; text preserved from earlier fetch) Three days later a spokesperson told WPRI that he "believes that the Trump Administration should release all of the Jeffrey Epstein files immediately and fully supports efforts of Democrats on the House Oversight Committee to issue subpoenas to compel their release," and said his father "had no knowledge of or involvement in any of the horrible crimes committed by Jeffrey Epstein," explaining that Epstein had lent his plane to Clinton for work trips in the early 2000s. (source ↗ https://www.wpri.com/news/politics/magaziner-speaks-out-about-father-appearing-in-epstein-flight-logs/) He signed the petition and voted for the bill; the WPRI story appears twice.

<a id="h-nancy-mace"></a>

### Nancy Mace (Republican-SC-1)
**Position:** Led the push · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** signed · **Distinct actions:** 12 (15 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Nancy%20Mace)

One of the four Republicans who signed the petition, and the one who kept working the case hardest after the law passed. On September 2, 2025, the day the petition opened, she walked out of the Oversight Committee's closed-door session with survivors in tears and posted, as USA Today reported, "Full blown panic attack. Sweating. Hyperventilating. Shaking. I can't breathe," adding, "I feel the immense pain of how hard all victims are fighting for themselves because we know absolutely no one will fight for us." (source ↗ https://www.usatoday.com/story/news/politics/2025/09/03/nancy-mace-jeffrey-epstein-house-meeting-victims-files/85952063007/) On the day of the vote, after meeting survivors in her office, she said "This is bigger than one case. This is a reckoning," and credited the president who had opposed the petition until it succeeded: "Real men protect women. President Trump understands justice delayed is justice denied, and he's not afraid to stand with survivors when we need it most." (source ↗ https://mace.house.gov/media/press-releases/rep-nancy-mace-votes-release-all-epstein-files-standing-survivors-long-denied) Once the files came out she went after the redactions. On February 4, 2026 she asked Chairman Comer to subpoena Bill Gates: "If these allegations are false, Bill Gates should have no problem saying so under oath before Congress." (source ↗ https://mace.house.gov/media/press-releases/rep-nancy-mace-demands-bill-gates-testify-under-oath-relationship-epstein) After two hours in the department's reading room on February 11 she told NPR, "My primary focus was to get my hands on documents naming co-conspirators," that she was "compiling a list of individuals that I want to see named," and that she was "vetting the list of names I've put together 'cause I want to make sure I'm not accidentally including any victims"; she also said, "I see Trump as being exonerated in the files." (source ↗ https://www.npr.org/2026/02/12/nx-s1-5710044/rep-nancy-mace-says-she-wants-americans-to-know-the-truth-about-the-epstein-files) She told Piers Morgan, "You would be shocked, Piers, at some of the names that I have seen that came across the DOJ in emails that the DOJ is protecting," and named no one. (source ↗ https://thehill.com/homenews/house/5735449-mace-shocked-epstein-files-names/) Newsweek quoted her post: "After today's review of the Epstein files at the DOJ, I have a list of names I will be requesting the House Oversight Committee invite to testify and, if necessary, subpoena." (source ↗ https://www.newsweek.com/nancy-mace-has-list-of-names-to-subpoena-after-viewing-epstein-files-11501665) On February 12 she wrote Bondi asking why documents had been pulled from the department's public site: "The DOJ released these files, then quietly pulled some of them down without explanation. We want to know why." (source ↗ https://mace.house.gov/media/press-releases/rep-nancy-mace-demands-doj-explain-why-epstein-files-were-removed-public) On February 16 she wrote U.S. Attorney Jay Clayton for the unredacted December 2019 co-conspirator memorandum after the department said it did not hold one (source ↗ https://mace.house.gov/media/press-releases/rep-nancy-mace-demands-unredacted-epstein-co-conspirator-memorandum-southern), and she wrote the CIA director for any Epstein records. On NewsNation she went further than her documents, saying "I think he was Intel asset because there's just no way you can explain this just being swept under the rug," and alleging the department was withholding "terabytes of data." (The Hill reported the interview.) (source ↗ https://thehill.com/homenews/house/5752547-nancy-mace-epstein-files-doj-records/) On March 4 she moved in committee to subpoena Bondi: "We want to know where all the audio and video footage is from ALL of the pinhole cameras at every Epstein property" (source ↗ https://mace.house.gov/media/press-releases/rep-nancy-mace-moves-subpoena-attorney-general-pam-bondi-oversight-committee); the motion carried, Comer issued the subpoena on March 17, and she said "They claim all files have been released. The facts say otherwise." (source ↗ https://mace.house.gov/media/press-releases/rep-nancy-mace-issues-statement-after-oversight-committee-issues-subpoena-ag) In August 2026, running for governor, she posted: "We were one of only four Republicans to sign the discharge petition to force the release of the Epstein files. We did it once, and we'd do it again." (source ↗ https://x.com/RepNancyMace/status/2093149063797682382) A Google News entry saying she knows the vote cost her with Trump carries no text; five other entries are dead or duplicate links.

<a id="h-william-r-timmons-iv"></a>

### William R. Timmons IV (Republican-SC-4)
**Position:** Took action, then redirected to the Clintons · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** did not sign · **Distinct actions:** 3 (4 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=William%20R.%20Timmons%20IV)

He did not sign the petition, then voted for the bill, and his record begins after the files came out. On January 30, 2026 he posted, over a report about emails in the release: "If Bill Gates was a participant in sex trafficking, he must be prosecuted to the fullest extent of the law." (source ↗ https://x.com/RepTimmons/status/2017308358349393924) As an Oversight member he joined the Clinton depositions in February: "Tomorrow I am headed to New York to depose Hillary and Bill Clinton regarding Jeffrey Epstein. The American people deserve complete and total transparency." (source ↗ https://x.com/RepTimmons/status/2026742593262858289) He told Fox News Digital afterward that Hillary Clinton was "obstinate and sort of annoyed at the process," while adding, "Honestly, I tend to find her to be fairly credible," and that Bill Clinton "has a lot of really hard questions to answer." (source ↗ https://timmons.house.gov/news/documentsingle.aspx?DocumentID=319) In June 2026 he urged the Justice Department to consider immunity for an Epstein associate who had told the committee she would answer more questions if granted it: "If we want more answers in the Epstein case, we need to give victims and witnesses a path to tell the truth. Immunity may be the key." (source ↗ https://x.com/RepTimmons/status/2065057820777619876) The database dates all four items to 2025; they are from 2026.

<a id="h-ralph-norman"></a>

### Ralph Norman (Republican-SC-5)
**Position:** Took action, then redirected to the Clintons · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** did not sign · **Distinct actions:** 5 (6 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Ralph%20Norman)

On July 14, 2025 he was the only Republican on the Rules Committee to vote for Ro Khanna's amendment to force the Justice Department to publish its Epstein records; it failed 5 to 7. (The Tampa Free Press reported the vote.) (source ↗ https://www.tampafp.com/south-carolina-rep-norman-only-republican-to-back-epstein-file-release-as-house-gop-blocks-effort/) He told Axios, as the Hindustan Times relayed, "The public's been asking for it, I think there are files. All of a sudden to not have files is a bit strange." (source ↗ https://www.hindustantimes.com/world-news/us-news/meet-ralph-norman-sole-republican-who-voted-yes-to-epstein-files-release-101752611076959.html) A week later he counseled patience and questioned Democrats' motives: "People forget this administration has only been in office for what, four months? Five months, and they're going to release the information." (source ↗ https://www.qcnews.com/news/politics/congressman-ralph-norman-urges-patience-on-epstein-file-release/) He did not sign the petition. A statement on his site says, "This isn't about politics. It's about accountability," and credits Chairman Comer's investigation. (source ↗ https://norman.house.gov/news/documentsingle.aspx?DocumentID=3236) He voted for the bill. The week of the vote he introduced the resolution to censure Delegate Stacey Plaskett and remove her from the Intelligence Committee over her 2019 texts with Epstein: "Delegate Plaskett's collaboration with Jeffrey Epstein crossed EVERY ethical line." (source ↗ https://norman.house.gov/news/documentsingle.aspx?DocumentID=3335) The resolution said her conduct was "the height of hypocrisy" (source ↗ https://norman.house.gov/news/documentsingle.aspx?DocumentID=3296); the House rejected it on November 18. Three WCNC interview links carry no text, and six other entries duplicate the items above.

<a id="h-james-e-clyburn"></a>

### James E. Clyburn (Democrat-SC-6)
**Position:** Took action · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** signed · **Distinct actions:** 5 (6 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=James%20E.%20Clyburn)

He wrote to constituents about the files for months, all in his own voice. In July 2025, after the department reversed Bondi's "client list" remark, he wrote: "Pam Bondi was directed by Trump to review the Epstein files. Now Trump's Department of Justice says they don't exist," and noted that Republicans had blocked a vote and "Speaker Mike Johnson then canceled votes and sent Congress home early." (source ↗ https://clyburn.house.gov/trump-promised-epsteins-client-list-now-his-doj-says-it-doesnt-exist/) In September, after survivors gathered at the Capitol, he wrote, "The release of the Epstein files isn't about politics. It's not about Republicans or Democrats. It's about the sexual abuse and trafficking of children." (source ↗ https://clyburn.house.gov/government-shutdown-threat-epstein-cover-up-were-fighting-back/) On Facebook he said, "During the Biden Administration, the Department of Justice was investigating Epstein's co-conspirators. Trump's DOJ killed the investigation." (source ↗ https://www.facebook.com/RepJamesClyburn/posts/during-the-biden-administration-the-department-of-justice-was-investigating-epst/1370796921079009/) He signed the petition, voted for the bill, and published a long explainer warning of the law's "ongoing investigation" exception: "Many fear, I among them, that Trump will exploit this provision to prevent information he considers politically damaging, from coming to light." (source ↗ https://clyburn.house.gov/i-voted-to-release-the-epstein-files-heres-why-its-important/) In February 2026 he posted: "The Department of Justice is withholding Epstein files that include allegations involving Trump, even though the law requires they be released. They are violating the law and shielding the President." (source ↗ https://x.com/RepJamesClyburn/status/2026786199025656104)

**Position:** Deflected, then redirected to the Clintons · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** did not sign · **Distinct actions:** 4 (6 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Russell%20Fry)

He added, "This is less about transparency for Democrats and more about political theater." (source ↗ https://www.wbtw.com/news/politics/fry-laces-into-house-democrats-after-voting-for-release-of-full-epstein-file/) Seven of his eleven entries are duplicates or empty Google News shells of these items.

**Position:** Marked the vote only · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** did not sign · **Distinct actions:** 5 (11 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Dusty%20Johnson)

<a id="h-tim-burchett"></a>

### Tim Burchett (Republican-TN-2)
**Position:** Took action, then deflected · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** did not sign · **Distinct actions:** 9 (14 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Tim%20Burchett)

An Oversight member who moved early and talked freely. After the department's July 2025 memo he told NewsNation, "I think the files existed at one time. I think they were destroyed in the previous administration," while adding, "I think there's some very prominent people. There's Hollywood people," and "I want to bury those dirtbags." Asked why Bondi would not say the evidence was destroyed, he answered, "She doesn't have any proof of it," and, "I'm just telling you what I think." (The Hill reported the interview.) (source ↗ https://thehill.com/homenews/house/5393787-tim-burchett-jeffrey-epstein-files-client-list/) He wrote Chairman Comer asking that Ghislaine Maxwell be brought in, and told WVLT, "Anytime there's an appearance of covering up something like that, it sends a message, first of all to the dirtbag child molesters that need to be sent into Hell, it gives them a green light," while cautioning, "Just because somebody flew on a plane doesn't mean they're a child molester." (source ↗ https://www.wvlt.tv/2025/07/18/if-you-mess-with-child-you-need-get-chair-rep-tim-burchett-calls-transparency-over-epstein-investigation/) On July 22 his motion to subpoena Maxwell passed the subcommittee unanimously. (source ↗ https://burchett.house.gov/media/press-releases/burchetts-motion-subpoena-ghislaine-maxwell-approved-house-oversight-committee) He cosponsored the Transparency Act (WKRN, video only) and demanded answers about the flight logs (dead link), but did not sign the petition. On November 12, after Democrats objected to his unanimous-consent attempt to bring the bill up at once, he posted, "I tried to release The Epstein Files immediately and the Democrats blocked it." (source ↗ https://x.com/timburchett/status/1988728014725890359) He voted for the bill. After Maxwell took the Fifth in February 2026 he told Newsmax she might one day "end up committing suicide by getting shot in the back of the head five times," said he still believes Epstein did not kill himself, called the Clintons "an evil pair," and predicted, "I don't think it'll boil down to anything." (source ↗ https://thehill.com/homenews/house/5731422-burchett-maxwell-epstein-files-release/) Eight of his seventeen entries are dead links or empty Google News shells.

(source ↗ https://www.dallasnews.com/news/politics/2025/09/03/texas-republicans-take-a-pass-on-bipartisan-move-to-force-quick-release-of-epstein-files/) His two database entries, a Star-Telegram item listing him among members who would vote yes and an NBC 5 item on his position, are both dead Google News links with no text; NBC 5's own page says his office had not responded. That Dallas item is filed under Brandon Gill.

**Position:** Took action · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** did not sign · **Distinct actions:** 7 (7 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Pete%20Sessions)

In 2026 he discussed the redacted release with ABC, and on C-SPAN with Rep. Raja Krishnamoorthi he said, "There are lots of questions that neither side can answer." (source ↗ https://x.com/cspan/status/2085780512455737555) Only the Mediaite item and the C-SPAN post carry his words in the record; the rest are video descriptions.

<a id="h-chip-roy"></a>

### Chip Roy (Republican-TX-21)
**Position:** Worked against release, then Took action · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** did not sign · **Distinct actions:** 5 (4 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Chip%20Roy)

He said the right things and cast the blocking vote. In a Rules Committee hearing in summer 2025 he called for a Judiciary subcommittee on Epstein and, per the San Antonio Current's account of Forbes footage, said "Pursue the truth wherever it may lead," and "What the hell are we afraid of?" About a week later, the Current reported, he was one of eight Republicans on the Rules Committee who voted 8-4 to keep the Epstein Files Transparency Act from reaching the floor. (source ↗ https://www.sacurrent.com/news/austin-san-antonio-u-s-rep-chip-roy-voted-to-block-release-of-epstein-files/) On September 3 the Dallas Morning News reported he "appreciates Massie's efforts and everyone wants the truth, but Congress can keep pushing to release as much as possible without using a discharge petition." (source ↗ https://www.dallasnews.com/news/politics/2025/09/03/texas-republicans-take-a-pass-on-bipartisan-move-to-force-quick-release-of-epstein-files/) He did not sign. He spoke on the floor on the bill the day it passed and voted for it (source ↗ https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=qjhmtS9yWXc; description only). Later he asked Attorney General Bondi at a hearing why some survivors' names had been left unredacted in the Justice Department's release (source ↗ https://www.cbsnews.com/video/rep-chip-roy-asks-bondi-why-some-epstein-survivors-names-were-unredacted-from-files/; description only). A Dailymotion interview clip carries no text.

(source ↗ https://www.mediaite.com/media/news/last-hard-no-gop-vote-on-releasing-epstein-files-flip-flops-will-vote-yes/) (link blocked at verification, Sep 3 2026; text preserved from earlier fetch) The Houston Chronicle quoted his remarks to reporters that night: "This is all about Donald Trump.
