(source ↗ https://www.dallasnews.com/news/politics/2025/09/03/texas-republicans-take-a-pass-on-bipartisan-move-to-force-quick-release-of-epstein-files/) On MSNBC on September 7 she said the resistance showed "the lengths that this administration will go to protect pedophiles to protect criminals." (source ↗ https://www.tampafp.com/texas-rep-jasmine-crockett-levels-major-accusations-against-trump-amid-epstein-files-push/) On November 13 she wrote to Attorney General Bondi and FBI Director Patel demanding answers about newly released emails and Trump's ties to Epstein: "We're talking about decades of allegations and survivors who've waited long enough. You can't claim to serve justice while keeping the public in the dark." (source ↗ https://crockett.house.gov/media/press-releases/rep-crockett-demands-answers-doj-and-fbi-efforts-conceal-trumps-ties-jeffrey) The same day on CNN she suggested Republicans had redacted a survivor's name in an email, and anchor Pamela Brown corrected her that Democrats had done it; the New York Post quoted her walking it back: "no, no, I understand. But just saying, like, our biggest concern is to actually make sure we are protecting victims." (source ↗ https://nypost.com/2025/11/13/us-news/rep-jasmine-crockett-gets-fact-checked-by-cnn-fails-to-smear-trump-over-epstein-docs/) Google News entries with no text record that she backtracked after saying Republicans took Epstein money, and an MSN item with no text says she attributed a Lee Zeldin donation to Epstein that reports traced to a different Epstein (summaries; source text not retrieved). Before the vote she told NBC 5: "It's a shame we've even gotten to the point of needing a discharge petition. They claim they have nothing to hide, but the way they keep dodging deadlines and slow-walking subpoenas says otherwise. Tomorrow's vote is about forcing the transparency the American people should've had months ago. Have no doubt: I'll be voting YES." (source ↗ https://www.nbcdfw.com/news/politics/lone-star-politics/north-texas-members-congress-epstein-files-vote/3945895/) After Ghislaine Maxwell took the Fifth in an Oversight deposition, Fox News quoted her on Trump: "We're gonna be on his a--," and "We have a 34-count convicted felon, and there are people that are still shielding him from any type of accountability as it relates to a child sex-trafficking ring." (source ↗ https://www.foxnews.com/politics/jasmine-crocketts-vulgar-6-word-message-trump-epstein-probe) At Bondi's Judiciary hearing on February 11, 2026 she said, "Now I'm not saying that the president is a pedophile, but there is a lot of evidence in these files that suggests that he's very close friends with a lot of men who are pedophiles." (source ↗ https://www.dallasnews.com/news/politics/2026/02/12/jasmine-crockett-bashes-pam-bondi-over-epstein-files-questions-trump-ties/) When Bondi was fired she said Bondi "has been complicit in corruption and weaponized the Department of Justice, all while leading the cover-up of the Epstein files" and was still obligated to testify (source ↗ https://crockett.house.gov/media/press-releases/rep-crocketts-statement-pam-bondis-firing); when Bondi skipped the April 14 deposition she posted, "Since she didn't show up, Oversight Democrats will move to hold her in contempt of Congress." (source ↗ https://x.com/RepJasmine/status/2044138394662387931) Later she posted, "They paused Oversight Committee hearings the minute we started getting answers about the Epstein files. Now it's 'roundtables': no rules, no oaths, no subpoenas." (source ↗ https://x.com/RepJasmine/status/2047122369391595924) A Google News entry with no text says she attacked Senate rival James Talarico over an Epstein-linked super PAC donor. Six entries are empty Google News shells, four are duplicates, and one is a 2023 release about an FBI subpoena that has nothing to do with Epstein.

### Julie Johnson (Democrat-TX-32)
**Position:** Took action · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** signed · **Distinct actions:** 3 (3 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Julie%20Johnson)

She spoke on the House floor on September 10, 2025 to call on the administration to release the full files (source ↗ https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=9PRUKBKVUVE; description only), signed the petition, and told NBC 5's Washington partners before the vote: "These Republicans are running scared on Epstein. (source ↗ https://www.nbcdfw.com/news/politics/lone-star-politics/north-texas-members-congress-epstein-files-vote/3945895/) Her statement after voting yes: "Donald Trump promised to release the files. He promised transparency. Instead, he and his handpicked lieutenants buried the truth beneath a mountain of lies and contradictions. That's why today I proudly voted to release the Epstein Files." (source ↗ https://juliejohnson.house.gov/media/press-releases/johnson-votes-release-epstein-files) Two database entries are the same release.

<a id="h-marc-a-veasey"></a>

### Marc A. Veasey (Democrat-TX-33)
**Position:** Led the push · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** signed · **Distinct actions:** 3 (5 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Marc%20A.%20Veasey)

He moved first among House Democrats after the Justice Department's July 2025 memo. On July 12 he introduced a resolution calling on the Justice Department, FBI and other agencies to release all unclassified Epstein records, flight logs and investigative materials, with 31 original co-sponsors: "One of two things is true: either Trump and his acolytes fueled the rumors of the significance of these files contain more serious information that they are actively hiding," adding that Congress owed it to the victims and to the credibility of the justice system to bring every fact to light. (source ↗ https://veasey.house.gov/media-center/press-releases/congressman-veasey-introduces-resolution-demanding-full-public-release) The Hill quoted his post announcing it: "Put up or Shut up!" (source ↗ https://thehill.com/homenews/house/5400433-marc-veasey-epstein-files-release/) He told TIME, "The MAGA constituents want to know what's in these files, Democratic constituents want to know what's in these files," and "Whether or not there's anything [in the files], I don't know. But since [Trump] made a big deal about it, I do want to see [those files]." (source ↗ https://time.com/7302296/jeffrey-epstein-files-trump-marc-veasey/) By August 15 the resolution had 46 co-sponsors, per CBS's Scott MacFarlane. (source ↗ https://x.com/MacFarlaneNews/status/1956326315688075513) He signed the petition and voted for the bill. One House link is a dead duplicate and one Google News entry is empty.

<a id="h-greg-casar"></a>

### Greg Casar (Democrat-TX-35)
**Position:** Took action · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Not voting · **Discharge petition:** signed · **Distinct actions:** 4 (6 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Greg%20Casar)

An Oversight member who signed the petition the day it was filed and missed the vote it produced. After the committee's September 2 meeting with survivors the Dallas Morning News described him as choked up: "This isn't just politics. This is hundreds and hundreds of women who were abused in ways that are hard to imagine and then blackmailed and threatened and so much of this has been covered up by people with a lot of money." (source ↗ https://www.dallasnews.com/news/politics/2025/09/03/texas-republicans-take-a-pass-on-bipartisan-move-to-force-quick-release-of-epstein-files/) He told Austin's KVUE the same, in interviews the database records four times, all behind dead links; Newsweek relayed the quote: "Why have some of these folks that participated in Jeffrey Epstein's crime not been held accountable? Why does the federal government have all of this information and hasn't moved forward on some of these cases? I think the only way to answer these questions is for full transparency." He did not vote on November 18; a spokesperson told Newsweek he had just welcomed his first child and would have voted yes. (source ↗ https://www.newsweek.com/five-house-members-missed-epstein-vote-heres-why-11072176) After reports that the department held back records, he posted: "The Department of Justice has illegally 'withheld some Epstein files related to allegations that President Trump sexually abused a minor.' The biggest coverup in presidential history just got worse. Release the files." (source ↗ https://x.com/RepCasar/status/2026350836427653563) That post is entered twice, once dated 2025-11 though its ID places it in 2026.

<a id="h-brian-babin"></a>

### Brian Babin (Republican-TX-36)
**Position:** Deflected · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** did not sign · **Distinct actions:** 1 (3 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Brian%20Babin)

I believe in full transparency for the American people, which is why I voted to release everything. But this is clearly just a political sideshow." Then it turns to a Democrat: "Meanwhile, Stacey Plaskett (D-V.I.) was literally caught texting Epstein in the middle of a congressional hearing, and the Left says nothing." (source ↗ https://babin.house.gov/news/documentsingle.aspx?DocumentID=14262) Nothing since.

**Position:** Took action · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** signed · **Distinct actions:** 8 (8 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Lloyd%20Doggett)

(source ↗ https://doggett.house.gov/media/press-releases/rep-doggett-trump-seeking-create-master-file-information-every-american) At a press conference on redistricting he said House Republicans fear the files' release (source ↗ https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=3ZNYwYYz2eM; description only). When reports emerged that the department had removed records, he posted: "Unsurprising but disturbing reporting: DOJ withheld and removed Epstein files referencing Trump, including interview notes tied to sexual abuse allegations against him." (source ↗ https://www.facebook.com/RepLloydDoggett/posts/unsurprising-but-disturbing-reporting-doj-withheld-and-removed-epstein-files-ref/1499617264865714/) Five 2026 entries are Google News shells with no text; their summaries say he praised survivors' courage, posted about emails obtained by Oversight Democrats, and said Bondi's department was defying the law by trickling files out and should release everything if it had nothing to hide (summaries; source text not retrieved).

**Position:** Deflected · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** did not sign · **Distinct actions:** 1 (3 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Blake%20D.%20Moore)

**Position:** Marked the vote only · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** did not sign · **Distinct actions:** 2 (5 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Ben%20Cline)

**Position:** Worked against release · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** did not sign · **Distinct actions:** 1 (2 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=H.%20Morgan%20Griffith)

<a id="h-suhas-subramanyam"></a>

### Suhas Subramanyam (Democrat-VA-10)
**Position:** Led the push · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** signed · **Distinct actions:** 13 (12 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Suhas%20Subramanyam)

An Oversight Democrat who signed the petition and then made the department's compliance his beat. In August 2025 he posted: that "this Administration is not releasing the full, unredacted files like they promised, and as usual House Republicans aren't holding them accountable." (source ↗ https://x.com/RepSuhas/status/1959309829400396012) On the vote: "I just voted YES to release the Epstein files to the public, but we didn't need this vote. The Oversight Committee subpoenaed these files months ago, and the President could have just released them." (source ↗ https://subramanyam.house.gov/media/press-releases/rep-subramanyams-statement-house-passage-epstein-files-bill) Three days later he wrote to FBI Director Patel about the security of the files, asking who would do the redactions, whether files at the FBI's Winchester, Virginia records center had been altered, tampered with or accessed since January 20, 2025, and to visit the facility. (source ↗ https://subramanyam.house.gov/media/press-releases/rep-subramanyam-demands-answers-fbi-about-potential-tampering-epstein-files) When the department released only part of the files on the December 19 deadline he told CNN, "They are absolutely in violation of the law. They just admitted they were in violation of the law," and "The law says they needed to release everything by today, not starting today, not part of it today, all of it by today." (source ↗ https://thehill.com/homenews/house/5658414-house-democrat-suhas-subramanyam-epstein-files-partial-release/) His New Year's resolution, he told TMZ, was "to get the files released, name names, and let the courts and the court of public opinion judge those who perpetrated, covered up, and enabled these crimes." (source ↗ https://news.meaww.com/rep-suhas-subramanyam-says-his-new-years-resolution-for-epstein-files-is-to-name-names) After reviewing the unredacted files on February 9, 2026 he told NPR, "There are many instances where people who were not victims were redacted," including "someone who regularly traffics women was redacted for some reason because they themselves were a woman." (source ↗ https://www.npr.org/2026/02/10/nx-s1-5707293/rep-suhas-subramanyam-d-va-talks-about-viewing-the-unredacted-epstein-files) He and Jamie Raskin brought the brother and sister-in-law of Virginia Roberts Giuffre to the State of the Union (source ↗ https://subramanyam.house.gov/media/press-releases/subramanyam-raskin-bring-family-epstein-survivor-late-virginia-roberts-giuffre); that release also notes his November 2025 letter to Andrew Mountbatten Windsor. In March he wrote to Sarah Ferguson asking her to submit to questioning. (source ↗ https://subramanyam.house.gov/media/press-releases/ranking-member-subramanyam-demands-sarah-ferguson-answer-questions-jeffrey) In May, after deposing Commerce Secretary Lutnick: "He once again said he was never in a room with Epstein after 2005, yet he admits he visited his Island AND visited his NY home after that," and "Lutnick needs to resign." (source ↗ https://x.com/RepSuhas/status/2052094679269642572) He also joined Oversight Democrats' West Palm Beach session with survivors. (source ↗ https://x.com/RepSuhas/status/2054213606921707965) Two Google News entries carry no text. Two of his 2025 items are dated 2026 in the database.

<a id="h-james-r-walkinshaw"></a>

### James R. Walkinshaw (Democrat-VA-11)
**Position:** Led the push · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** signed · **Distinct actions:** 10 (11 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=James%20R.%20Walkinshaw)

He signed the petition the day he arrived. "Moments after being sworn in to represent Virginia's 11th District, I became the 217th Member of the House to sign the discharge petition to force a floor vote on bipartisan legislation requiring the release of" the Epstein documents. (source ↗ https://walkinshaw.house.gov/news/documentsingle.aspx?DocumentID=92) Before the vote he said Trump "recently attempted to bully House Republicans into removing their names from the bipartisan discharge petition," and that the released emails "suggest Trump knew about Epstein's abuses, spent hours with one of the victims at Epstein's homes, and took no action," adding, "But this vote is not about him. It is about the victims and survivors." (source ↗ https://walkinshaw.house.gov/news/documentsingle.aspx?DocumentID=170) After the December release he told The Hill's Sunrise the mass redactions needed explanations and further votes (source ↗ https://thehill.com/video/va-rep-walkinshaw-mass-redactions-in-epstein-files-warrants-votes-explanations-in-2026-sunrise/11370410/; description only), and Dogwood reported he called Deputy Attorney General Blanche's handling a "complete failure" (summary; source text not retrieved). As an Oversight member he hosted survivor Jess Michaels at the State of the Union (source ↗ https://walkinshaw.house.gov/news/documentsingle.aspx?DocumentID=352) and, after the committee voted to subpoena Bondi, said: "Pam Bondi says Trump's Department of Justice has released all the Epstein files. They haven't. Records that mention Trump appear to have been pulled." (source ↗ https://walkinshaw.house.gov/news/documentsingle.aspx?DocumentID=376) When she was fired: "She must testify. Fired or not. Or face contempt." (source ↗ https://x.com/Rep_Walkinshaw/status/2043048872847569019) He accused Chairman Comer of having "deliberately ground the House Oversight Committee to a halt" and "replacing public hearings with closed-door, off-the-record roundtables that produce zero accountability" (source ↗ https://walkinshaw.house.gov/news/documentsingle.aspx?DocumentID=507), and posted that "The FBI never interviewed Epstein's lawyer, his accountant, or the primary financier of his sex trafficking." (source ↗ https://x.com/Rep_Walkinshaw/status/2075622310019535106) Two Google News entries carry no text. Several 2026 items are dated 2025 in the database.

<a id="h-stacey-e-plaskett"></a>

### Stacey E. Plaskett (Democrat-VI-0)
**Position:** Deflected · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Delegate (could not vote) · **Discharge petition:** did not sign · **Distinct actions:** 2 (7 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Stacey%20E.%20Plaskett)

She is in this record as a subject of the files, not a voice on them. On November 14, 2025 CNN reported, from the Oversight Committee's release and a Washington Post analysis, that she exchanged texts with Epstein during Michael Cohen's February 27, 2019 hearing, including his "Cohen brought up RONA - keeper of the secrets" and her "Quick I'm up next is that an acronym," and his "Good work" a minute after she finished. Her spokesperson told CNN she "received texts from staff, constituents and the public at large offering advice, support and in some cases partisan vitriol, including from Epstein." (source ↗ https://www.cnn.com/2025/11/14/politics/epstein-texts-stacey-plaskett) The Freedom Caucus moved to censure her the night of the House vote; the motion failed after Democratic leaders defended her. On CNN the next day she said: "I believed that Jeffrey Epstein had information, and I was going to get information to get at the truth. Having a friendship with him is not something that I would deem to have," and "as a prosecutor, you get information from people where you can." (source ↗ https://www.foxnews.com/media/dem-lawmaker-justifies-text-exchange-epstein-says-information-from-people-where-can) The local station WTJX published a timeline drawn from her 2023 deposition in the Virgin Islands' suit against JPMorgan: it reports she met Epstein as a donor in 2012, that Epstein and four affiliates gave more than $30,000 to her campaigns from 2014 to 2020, and that she pledged in July 2019 to donate an equal amount to local charities. (source ↗ https://newsfeed.wtjx.org/podcast/wtjx-newsfeed/2025-11-22/stacey-plaskett-and-jeffrey-epstein-a-timeline) In February 2026 the New York Post, citing NOTUS, reported the Justice Department release showed more correspondence, including a September 2018 text in which she wrote she hoped she was "not presumptuous to consider you a friend," and that her office did not respond. (source ↗ https://nypost.com/2026/02/02/us-news/dem-usvi-delegate-stacey-plasketts-ties-to-jeffrey-epstein-detailed-in-latest-doj-dump/) As a delegate she could not vote on final passage. Two Google News entries carry no text. No statement from her on whether the files should be released appears in the record.

**Position:** Took action · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** signed · **Distinct actions:** 5 (8 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Becca%20Balint)

**Position:** Marked the vote only · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** did not sign · **Distinct actions:** 2 (7 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Dan%20Newhouse)

**Position:** Led the push · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** signed · **Distinct actions:** 11 (14 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Pramila%20Jayapal)

She signed the petition, spoke on the floor the day the bill passed (source ↗ https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=HYR902rXi54; description only), and voted yes; the record then follows her into the unredacted files. In February 2026 she posted, "Tomorrow, I'll be going to view the unredacted Epstein Files again at the DOJ. If there are files you want me to review, comment with the EFTA number below." (source ↗ https://x.com/RepJayapal/status/2025709604215132634) Afterward: "Today, I spent almost 4 hours at the Department of Justice to once again look through the unredacted Epstein files. It is very clear that there is evidence here that needs to be investigated." (source ↗ https://x.com/RepJayapal/status/2026068881534579192) At Bondi's February 11 hearing she asked survivors in the room to stand if the department had not met with them, and asked Bondi to apologize; Bondi said, "I'm not going to get in the gutter for her theatrics." Photographers then caught a sheet in Bondi's binder labeled "Jayapal Pramila Search History." She posted: "Bondi showed up today with a burn book that held a printed search history of exactly what emails I searched. That is outrageous and I intend to pursue this and stop this spying on members." (source ↗ https://www.cbsnews.com/news/bondi-epstein-files-search-history-hearing-pramila-jayapal/) Two days later she, Raskin and Robert Garcia wrote to the department calling the monitoring "a blatant violation of the separation of powers" (source ↗ https://jayapal.house.gov/2026/02/13/jayapal-raskin-garcia-demand-doj-end-its-outrageous-s), and in March they demanded a new review process, writing that the department "continues to cover up for Jeffrey Epstein and Ghislaine Maxwell's co-conspirators and enablers." (source ↗ https://jayapal.house.gov/2026/03/23/judiciary-oversight-democrats-demand-doj-take-5-steps) She hosted survivor Marijke Chartouni, a constituent, at the State of the Union. (source ↗ https://jayapal.house.gov/2026/02/23/jayapal-announces-epstein-survivor-marijke-chartouni-) When Bondi was fired: "AG Bondi called apologizing to the Epstein survivors getting into the 'gutter.' Good riddance." (source ↗ https://x.com/RepJayapal/status/2039758786378780689) In July she and Senator Booker introduced the REDACT Act to let survivors sue over the department's release of their personal information: "Their fight for justice was met by an incredibly incompetent DOJ that released their private information, while protecting abusers." (source ↗ https://jayapal.house.gov/2026/07/14/jayapal-booker-introduce-redact-act-to-protect-epstei) Items on her accusing Patel of a cover-up and calling Blanche unfit are dead or empty links. <a id="h-gwen-moore"></a>

### Gwen Moore (Democrat-WI-4)
**Position:** Took action · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** signed · **Distinct actions:** 3 (4 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Gwen%20Moore)

She signed the petition and voted for the bill, and her statement named who had stood in the way: "Speaker Johnson worked to block efforts to release these files from reaching the House floor, including recessing the House several times to stop Democratic attempts to push for the release. At the White House, Trump engaged in a major pressure campaign to keep Republican members from supporting the petition. It did not work." (source ↗ https://gwenmoore.house.gov/news/documentsingle.aspx?DocumentID=5791) A Thanksgiving post aimed at the Speaker over seating a member-elect ("Pass the gravy") survives only as a summary in two Google News entries, one dead (summary; source text not retrieved). On Trump's birthday in June 2026 she posted: "Happy Birthday, @POTUS. Release the files so the American people can know what wonderful secrets you share with Jeffrey Epstein." (source ↗ https://x.com/RepGwenMoore/status/2066269795142496628)

<a id="h-scott-fitzgerald"></a>

### Scott Fitzgerald (Republican-WI-5)
**Position:** Marked the vote only · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** did not sign · **Distinct actions:** 1 (2 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Scott%20Fitzgerald)

He did not sign the petition, voted for the bill, and posted: "Today, I voted YES to release the Epstein files. This vote promotes greater transparency and oversight by giving the public access to information held by the government. I am confident the DOJ will..." (source ↗ https://www.facebook.com/CongressmanScottFitzgerald/posts/today-i-voted-yes-to-release-the-epstein-files-this-vote-promotes-greater-transp/1218973266710339/) Only the preview was retrieved. The other entry is an empty Google News shell for a Journal Sentinel roundup.

<a id="h-glenn-grothman"></a>

### Glenn Grothman (Republican-WI-6)
**Position:** Deflected · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** did not sign · **Distinct actions:** 1 (2 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Glenn%20Grothman)

An Oversight member who did not sign the petition and voted for the bill. His one recorded remark, in a clip circulated by journalist Aaron Rupar in early 2026 while discussing the committee's investigation, blamed a missing tranche of files on liberals: "I do think that perhaps early on some of those files disappeared. It says something about the elites of this country and it seems largely we're dealing with liberal elites. Their sexual mores are very different than that of the average person." (source ↗ https://x.com/atrupar/status/2026734894102036785) An X trending summary of the same clip notes critics pointed instead to gaps in the Trump-era department's releases. (source ↗ https://x.com/i/trending/2026790729075269856) The database dates both entries to 2025; the post IDs place them in February 2026.

<a id="h-thomas-p-tiffany"></a>

### Thomas P. Tiffany (Republican-WI-7)
**Position:** Deflected · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** did not sign · **Distinct actions:** 2 (4 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Thomas%20P.%20Tiffany)

He did not sign the petition, and his statement after voting yes put the delay on the other party: "For years under President Biden, Democrats kept these files sealed, and today, I voted to release the Epstein files and give the American people the sunlight they deserve." (source ↗ https://tiffany.house.gov/media/press-releases/tiffany-statement-vote-release-epstein-files) Three entries are dead copies of that statement. In 2026, running for governor, he posted a thread on Reid Hoffman's donations to the state party: "@WisDems continues to take money from confirmed Epstein Island visitor Reid Hoffman. In April, they accepted another $750,000 from him," and, citing the files, that Hoffman "sent Epstein ice cream 'for the girls.'" (source ↗ https://x.com/TomTiffanyWI/status/2077871709898592743 and https://x.com/TomTiffanyWI/status/2077871712801005953) Those posts are dated 2025 in the database; their IDs place them in July 2026.

<a id="h-tony-wied"></a>

### Tony Wied (Republican-WI-8)
**Position:** Deflected · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** did not sign · **Distinct actions:** 2 (2 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Tony%20Wied)

He voted for the leadership's September 3 resolution, did not sign the petition, and voted for the bill. His statement leaned on the first and jabbed at Democrats: "While I had already taken direct action to ensure the House Committee on Oversight continued investigating and releasing previously sealed documents by voting to pass H. Res. 668 on September 3rd, it was clear the American people believed more needed to be done." He said the House "under the Trump Administration, has shown the American people the transparency they deserve" and that they did not see it under Biden. (source ↗ https://wied.house.gov/media/press-releases/rep-wieds-statement-voting-release-epstein-files) A longer version on a local site added: "Although Democrats joined us in voting to release the files yesterday, their policies did not address the issue prior to President Trump taking office." (source ↗ https://appletonwi.org/congressman-tony-wied-statement-on-my-vote-to-release-the-epstein-files/)

<a id="h-carol-d-miller"></a>

### Carol D. Miller (Republican-WV-1)
**Position:** Marked the vote only · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** did not sign · **Distinct actions:** 1 (2 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Carol%20D.%20Miller)

She did not sign the petition, voted for the bill, and posted about it; WV MetroNews quoted the post: "The American people deserve transparency, and victims of his heinous abuse deserve justice," and "I trust our Department of Justice and the House Oversight Committee to handle this investigation with care to prevent any further pain to the victims and their families." The same report carried the state Democratic chairman's charge that she and Riley Moore "stood with Trump to stop that release until the very last second." (source ↗ https://wvmetronews.com/2025/11/18/moore-and-miller-vote-for-bill-to-compel-release-of-files/) Her own post was retrieved only as a preview. (source ↗ https://www.facebook.com/RepCarolMiller/posts/1233193728631014/)

<a id="h-riley-m-moore"></a>

### Riley M. Moore (Republican-WV-2)
**Position:** Marked the vote only · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** did not sign · **Distinct actions:** 2 (4 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Riley%20M.%20Moore)

On September 3, 2025 he voted for the leadership's H.Res. 668 and put out a release titled "Congressman Moore Votes to Release Epstein Files": "Today, House Republicans took an important step to fulfill our commitment to provide the American people with radical transparency regarding the Epstein files." (source ↗ https://rileymoore.house.gov/media/press-releases/congressman-moore-votes-release-epstein-files) RealWV set that beside Massie's description of the same resolution as a "fig leaf" that "does absolutely nothing" (source ↗ https://therealwv.com/2025/09/04/moore-votes-in-favor-of-formalizing-investigation-into-epstein-files/), and a Morgan County site noted Politico had to correct a story that took the release to mean he backed the petition. (source ↗ https://morgancountyusa.org/?p=6226) He did not sign it. After the November vote: "I voted to release the files today. This bill continues the Trump Administration's efforts to provide as much information to the public as possible." (source ↗ https://rileymoore.house.gov/media/press-releases/congressman-moore-votes-release-epstein-files-advancing-radical-transparency) One entry is a local copy of the September release.

<a id="h-harriet-m-hageman"></a>

### Harriet M. Hageman (Republican-WY-0)
**Position:** Deflected · **Nov. 18, 2025 vote:** Aye · **Discharge petition:** did not sign · **Distinct actions:** 3 (3 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Harriet%20M.%20Hageman)

Asked at an August 2025 town hall whether she would support a House vote to release the files, she said, per SSBCrack News: "I don't know if that vote's going to come up on the floor, but what a lot of people don't realize is that is for the courts to decide." (source ↗ https://news.ssbcrack.com/rep-hageman-faces-backlash-at-town-hall-over-mail-in-ballots-and-epstein-files/) A Google News entry with no text says she dismissed angry attendees as bizarre hysteria (summary; source text not retrieved). She did not sign the petition. After voting yes she told Cowboy State Daily, "Americans deserve full transparency, and I expect we will get to the bottom of why a convicted sex felon was planting questions with a member of the Democrat caucus during a House Oversight hearing," a reference to Delegate Plaskett's texts. (source ↗ https://cowboystatedaily.com/2025/11/18/hageman-and-426-other-house-members-vote-to-release-epstein-files/)

<a id="part-iii"></a>

# Part III. The 2026 Challengers

*What the people running against sitting members have said about the Epstein files, read from the record, one entry each.*

None of these candidates cast a vote or signed the petition. What their record shows is something different: how the files are being used on the campaign trail, by whom, and against whom. The database tracks 3,810 people who have filed to run for the House or Senate in 2026 ([FEC candidate file, via Where Congress Stands](https://epstein-data.com/statements)). The overwhelming majority have said nothing about the Epstein files that the record could find. This part covers the 162 non-incumbent challengers who have, organized by state and then by the office they are seeking. 25 sitting members who also appear in the candidate file (running for another office, or simply listed as candidates for their own seats) are listed at the end of the rosters with a pointer to their entry in Part I or Part II; their records are not repeated here.

Each entry gives the office the candidate is seeking, one of six plain-English labels for how they are using the subject, how many distinct things they actually said once duplicates are collapsed, a link to their full record, and then the prose, with the lines that matter quoted exactly and linked. A challenger's claim about an opponent is a campaign allegation and is written as one. The labels are defined in the Terms table at the front of this document.

## Entries


<a id="c-daniel-sullivan"></a>

### Daniel Sullivan (Republican)
This packet is Sen. Dan Sullivan of Alaska, a sitting member filed under a variant of his name and mislabeled as a challenger; see his entry in Part I, "Dan Sullivan (Republican-AK)," which is not rewritten here. The two items in this packet (Alaska's News Source on the September 2025 tabling vote, and the FactCheck.org piece on the Peltola ad) are covered there and under Mary Peltola above. [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Daniel%20Sullivan)

<a id="c-mary-peltola"></a>

### Mary Peltola (Democrat)
**Running for:** U.S. Senate, AK · **Position:** Attacks opponent over it · **Distinct actions:** 3 (3 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Mary%20Peltola)

The former Alaska congresswoman is running against Sen. Dan Sullivan, and every Epstein item in her record is aimed at him. In August 2026 she posted that "Dan Sullivan voted to keep the Epstein files sealed. He then raked in campaign donations from Epstein associates, including one from Epstein's “black book.” It's sickening. Alaska deserves better than a senator who profits off of protecting pedophiles." (source ↗ https://x.com/MaryPeltola/status/2087681409502830875) A week later she wrote that "He was the deciding vote to silence victims, shield pedophiles, and cover for the people who protected them. We deserve a senator who isn't bought by the Epstein class." (source ↗ https://x.com/MaryPeltola/status/2090526750199722220) Both are her campaign's allegations. Her television ad, as quoted by FactCheck.org, asks "Who's the real Dan Sullivan? Sullivan claimed the Epstein files should be released, but when it came time to vote, Sullivan cast the deciding vote to keep them hidden." FactCheck.org called the ad misleading: Sullivan voted in September 2025 to table a Schumer amendment to the defense bill, then voted with the whole Senate for the Epstein Files Transparency Act in November, and the two donors the campaign calls "Epstein associates" have not been accused of any wrongdoing. The same piece reports the Sullivan campaign's counter-claim that Peltola took more than $140,000 from "associates of Jeffrey Epstein," which it found equally thin. (source ↗ https://www.factcheck.org/2026/08/alaska-senate-candidates-attempt-to-link-each-other-to-epstein/) The two posts and the fact-check are each filed twice in the database.

### Terri Lapoint (Republican)
**Running for:** U.S. House, AL-3 · **Position:** Mentioned it · **Distinct actions:** 1 (1 entry in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Terri%20Lapoint)

<a id="c-case-dixon"></a>

### Case Dixon (Republican)
**Running for:** U.S. House, AL-6 · **Position:** Campaign plank, and attacks opponent over it · **Distinct actions:** 3 (3 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Case%20Dixon)

A Republican primary challenger to Rep. Gary Palmer who made the files a test of his own party. In a column for 1819 News he wrote that "Regardless of who's implicated, Republicans should be leading the charge to release those files," that "They're not gossip. They're not a distraction. They're evidence," and that "If Republicans choose secrecy now, we're no better than the politicians we once vowed to hold accountable." (source ↗ https://1819news.com/news/item/case-dixon-epstein-files-fact-or-fiction) In a statement reported by AL Politics he called on Palmer to sign the Massie-Khanna discharge petition: "If I were the representative for Alabama's 6th District, I would sign this petition without hesitation. Congressman Palmer must forget the politics, forget the optics, and do the right thing." He broke with the president in the same statement: "On this issue, the President is wrong. Calling the Epstein revelations a hoax dishonors victims and minimizes real crimes." (source ↗ https://alpolitics.com/gop-challenger-case-dixon-demands-congressman-gary-palmer-back-epstein-files-transparency-act/) After the November 2025 House vote his campaign site reprinted an AL Politics piece quoting him: "I applaud the House for passing this bill. It is one step closer to accountability for perpetrators and one step closer to justice for victims," adding that "This vote should have happened months ago." (source ↗ https://dixonforuscongress.com/dixon-says-epstein-files-vote-is-a-win-for-victims/)

**Running for:** U.S. House, AZ-7 · **Position:** Called for release · **Distinct actions:** 1 (1 entry in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Daniel%20Butierez)

<a id="c-aaron-rowden"></a>

### Aaron Rowden (Democrat)
**Running for:** U.S. House, CA-8 · **Position:** Called for release · **Distinct actions:** 1 (1 entry in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Aaron%20Rowden)

One long Substack essay, "Why the Epstein Files Matter," dated September 11, 2025, which treats the files as a threat to what he calls an emerging American aristocracy rather than as a Trump story. "Epstein the man was a loathsome and small figure, despite his wealth and proximity to power. Epstein the idea is a manifestation of the insidious cancer of American politics that Trump tried to harness for his own ends and now seeks to make us forget: aristocracy." He argues that "the exposure Epstein's crimes and the complicity of the powerful in them pose a danger not only to the personal status of the American economic and political elite, but to the very mythology that undergirds their dubious claim to power." Most of the essay is a history of Jefferson and Adams on natural aristocracy; the retrieved portion ends before any direct demand of Congress. (source ↗ https://aaronrowden.substack.com/p/why-the-epstein-files-matter)

<a id="c-jamie-joyce"></a>

### Jamie Joyce (Democrat)
**Running for:** U.S. House, CA-12 · **Position:** Campaign plank · **Distinct actions:** 4 (4 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Jamie%20Joyce)

A candidate who has built her campaign around rewriting the Epstein Files Transparency Act, drawing on what she describes as a background in government records management. Her campaign site reports that on June 29, 2026 she presented draft legislation in Washington to fix three flaws in the Act, and that three survivors present endorsed her; the site is the only source for the endorsement. Her central point is a single word: "It was a one difference - it could have been “shall,” it could have been “must,” but it was a one word difference that failed to protect survivors' personally identifiable information." She went further on why the case matters to her: "We must investigate the Epstein case to the maximal extent," she said, "because it may reveal that we are not actually a sovereign nation after all, but instead operating under blackmail that's potentially held by a foreign nation which was originally created by Epstein." That is her theory, not a finding. (source ↗ https://www.jamiejoyce.com/in-the-news/2026/6/29/jamie-speaks-in-dc-endorsed-by-epstein-survivors) Two Facebook videos carry the message in caption form: "There's still a way to get more Epstein Files. I'm a congressional candidate who wrote The MAD Act - a ten title bill." (source ↗ https://www.facebook.com/JamieJoyce/videos/we-can-still-get-the-epstein-files/1460039741992555/) and, by title only, "Just one word failed Epstein Survivors in the Epstein Files Transparency Act." All of Cargile's Epstein material is one allegation, pushed across platforms and a podcast, against Steve Hilton, a Republican candidate for governor who is not his opponent. His video, reposted on LinkedIn with a transcript, opens: "Steve Hilton is in the Epstein files and it is a BIG DEAL for the voters of California." His claim is that "At least two times that we know of over many months, Steve Hilton and a friend tried to meet with Woody Allen through Jeffrey Epstein's facilitator Ian Osborne," citing 2011 and 2012 emails from Hilton's time in David Cameron's office; he concedes "we're not saying that Steve Hilton was involved in ****** little children" but argues that "This would make him thoroughly disqualified for the office of Governor of the State of California." <a id="c-lev-parnas"></a>

### Lev Parnas (Independent)
**Running for:** U.S. House, FL-22 · **Position:** Campaign plank · **Distinct actions:** 3 (3 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Lev%20Parnas)

The former Giuliani associate runs his Epstein material through a Substack that doubles as a fundraising channel, and the files are its main subject. On November 17, 2025, before the House vote, he wrote: "This Tuesday, that vote finally comes to the House. No more delays. No more excuses. No more hiding. And that's exactly why, according to my sources, Donald Trump is on the phone, over and over, trying to stop it." The claim about Trump's calls is his, attributed to unnamed sources. He laid out a veto-proof target, "290+ votes in the House, and 67+ votes in the Senate," asked readers to call Congress and share a survivors' PSA, and closed with a GoFundMe pitch. (source ↗ https://levremembers.substack.com/p/urgent-message-from-lev-parnas-trumps) Two days later he hosted a livestream, "SPECIAL REPORT: First Vote To Release The Epstein Files," whose page text is a promotional blurb and the same fundraising appeal. (source ↗ https://levremembers.substack.com/p/special-report-first-vote-to-release) A Substack note defends his "Wolf Pack" readership against critics, naming Michael Cohen and Michael Wolff as people who "were happy to live in our community while it benefited them. But accountability? Transparency? Answering direct questions?", and ends by urging readers to "Demand congress subpoena key figures on Epstein case." (source ↗ https://substack.com/@levparnas/note/c-214904840)

<a id="c-john-deaton"></a>

### John Deaton (Republican)
**Running for:** U.S. Senate, MA · **Position:** Campaign plank, and attacks opponent over it · **Distinct actions:** 6 (9 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=John%20Deaton)

A former federal prosecutor running against Ed Markey, with Seth Moulton in the race, who has made the files a through-line of his campaign since before it was fashionable in his party. He posted that he "💯 support[s] everything @RepThomasMassie is doing related to the Epstein documents," said he "was one of the FIRST people in the country to push for the release," and accused the administration's own officials, naming Blanche, Patel, Bongino and Bondi, of "lying, claiming that there are no other predators to investigate or prosecute," listing "Jes Staley, Leon Black, Les Wexner, Prince Andrew, Jean-Luc Brunel, just to name a few." (source ↗ https://x.com/JohnEDeaton1/status/2053958005746504113) He frames it as bipartisan rot: "the truth doesn't have a party - it has a list of names that would end careers on both sides." (source ↗ https://x.com/JohnEDeaton1/status/2030023100721516738) He has said in his own posts that he was raped as a child and that he "will NEVER let it go once I get to the Senate." (source ↗ https://x.com/JohnEDeaton1/status/2053119934352224653) In February 2026 he answered Pam Bondi's remark that prosecuting everyone involved would halt the system: "if that's what it takes to prove we are not a nation that protects pedophiles, so be it." (source ↗ https://x.com/JohnEDeaton1/status/2023792724671971404) He uses it against both Massachusetts Democrats. In a 2024 post he said he had confronted Elizabeth Warren over JPMorgan's Epstein settlements and claimed her "ex-husband is on the list of people who traveled with Epstein," a campaign allegation the entry does not verify. (source ↗ https://x.com/JohnEDeaton1/status/1850574685949362557) In August 2026 he mocked the Moulton and Markey fight as "two career politicians arguing over which one is more corrupt." (source ↗ https://x.com/JohnEDeaton1/status/2084682896510521463) A podcast episode the database files under the Epstein files is, by its page text, about Massachusetts housing and politics; the episode list shows a July 21, 2025 episode titled "Where Are The Epstein Files?" but no text from it. (source ↗ https://podtail.com/en/podcast/the-hard-truth-with-john-deaton/where-are-the-epstein-files/) Two Google News entries, one on a debate fact-check, carry no text.

<a id="c-jordan-wood"></a>

### Jordan Wood (Democrat)
**Running for:** U.S. Senate, ME · **Position:** Campaign plank · **Distinct actions:** 3 (3 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Jordan%20Wood)

The database lists him as a Senate candidate; his own site and Substack say he ran for Maine's 2nd District House seat and lost the Democratic primary by 331 votes. His Epstein material is one sustained argument: a bipartisan select committee. In a March 3, 2026 Substack essay, "The Epstein Cover-Up Is Bipartisan. It's Time to Act Like It.", he wrote: "I'm a Democrat, and I'll say what too few in my party will: our side has serious questions to answer." He then listed names, saying George Mitchell "appeared over 300 times in the files" and that FBI records contain a woman's allegation of trafficking to him, and repeating allegations about Larry Summers, Kathryn Ruemmler and Bill Richardson; these are his readings of the files and other people's allegations, not findings. "Republicans are equally implicated and equally protected," he wrote, naming Trump, Musk, Lutnick and Acosta. His demand: "It's time for a Select Committee. No more excuses. No more cover-ups. No one is above the law." (source ↗ https://jordanwoodmaine.substack.com/p/the-epstein-cover-up-is-bipartisan) The same case is a plank on his campaign site, which argues that a select committee "takes the investigation out of the hands of a Justice Department with documented conflicts of interest." (source ↗ https://electjordan.com/issue/establish-a-bipartisan-select-committee-to-investigate-the-epstein-files/) A Facebook post carries the essay's opening lines. (source ↗ https://www.facebook.com/jordanwoodmaine/posts/jeffrey-epstein-was-indicted-for-running-a-sex-trafficking-network-that-exploite/122163187910853503/) The Substack essay and the Facebook post are each filed twice.

<a id="c-john-sununu"></a>

### John Sununu (Republican)
**Running for:** U.S. Senate, NH · **Position:** Deflected · **Distinct actions:** 2 (2 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=John%20Sununu)

Sununu is in this catalogue as a subject rather than an advocate; both items are third-party reporting and nothing here is in his own voice. Granite Post reported that a May 2010 email from Epstein to Boris Nikolic in the released cache reads, in part, "john sununu, has good stories," and noted that the document does not say whether it means the candidate or his father of the same name. The campaign's response, through spokesman Mike Schrimpf: "This is a complete fabrication by someone proven to be a compulsive liar. Neither John nor his father ever met or communicated in any way with Boris Nikolic or Jeffrey Epstein. John believes Epstein was a despicable human being." The same statement turned the subject on Democrats: "Unlike Chris Pappas who celebrated federal funding for Kamen's ARMI, or Stefany Shaheen who worked for him last week, John never advocated or requested funding for any of Kamen's ventures." (source ↗ https://granitepostnews.com/news/politics/sununu-denies-ties-despite-being-named-in-epstein-files/) A Washington Post analysis carried by MSN, which the database summarizes as reporting that Sununu's false answer about Qatar fueled an Epstein-files fight, returned no text (summary; source text not retrieved) (source ↗ https://www.msn.com/en-us/news/politics/sununu-s-false-answer-about-qatar-fuels-epstein-files-fight/ar-AA282cBN).

<a id="c-eddy-aragon"></a>

### Eddy Aragon (Republican)
**Running for:** U.S. House, NM-2 · **Position:** Campaign plank · **Distinct actions:** 2 (3 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Eddy%20Aragon)

An Albuquerque radio host who has made Epstein's Zorro Ranch and the files a running subject of his daily show, and who is unusual among Republican candidates in treating the fight as a danger to his own party. The show notes for a November 2025 episode of his Rock of Talk Substack say Trump "personally called Boebert and Mace urging them to withdraw their support for the discharge petition," that "instead of derailing the effort, these tactics heightened suspicion," and that "Republicans now appear to be blocking transparency and protecting elites." The notes quote him warning that "there is no outcome for this whole thing that makes Donald Trump politically safe." (source ↗ https://rockoftalk.substack.com/p/the-epstein-files-showdown-how-transparency) His other action is a podcast interview about an anonymous 2019 email he received and forwarded to the FBI, which is now among the released documents and which alleged that two girls' bodies were buried near the ranch; that is an anonymous, unverified claim, reported as such. Searchlight New Mexico wrote that Aragon "says he knows who the sender of the anonymous email is and that he relayed that information to an FBI agent," and quoted him on the state's new truth commission: "We need to get this stain off of our state of New Mexico." (source ↗ https://searchlightnm.org/new-mexico-radio-host-discusses-anonymous-email-alleging-buried-bodies-at-epsteins-zorro-ranch/) A second outlet's write-up of the same interview renders the quote as "We demand to get this stain off of our state of New Mexico" (source ↗ https://news-usa.today/epstein-ranch-nm-radio-host-details-buried-bodies-email-to-fbi/). His Substack's episode list also shows later Epstein episodes, including one on Bill Gates and one on Thomas Massie, that are not in the database.

(source ↗ https://www.facebook.com/marklynchsc/posts/behold-the-epstein-files-playset-complete-with-shredded-files-missing-evidence-a/1240634781212224/) A video titled "'Release the Epstein Files' | A Message from Mark Lynch to Washington" is in the record by its title only (source ↗ https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=C-oqZa1TAdk). In March 2026 he relayed CBS reporting that the DOJ "has taken down files included in the Epstein disclosure," putting the number "around 47,000, accounting for more than 65,000 pages" (source ↗ https://x.com/MarkLynchSC/status/2028950846692630976). It was reviewed." (source ↗ https://isaiahrmartin.substack.com/p/breaking-another-trump-redaction) A campaign Facebook post titled "Donald Trump is in the Epstein files" returned no text (summary; source text not retrieved) (source ↗ https://www.facebook.com/isaiahmartintx/posts/donald-trump-is-in-the-epstein-files/816498648020904/). The redaction claim is his reading of the files, not verified here.

<a id="c-jimmy-skovgard"></a>

### Jimmy Skovgard (Republican)
**Running for:** U.S. Senate, WY · **Position:** Attacks opponent over it, and called for release · **Distinct actions:** 2 (2 entries in the database) · [all their statements ↗](https://epstein-data.com/efta_statements/statements?name__exact=Jimmy%20Skovgard)

A Republican Senate candidate who has turned the files into a report card on Harriet Hageman, his rival for the seat. In November 2025 he wrote that he had asked the delegation to "Protect survivors completely. Then give the public a clear map of the truth," that Hageman "confirmed a 'yes' vote on the Epstein Files Transparency Act, H.R. 4405," and thanked her for it, while proposing "A public index of every Epstein-related record that still sits behind a wall, with a short explanation and an expiration date for each." (source ↗ https://jimskovgard.substack.com/p/wyoming-here-is-where-the-epstein) By March 2026 the tone had hardened. In a podcast post he graded her on a five-point scale: "Our representative, Harriet Hageman, voted yes on that procedural package. This was a choice to align with a leadership strategy that favored process over immediate transparency," and on her final vote, "Voting yes when the outcome is already inevitable is just doing the job. Real leadership is being on that petition when the pressure is at its highest." His tally: "Our representative lands at a 2.3 out of 5. This score reveals a follower mentality." (source ↗ https://jimskovgard.substack.com/p/grassroots-rising-the-epstein-receipt) The account of her procedural votes is his.
